## 2026-09-08 — Claude — useCfdTickers malformed-response hardening

- Agent: Claude. Same branch `claude/crypto-catalogue-500-v1`, on top of `d808da5`. Scoped to the one defect found during Crypto Catalogue browser QA. No merge, no deploy, no production access.
- **The defect**: `useCfdTickers` did `setTickers(res.tickers)` with no validation. A 200 response whose `tickers` is not an array (proxy error page, truncated body, changed upstream) put a non-array into state, and on the next render `resolveCfdSymbol` read `.length` off it — `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'length')`, which took the whole Trade page down through the error boundary. Found with a bad QA fixture, not with the real endpoint; the endpoint contract has always supplied the field.
- **The fix**, entirely inside `frontend/src/lib/useCfdTickers.ts`: `parseTickerPayload()` validates the payload before it reaches React state and returns `null` for anything untrustworthy. A row is admissible only with a non-empty string `symbol` (the React key, the icon lookup and the `resolveCfdSymbol` match all key on it) and a number-shaped `price` (a listed row is clickable and drives `CfdOrderForm`, whose submit is enabled by the row's mere existence — a row you can click into an order form against a non-number is worse than an absent row). `name` falls back to the symbol, a label rather than a market value. `changePercent24h` is omitted when not number-shaped, which the `CfdTickerRow` contract already defines as "unknown" and renders as a dash, never 0.00%.
- **On a malformed payload**: last-good rows are preserved, `configured` keeps its last known value (asserting "CFD unavailable" on the strength of a payload we could not read would be a claim the response does not support), and `loadError` is set — routing into the **existing** honest error state and retry button rather than a new one. A well-formed empty array is still treated as truth, not as an error. A real `0` price passes through unchanged.
- **CFD business logic untouched**: no change to sizing, leverage, margin, liquidation, the open/close payloads or any CFD component. The `CfdOrderForm.tsx`, `CfdPositionsPanel.tsx` and `TradePage.tsx` preservation fingerprints in `cfdTerminal.test.ts` all still pass unchanged. The Crypto Catalogue implementation was not touched.
- **Tests**: 12 focused regression tests appended to `cfdTerminal.test.ts`, reusing its existing isolated-hook harness — seven malformed shapes (missing/null/object/string `tickers`, null body, string body, rows that all fail to parse) each proving the state stays an array, `resolveCfdSymbol` does not throw, and `configured` is not falsely flipped; last-good preserved across a malformed refresh; well-formed empty still truth; unusable rows dropped without a manufactured price; a real zero and a numeric price surviving; name fallback. Suite 36/36.
- **Validation actually run**: frontend `tsc -b` PASS; backend `tsc --noEmit` PASS; production Vite build PASS; full Jest **1781 passed / 1784**, the same 3 pre-existing failures (`avatarIdentityPresentation`, `nazaraCardPresentation`, `walletUxRefinement`), +12 tests.
- **Browser-verified end to end** against a fixture serving the exact `{}` payload that crashed the page: `/trade?pair=BTC/USDT` renders with zero page errors (it crashed before). With only `/cfd/tickers` malformed and every other CFD endpoint valid, `/trade?market=cfd` renders the terminal — market price and 24h change show `—`, the instrument list shows its existing "failed to load, tap to retry" control, the submit button is **disabled**, and a DOM sweep found **no bare `0.00`/`$0.00` market value anywhere**. The only zeros on screen are the order form's own cost/margin, derived from the user's empty quantity input.
- **Found but NOT fixed, out of the scope given**: `CfdPositionsPanel` has the same defect class — `positions.map is not a function` when `/cfd/positions` returns a non-array. It is a different component, it is under a preservation fingerprint, and it was not part of this task. Worth its own follow-up.

## 2026-09-08 — Claude — Copy Trading deposit gate $20,000 → $10,000

- Agent: Claude. Same branch `claude/crypto-catalogue-500-v1`, on top of `fbbedaf`. Owner-requested figure change. No merge, no deploy, no production access.
- **The ask**: the VIP Copy Trading dialog said «Копировать этого трейдера можно при депозите от $20 000.» The owner wrote the new figure ambiguously ("$10 0000"); asked, and they confirmed **$10 000** (ten thousand) — a lowering of the gate, not a raise to $100,000.
- **The gate**: `COPY_ELIGIBILITY_THRESHOLD_USD` in `frontend/src/pages/copy-trading-bolt/CopyEligibilityContext.tsx`, 20_000 → 10_000. Also updated every figure quoted to a member: the dialog heading in `CopyDepositDialog.tsx`, and both marketing keys (`marketing.feature.copyTrading.text`, `marketing.faq.a5`) across all seven languages — JA and KO spell it in words, so 2万ドル → 1万ドル and 2만 달러 → 1만 달러.
- **A latent defect fixed alongside it**: the constant that decides eligibility and the strings that quote the figure were independent literals, so changing one silently left the other lying about the price of entry. `copyDepositUx.test.ts` now sweeps every `$<figure>` on the Copy Trading path — the dialog plus all fourteen marketing strings — and asserts each one equals the constant, in both the `10 000` and `10,000` separator styles; JA/KO are pinned separately because they print no digits, and the count of 14 is asserted so a new language cannot slip past. Mutation-checked: moving the constant to 25_000 fails the guard. `copyPrelaunchPromotion.test.ts` now reads the constant instead of restating 20_000, so it tests the rule rather than the number.
- **Deliberately NOT changed**: `currentCopyMinimum: 20_000` in `src/services/copyTrading/canonical/reviewEconomicsConfig.ts`. Despite the similar name it is a different thing — a historical policy figure in Ksenia's canonical review ledger, carrying its own `copyMinimumPolicyEffectiveDate` and feeding `grandfatheredBelowCurrentMinimum` in the reconciliation report. Editing it would rewrite a financial report rather than change a UI gate. Flagged for the owner; not touched.
- **Three preservation fingerprints re-taken**, each with the reason recorded next to it in `cryptoCardProductionPromotion.test.ts`: `CopyEligibilityContext.tsx` (the gate itself — its fingerprint moving IS the record of the decision), `traders.ts` (COMMENT ONLY, +1/-1) and `CopyTradingPage.tsx` (COMMENT ONLY, +1/-1). Both comment-only changes were verified by stripping comments and diffing the executable source against `HEAD` — byte-identical. No assertion weakened.
- **Validation actually run**: frontend `tsc -b` PASS; backend `tsc --noEmit` PASS; production Vite build PASS; full Jest **1782 passed / 1785**, the same 3 pre-existing failures (`avatarIdentityPresentation`, `nazaraCardPresentation`, `walletUxRefinement`).
- **Browser-verified in the real production build**, against fixtures generated from the deterministic `CopyPerformanceService` (the actual read-model payloads, not hand-written numbers; the generator was a throwaway and is not committed). Portfolio value **$9,500** → clicking «Копировать трейдера» opens the dialog reading «…при депозите от $10 000.» with the Пополнить/Закрыть actions. Portfolio value **$12,000** — above the new gate, below the old one — → **no dialog, copying starts** («Копирую»), which is the whole point of the change and would have been refused before. Zero page errors in both. Screenshots under untracked `outputs/copy-gate/`.

## 2026-09-08 — Claude — CfdPositionsPanel malformed-response hardening

- Agent: Claude. Same branch `claude/crypto-catalogue-500-v1`, on top of `c04d930`. The second defect of the same class found during Crypto Catalogue QA and flagged in the previous handoff entry. No merge, no deploy, no production access.
- **The defect**: `CfdPositionsPanel` did `setPositions(res)` and `setHistory(res)` with no validation. A 200 whose body is not an array went straight into state; the next render evaluated `positions.length === 0` (false for a non-array) and fell through to `positions.map(...)` — `TypeError: positions.map is not a function`, which took the CFD terminal down through the error boundary.
- **The fix**, in `frontend/src/components/CfdPositionsPanel.tsx`: `parsePositions()` and `parseHistory()` validate before render state and return `null` for a payload that cannot be trusted. Deliberately **all-or-nothing**, unlike the instrument-list fix which drops unusable rows: these rows are the user's own open exposure, and quietly showing a subset would tell someone they hold three positions when they hold five — worse than saying the list could not be loaded. A row must carry a non-empty string `id` (what `closeCfdPosition` is called with, so a wrong one would aim a real close at the wrong position), a `side` of exactly LONG or SHORT (anything else silently rendered as SHORT and misstated the direction of the exposure), a finite `leverage`, and string `symbol`/`size`/`entryPrice`/`liquidationPrice`. History additionally requires a status and a parseable `realizedPnl`, which goes through `parseFloat().toFixed(2)` unguarded and otherwise printed the literal "NaN".
- **`markPrice`, `unrealizedPnl` and `roe`** are normalised to `string | null`: absent or unparseable becomes `null`, which the existing markup renders as a dash. A real `'0'` is finite and passes through untouched — zero PnL is a fact about the position, and it still renders `0.00` / `0.00%`.
- **On a malformed payload**: last-good rows stay on screen and a `role="alert"` notice appears above them; with no last-good, the new `futures.loadPositionsError` message takes the place of the empty state, so a failed load is never presented as "no positions". A rejected request now routes to the same state — previously `.catch(() => {})` swallowed it and a first-load failure read as an empty account, which is the same lie by a different route. Real `[]` remains a legitimate empty state and still shows «Нет позиций».
- **New i18n key** `futures.loadPositionsError` across all seven languages, inserted beside the existing `futures.closePositionError` the panel already uses. No existing string fit: `markets.loadError` is about market data and `analytics.loadFailed` is another namespace.
- **CFD financial logic untouched**: `handleClose` is byte-identical — verified by printing the exported function's statement list from `HEAD` and from the working tree and diffing them. Exactly two statements differ, both in the read path: the added `loadFailed` state and the load effect's two `.then` handlers. The close API call, closing state, error path, refresh bump, 4s poll cadence, cancellation flag and effect cleanup are all unchanged, and this file contains no sizing, margin, leverage, liquidation or balance code. Crypto Catalogue code was not touched.
- **One preservation fingerprint re-taken** in `cfdTerminal.test.ts` for `CfdPositionsPanel.tsx`, with that statement-level diff recorded next to it as the reason. No assertion weakened; the pre-existing positions/history test (polling, close API, closing state, error, liquidation status) still passes unchanged.
- **Tests**: 21 focused regression tests added to `cfdTerminal.test.ts`, which now runs **56/56**. Eleven malformed positions shapes (non-array object, non-array string, missing, null, array holding null, array holding a primitive, row missing/blank `id`, unrecognised `side`, non-numeric `leverage`, missing `entryPrice`) each proving no throw, the honest error state, no "no positions" claim, no invented rows and no fabricated `0.00`; last-good preservation across a malformed poll; legitimate `[]` still empty; a valid payload rendering unchanged with real zeros intact; absent optionals rendering as a dash and never NaN; four malformed history shapes; and a valid history payload with a real zero realized PnL.
- **Validation actually run**: frontend `tsc -b` PASS; production Vite build PASS; full Jest **1802 passed / 1805**, the same 3 pre-existing failures (`avatarIdentityPresentation`, `nazaraCardPresentation`, `walletUxRefinement`), +21 tests.
- **Browser-verified in the real production build** against the exact payload that crashed it (`/cfd/positions` returning `{}`): the CFD terminal renders, no error boundary, no page error; the panel shows «Не удалось загрузить позиции» as a `role="alert"` on both the positions and history tabs, with zero fabricated rows and no `0.00` anywhere. Against a valid payload: two open rows render with correct sides and figures, the real-zero position shows `0.00` / `0.00%` rather than a dash, and the history row shows `0.00` with status LIQUIDATED — no alerts. Screenshots under untracked `outputs/cfd-positions/`.

## 2026-09-08 — Claude — Analytics Phase 2: real external market intelligence

- Agent: Claude. Branch `claude/analytics-phase2-external-data`, created from `origin/main` `778cb97` (the owner had merged the catalogue + CFD-hardening branch). No merge, no deploy, no production/Render/Neon/database access. **Paid-provider budget $0 and nothing paid was implemented, required or recommended.**
- **Providers researched**: Binance USDⓈ-M Futures public market data, OKX v5 public market data, Kraken OHLC (already integrated), CoinGecko catalogue (already integrated). Deribit was not needed for anything on the page. CoinGlass / CryptoQuant / Glassnode were researched and deliberately NOT implemented — no adapter, no key, no code path.
- **Implemented, free and unauthenticated**: `BinanceDerivativesService` (`/fapi/v1/openInterest`, `/fapi/v1/premiumIndex`, the three `/futures/data/*LongShort*Ratio` endpoints) and `OkxDerivativesService` (`/api/v5/public/open-interest`, `/api/v5/public/funding-rate`, `/api/v5/public/mark-price`, `/api/v5/market/index-tickers`), composed by `ExternalDerivativesService`. All built on the EXISTING primitives — `HttpProviderClient`, `ProviderHealth`, `ProviderCache` — with no new networking or caching framework. Each venue has its own circuit, named apart from the `binance`/`okx` circuits `ArbitrageService` already owns, so spot rate-limiting cannot blind the derivatives page. `DerivedAnalyticsService` adds realized volatility, crypto correlations and sector rotation from series the gateway already caches.
- **Seven modules became real**: tracked-venue open interest, external funding comparison, long/short positioning, perpetual premium (basis), realized volatility, crypto correlations, sector rotation. Each left the `unsupported` map, and a test asserts the two lists can never both claim a module.
- **Seven stay unavailable, each with a recorded reason**: recent liquidations (Binance's public REST liquidation endpoint is retired and its remaining feed is WebSocket-only, which this backend has no socket to consume — §9; no verified free REST source), liquidation heatmap, implied volatility, futures term structure, ETF flows, exchange flows, whale activity. No heatmap is manufactured from anything, and trading volume is never used as a proxy for on-chain flow.
- **Honesty rules enforced in code and in tests**: the aggregate is called *tracked-venue* open interest and never market-wide; `sources`/`venues` contains only venues that actually contributed, so the UI label built from it cannot keep saying "Binance + OKX" while OKX is down; Binance's three positioning measures stay three separate labelled measures; basis carries its formula; realized volatility is never called implied; correlations are labelled crypto-only. A real 0 (flat funding, untraded contract, a 0% sector) stays 0; a missing figure is `null` and renders as a dash. There is no 50/50 positioning fallback anywhere.
- **Separation (§27)**: `AnalyticsSeparation.test.ts` reads the eleven shipped financial modules — mark price, funding settlement, liquidation, positions, execution, margin math, exposure, insurance fund, matching, orders, CFD positions — and asserts none imports an analytics or derivatives module, none reaches an external venue endpoint, and none is constructed with one in `index.ts`. Flow is one-directional: Analytics reads the futures services, never the reverse. The adapters were also asserted to have no write path and to read no credential.
- **Caching / coalescing**: OI 15 s, funding 30–60 s, positioning 60 s, prices 15 s, each with its own stale budget; derived metrics inherit the candle and catalogue caches and add no provider sweep. **100 simultaneous Analytics consumers produce 9 provider requests in total** (5 Binance + 4 OKX), and a second wave of 100 produces none — proven deterministically.
- **Validation actually run**: backend `tsc --noEmit` PASS; frontend `tsc -b` PASS; production Vite build PASS. Jest **1889 passed / 1892**, +107 tests over the branch point, with the same 3 pre-existing failures (`avatarIdentityPresentation`, `nazaraCardPresentation`, `walletUxRefinement`) verified unrelated. New suites: `ExternalDerivatives.test.ts` (32), `DerivedAnalytics.test.ts` (23), `AnalyticsSeparation.test.ts` (28).
- **One preservation fingerprint re-taken**: `frontend/src/lib/api.ts` in `futuresTickerHeader.test.ts` (+143/−1; the single deletion is `getAnalyticsOverview` gaining an optional `asset` parameter, everything else additive analytics types). `src/api/routes/futures.ts` is still at its original fingerprint. No assertion weakened; the existing Analytics suites were updated to the new module set and gained new claims.
- **i18n**: 30 new `analytics.*` keys across all seven languages, TypeScript-enforced complete. No hardcoded user-visible text in the new components.
- **Browser QA** on the real production build at 1920/1440/1366/1280/1024/768/430/390: **zero horizontal overflow and zero console errors at every width**, one analytics request per page load. Four scenarios at 1440 — full (label "BINANCE + OKX", $4.19B), one venue down (label collapses to **"BINANCE"**, total drops to **$4.07B**, OKX rows gone), total outage (7 honest empty states, zero fabricated figures), and stale (values shown with 9 stale tags). The unreported positioning ratio draws **no bar** rather than a 50/50 one; the 30d volatility window with too few observations renders `—` beside its real sample count; the RWA sector's genuine 0% renders as +0.00%. Asset switching sends `?asset=ETH` and costs exactly one extra request. Screenshots under untracked `outputs/analytics-phase2/`.
- **Live provider verification: NOT performed.** The sandbox proxy returns a 403 policy denial for CONNECT to fapi.binance.com, www.okx.com, api.kraken.com and api.coingecko.com. The nine endpoint families that must be exercised from Render are listed in `docs/MARKET_DATA_ARCHITECTURE.md` §10a.
- **Business logic untouched**: no change to matching, execution, margin, leverage, tiers, liquidation, funding settlement, positions, wallet, copy trading, card, auth, KYC, deposits, withdrawals, schema, migrations or dependencies. No new dependency was added.
- Unresolved / next: liquidations need either a verified free REST source or the server-side WebSocket fan-out already documented as a gap in §9 — that is the single highest-value follow-up; the TTLs are deliberately conservative and can be relaxed once real rate-limit headers are observed from Render; OKX publishes no equivalent of Binance's positioning ratios, so that module stays single-venue by construction. Ready for owner review only.

## 2026-09-08 — Claude — Futures drawing toolbar (shared with Spot)

- Agent: Claude. Branch `claude/futures-drawing-toolbar` from `origin/main` `4d4f65c`. No merge, no deploy, no market-data or financial change.
- **Generalized, not duplicated.** `PriceChart`'s `spotTools` prop became `drawingTools` plus a `market` namespace; `spotDrawingTools` became `drawingToolsOn`; `SpotDrawingTools.css` became `DrawingTools.css` with `spot-drawing-*` classes renamed to `drawing-*`. Futures already rendered `chrome="terminal"` inside `.trade-terminal`, so enabling the rail needed no second stylesheet and no second implementation. A test asserts no `FuturesPriceChart.tsx` / `FuturesDrawingTools.css` exists.
- **A coupling caught and undone.** The spot-terminal price-axis precision and the MACD warm-up were gated on the same flag as the rail, purely because "has the rail" and "is the spot terminal" used to be the same thing. Enabling the rail on Futures would have silently changed that page's indicator warm-up and axis precision — neither asked for. They now ride on `spotChartRefinements = terminal && market === 'spot'`, so both pages keep exactly what they had, and two suites assert the split.
- **Reused unchanged**: cursor, trend line, ray, horizontal, vertical, rectangle, Fibonacci retracement, brush, ruler, text, fit-to-content, hide/show, stay-in-drawing-mode, the group flyout with its keyboard handling, and the in-app text/clear dialogs.
- **Added, each with a real implementation**: extended line (drawn through both anchors and projected to the plot edges), magnet, lock, and a per-drawing eraser. Magnet snaps each new anchor to the nearest OHLC level of the nearest loaded candle via `magnetSnap`; lock refuses adding, erasing and clearing while leaving drawings visible and the chart fully navigable; the eraser hit-tests in screen space against the same coordinates the overlay draws from, with a clamped point-to-segment distance so a click past an endpoint does not delete.
- **Deliberately NOT added**: parallel channel (needs a third anchor and a two-stage gesture) and a separate price-range tool (the ruler already reports price delta, percent and bar count over the same drag). No icon exists for either — no dead buttons.
- **Persistence**: `voltex.drawings.<market>.<SYMBOL>` in localStorage, v1-versioned, capped at 400 drawings, every point stored as real `{time, price}` and never as pixels. Deliberately NOT keyed by timeframe — a drawing anchored to real timestamps is the same drawing on 15m and 1d. Horizontal levels moved from bare `createPriceLine` calls into React state with one effect owning the derived chart objects, which is what made them saveable at all. A malformed or foreign payload parses to an empty set rather than crashing, and a storage failure leaves a working chart without persistence.
- **Symbol isolation**: proven in a browser — BTC 2 drawings → ETH 0 → ETH gets its own 1 → back to BTC 2 → after a full reload still 2, under two separate keys. The "which key does this state belong to" marker is React state, not a ref, specifically so a symbol switch cannot save the previous symbol's drawings into the new symbol's slot.
- **Validation**: frontend `tsc -b` PASS, backend `tsc --noEmit` PASS, production Vite build PASS, Jest **1918 passed / 1921** (+29 tests), the same 3 pre-existing failures (`avatarIdentityPresentation`, `nazaraCardPresentation`, `walletUxRefinement`). `chartDrawings.test.ts` now runs 56. Three fingerprints re-taken with reasons recorded: `TradePage.tsx` and `FuturesPage.tsx` (one line each — the PriceChart props) and the `spotIndicators`/`spotChartPriceFormat` structural gates, which were strengthened to assert the decoupling rather than weakened.
- **Browser QA** at 1920/1440/1366/1280/1024/768/430/390: rail present with 14 buttons at every width, **zero horizontal overflow and zero console errors throughout**. At 1440 each tool was exercised individually and all ten drawing tools plus the eraser work; drawings survived pan, zoom, a timeframe switch and a full page reload. Magnet verified against the real candle feed: with it off the anchors land on arbitrary prices, with it on **both anchors sit exactly on real OHLC levels**. Lock verified: a drawing attempt while locked adds nothing and the existing drawings stay. At 430/390 the rail becomes a 44px scrollable strip above the chart rather than covering it. Screenshots under untracked `outputs/futures-drawing/`.
- **CFD untouched**, asserted by a test that `CfdChart.tsx` references none of this. No market-data provider, turnover, OI, funding, mark price, matching, order, margin, leverage, liquidation, position or Spot execution code was changed.
- Unresolved / next: drawings still cannot be selected and moved after they are placed, so lock protects creation and deletion rather than dragging; parallel channel and price range remain unbuilt; rail geometry was left at the existing 34x32 buttons rather than retuned, since changing it would alter Spot for no functional gain. Ready for owner review only.

## 2026-09-08 — Claude — Futures header: real external derivatives market stats

- Agent: Claude. Branch `claude/futures-external-market-stats` from `origin/main` `e51dccb` (verified, matches the SHA in the brief). No merge, no deploy.
- **Two header cells now answer the question their labels ask.** `Оборот за 24ч` was Kraken **SPOT** `quoteVolume24h` for the underlying pair; it is now the summed 24h quote turnover of the tracked **perpetual** venues, labelled `(USD)`. `Открытый интерес` was VOLTEX's **own** `FuturesPosition` aggregate; it is now the tracked venues' open interest. Both come from one new lightweight endpoint, `GET /market/derivatives/:baseAsset`.
- **No new provider architecture.** Reuses `HttpProviderClient`, `ProviderCache`, `ProviderHealth`, `ExternalDerivativesService` and the existing Binance/OKX adapters. Two additions: `getTurnover24h` on each adapter, and `getTrackedTurnover24h` + `getFuturesMarketStats` on the service. Open interest reuses `getTrackedOpenInterest` unchanged.
- **OKX turnover: `volCcyQuote24h`, and only that.** Third-party summaries describe `volCcy24h` as a USD-equivalent volume; against CCXT's own OKX adapter that is false for derivatives — `volCcy24h` is a BASE amount there, and CCXT reads it as `quoteVolume` for spot only. Publishing it would have shown a BTC turnover of a few thousand dollars against a real figure in the billions. When `volCcyQuote24h` is absent the venue returns `null` and contributes nothing; no conversion is invented from another venue's price. Binance uses `quoteVolume` from `/fapi/v1/ticker/24hr` directly — no `lastPrice × baseVolume` approximation anywhere.
- **Units are never mixed.** Base units are summed only across venues that reported base units. If none did, the cell falls back to the USD notional **and relabels itself**, so the unit on screen is always the unit of the number. `turnoverVenues`, `openInterestBaseVenues` and `openInterestUsdVenues` are three separate contributor lists and the source line beside each figure is derived from the list that actually backs it — there is no hardcoded "Binance + OKX" string in the component.
- **`/futures/open-interest` is untouched**: route, service, client method, internal risk and the Analytics VOLTEX section are all unchanged and `src/api/routes/futures.ts` is still at its original fingerprint. The header's own 4s poll of it was removed because the cell it fed no longer exists — VOLTEX's book was never an answer to "how big is this market".
- **Preserved from Codex/prior work**: no change to matching, execution, margin, leverage, tiers, liquidation, funding settlement, positions, mark/index price, wallet, copy trading, card, auth, KYC, schema, migrations or dependencies. The drawing toolbar from the previous task is untouched — no chart file is in the diff. Last price, 24h change, 24h high, 24h low, VOLTEX mark price, funding rate and the funding countdown all still render exactly as before.
- **Load**: server-side cache 30s TTL with in-flight dedup; frontend polls at 30s, not the 4s VOLTEX cadence. **100 concurrent header readers produce 5 upstream provider requests** (Binance `openInterest` + `premiumIndex` + `ticker/24hr`, OKX `open-interest` + `ticker`); a second wave inside the TTL produces zero. Proven deterministically in `ExternalDerivatives.test.ts`.
- **Validation actually run**: backend `tsc --noEmit` PASS; frontend `tsc -p` PASS; production Vite build PASS. Jest **1953 passed / 1956**, +37 tests, with the same 3 pre-existing failures (`avatarIdentityPresentation`, `nazaraCardPresentation`, `walletUxRefinement`) — re-confirmed failing on the stashed clean tree, so unrelated. New suite `futuresMarketStats.test.ts` (17); `ExternalDerivatives.test.ts` now 48.
- **Two preservation fingerprints re-taken, with reasons recorded in the test file**: the `FuturesTickerBar` reads block (one added external-stats effect, one removed internal-OI read; the VOLTEX mark/index/funding reads are byte-unchanged, and the behavioural test was re-scoped to assert that against the per-symbol loop in isolation) and `frontend/src/lib/api.ts` (+13/−0, purely additive). No assertion was weakened; `futuresTickerHeader.test.ts` gained a scenario and went from 17 to 19 tests.
- **Browser QA** on the real production build against a new read-only loopback fixture harness (`scripts/qa-futures-market-stats.cjs`, GET-only, 405 on every write verb, no egress) at 1920/1440/1366/1280/1024/768/430/390, for BTC/ETH/SOL/XRP, in six scenarios: **zero horizontal overflow at every width and no application console error** (the only console errors are the sandbox proxy refusing `wss://ws.kraken.com/v2`). Both venues → `22.6B` / `105,219.5398 BTC`, source "Binance + OKX". Binance only → `18.42B` / `84,215.4218`, label collapses to **"Binance"**. OKX only → `4.18B` / `21,004.118`, label **"OKX"**. Total outage → **both cells `—`, and no source line at all**. Base units unavailable → cell **relabels to `(USD)`** and shows `1.28B`. Stale → same values, dimmed. Symbol switching costs exactly one derivatives read per symbol and never leaves the previous contract's figures on screen. `/futures/open-interest/*` does not appear in the request log at all. Screenshots and the raw cell dump under untracked `outputs/futures-market-stats/`.
- **Live provider verification: NOT performed.** The sandbox proxy returns a 403 CONNECT policy denial for `fapi.binance.com` and `www.okx.com` (and for their documentation hosts). All coverage is deterministic mocked tests plus the fixture-backed browser QA above. The two new endpoint families to exercise once from Render are listed in `docs/MARKET_DATA_ARCHITECTURE.md` §10a items 10–11; §10b documents the whole change.
- Unresolved / next: Binance + OKX is a large share of perpetual open interest but is **not** market-wide, and nothing in the UI claims otherwise — a genuine market-wide figure still needs a source that does not exist for free; if OKX later publishes `volCcyQuote24h` inconsistently across contracts the turnover total will silently narrow to Binance alone, which the source line makes visible but no alert covers; the 30s TTLs are conservative and can be relaxed once real rate-limit headers are observed from Render. Ready for owner review only.

## 2026-09-09 — Claude — Remove all provider branding from the customer-facing UI

- Agent: Claude. Same branch `claude/futures-external-market-stats`, on top of `cb1ec88`. Owner correction before release. No merge, no deploy.

Four suites updated to the new contract: `futuresTickerHeader` (gained "the header names no upstream venue, in any state"), `futuresMarketStats` (gained "names no upstream venue" and "keeps the contributor lists in the API contract, out of the UI"), `analyticsLive`, plus the new guard.

## 2026-09-09 — Claude — Bybit full market universe (discoverable vs executable)

- Agent: Claude. Branch `feat/bybit-full-market-universe` from `origin/main` `52edfa8` (verified against the SHA in the brief). No merge, no deploy.
- **Audit first.** The finding that shaped everything: `MarkPriceService.getIndexPrice()` reads the existing spot index feed, and mark price, funding, margin and liquidation all read it. A contract with no index price has no mark price and therefore no liquidation price. That, not a config constant, is the real ceiling on the executable futures universe. Second finding: VOLTEX Spot is an INTERNAL CLOB (`MatchingEngine` + per-pair `OrderBook`, funds locked from internal `Balance` rows) with no external routing and no executable-pair registry at all — the order route accepts any `A/B` string.
- **Architecture**: `BybitMarketDataService` (public V5, no key) → `ProviderCache`/`ProviderHealth`/`HttpProviderClient` → `MarketUniverse` → `FuturesMarketRegistry` (executable) and `GET /market/universe` (discoverable). No second cache, no second health system, no direct browser→venue call.
- **The 40-market cap is GONE** and not replaced by a bigger number. It was a rendering limit wearing a listing rule's clothes — nothing financial read it. Rendering is now handled by `useWindowedRows`, so the executable set is data-driven.
- **The $1M 24h volume floor is KEPT**, explicitly evaluated as a safety rule: liquidation must be able to actually close a position, and a market too thin to exit is the failure the insurance fund absorbs. Raising the visible count is not a reason to lower it.
- **The venue universe can only REMOVE.** Executable = live index price AND volume floor AND (universe loaded → a real Trading USDT-settled LinearPerpetual). The third clause stops VOLTEX offering a perpetual on a pair that has no real perpetual anywhere, and keeps dated LinearFutures, USDC-settled and inverse contracts out of an engine with no expiry, no second settle asset and no coin margin. When the universe has not loaded the clause is skipped entirely — an unreachable provider may never shrink the exchange.
- **Spot: registry and data layer only, no Trade button.** Bybit spot instruments are discoverable but are NOT made executable, because VOLTEX spot liquidity is its own internal book — a Bybit-only pair would render a Trade button over an order book that is empty by construction. The missing execution dependency is external spot routing or a market-maker, and it is reported rather than faked.
- **Normalization is from provider metadata**, never from the symbol string: `baseCoin`/`quoteCoin`/`settleCoin`/`contractType`/`status`. `contractType` goes through an explicit table so an unrecognised type is refused, not assumed perpetual. Two conversions happen once at the boundary: `price24hPcnt` FRACTION → PERCENTAGE (the off-by-100 bug already shipped once here), and `deliveryTime: "0"` → null, not 1970.
- **Load measured**: whole universe (~1,900 instruments) in **3** upstream requests (1 spot + 2 cursor pages at `limit=1000`); ticker refresh for a whole category in **1**; 100 concurrent readers in **1**; **0** per additional market. `/market/universe` is 613 KB raw / **16 KB gzipped**.
- **A real bug found and fixed in browser QA**: the first windowing implementation rendered NOTHING when a search shrank 520 rows to 6 — a stale `scrollTop` put the window past the end of the shortened list. Clamped against the current row count, with a regression test.
- **No fake zero**: unpriced markets now carry `null` instead of `0` in the futures list and sort LAST in both directions. At 520 contracts the unpriced tail used to sort as the cheapest markets on the exchange.
- **Validation actually run**: backend `tsc` PASS; frontend `tsc` PASS; production Vite build PASS. Jest **2020 passed / 2034**, +64 tests. The 14 failures are pre-existing on `52edfa8` — verified by stashing and re-running: the failing set is byte-identical before and after.
- **Two fingerprints re-taken with reasons**: `FuturesPairList.tsx` in `futuresUiPolish` (a REAL semantic change — nulls-last sorting and windowing — recorded as such, not waved through) and in `cryptoCatalogue`. No assertion weakened; both suites' actual guarantees are re-asserted.
- **Browser QA** at 1920/1440/1280/768/390 with **520 listed contracts**: 23–29 DOM rows at any time, ~400 ms to first paint, search correct and instant, **zero horizontal overflow, zero application console errors**, exactly **1** candle request and **1** order-book request regardless of market count, 23 API calls on first load (does not scale with the universe). Unpriced tail renders `—`, never `0`.
- **LIVE PROVIDER VERIFICATION NOT PERFORMED.** The sandbox egress proxy returns 403 to CONNECT for `api.bybit.com`, so no real Bybit response was ever seen. Every count in the tests is fixture-derived and none is hardcoded into production. Whether production's region is refused is unknown from here; the adapter treats a refusal as an ordinary provider failure and does nothing to work around it.
- **Untouched**: deposits, TRC20, wallets, Copy Trading economics, Crypto Card, homepage, drawing tools, CFD, Arbitrage, Analytics design, Cloudflare, Render, secrets, database. `src/futures/auditActiveOrders.ts` and `src/wallets/WalletService.ts` were read only. No financial formula, leverage tier, margin, liquidation or funding rule changed.
- Unresolved / next: venue-sourced index pricing (docs §19) is the one change that would make the Bybit-only perpetuals executable, and it is deliberately not made on data nobody has seen — it changes the input to liquidation pricing and needs live verification from a non-refused region first. Ready for owner review only.

## 2026-09-09 — Claude — Fix: market-universe freshness was discarded (PR #10 review)

- Agent: Claude. Same branch `feat/bybit-full-market-universe`, on top of `5d83b37`. Owner-found correctness issue, fixed before merge. No merge, no deploy, no scope change.
- **The bug, exactly as reported.** `ProviderCache` returns `CachedValue<T>` with `fetchedAt` and `stale`. `MarketUniverse.refresh()` copied only `.value`, discarded both, and then set `refreshedAt = now()`; `GET /market/universe` answered a hardcoded `stale: false`. Net effect: when a Bybit refresh failed and the cache served bounded stale-last-good data, the API presented day-old provider data as freshly refreshed — on the endpoint that says which markets exist.
- **Semantics now**: `refreshedAt = min(spot.fetchedAt, linear.fetchedAt)`, never `now()`; `stale = spot.stale || linear.stale`. A combination is only as fresh as its stalest half, because a caller cannot act on "perpetuals current, spot a day old" without being told which is which.
- **Failure paths keep the discipline**: a refresh failing past the stale budget still serves the previous universe (stale-last-good is untouched) but marks it `stale: true` and leaves `refreshedAt` at the real provider fetch time; so does a successful-looking empty answer. A universe that has never loaded is NOT stale — it has nothing to be stale about — and answers `available: false`. A recovered provider clears the flag and re-dates from the new read.
- **Files changed**: `src/services/marketData/bybit/MarketUniverse.ts` (snapshot gains `stale`, refresh carries `CachedValue` through, the self-clock `now()` helper removed since the class no longer dates anything itself), `src/api/routes/marketData.ts` (`stale: snapshot.stale`), plus the two test suites and this doc.
- **12 new deterministic regression tests**, driving one injected clock through the adapter's real caches rather than stubbing flags: fresh+fresh, stale+fresh, fresh+stale, stale+stale, recovery, provider failure with valid stale-last-good, stale budget exceeded, unloaded-and-failing, "never dates from its own clock", and three route tests proving `/market/universe` exposes real `stale`/`fetchedAt` and does not re-date on read.
- **Validation actually run**: backend `tsc` PASS; frontend `tsc` PASS; production Vite build PASS. Jest **2032 passed / 2046**, +12 tests; the same 14 pre-existing failures on `52edfa8`, unchanged. Market-data, routes and futures suites: **674/674 passing**.
- **Not changed**: no futures financial logic, no listing or risk rule, no leverage tier, no margin/liquidation/funding formula, and stale-last-good behaviour is intact in both layers. `FuturesMarketRegistry` still reads only `loaded` from the universe, so a stale universe restricts exactly as a fresh one does — the safe direction, and changing it would have been a listing-rule change.

## 2026-09-09 — Claude — Perceived-performance audit and route code splitting

- Agent: Claude. Branch `perf/voltex-perceived-performance` from `origin/main` `b363752` (verified). No merge, no deploy. No business, trading, financial or visual change.
- **Audit first, on a fresh production build of current main.** The dominant finding: the app shipped as ONE 1,601 kB JS chunk and ONE 337 kB CSS file. Opening the wallet downloaded the futures terminal, the charting library, the Copy Trading marketplace and all eight admin screens; a signed-out visitor reading the homepage downloaded the same. Zero `React.lazy` anywhere.
- **Implemented: route-level code splitting.** `HomePage` and `AuthPage` stay eager (lazy-loading the homepage would put a second round trip in front of the first pixel); the other 22 routes are `lazy()`. One `Suspense` boundary with `RouteShell` — the app's own ground colour at full viewport height, not a spinner and not fabricated skeleton rows, because every page here has a different layout and one generic skeleton would introduce the layout jump it was meant to prevent. An idle-only prefetch warms the user's default terminal and the wallet after first paint, so splitting does not make the first click slower.
- **Measured, production build, per page, critical path only** (bytes arriving BEFORE first meaningful render — idle prefetch excluded, since it delays no pixel): signed-out homepage JS **1728 → 763 KB (−56%)**, CSS **329 → 48 KB (−85%)**, first meaningful render **404 → 213 ms (−47%)**. Analytics −53% JS, Markets −52%, Wallet −51%, Copy Trading −47%, Crypto Card −44%, Spot/Futures −38%. Largest chunk **1601 → 655 kB**. First meaningful render improved on every one of the nine scenarios (−11% to −47%).
- **Navigation verified in a browser**: seven in-app route changes, 17–237 ms to ready, shell hold 0 ms on six of seven and 25 ms on Copy Trading, `body` background constant at `rgb(10,12,16)` throughout — no white flash, no layout jump, no page errors, no horizontal overflow.
- **500+ market scale preserved**: 23–29 DOM rows of 520 at five widths, one candle request and one order-book request regardless of market count, search correct, unpriced tail still a dash.
- **Measured but deliberately NOT changed**, with reasons, rather than rushed into a performance PR:
  - **i18n: 268 kB raw / 101 kB gzip on every page load.** `i18n.tsx` carries seven full dictionaries and all seven ship to every user. Proven by a throwaway build with the six non-RU dictionaries stubbed: shared chunk 655 → 387 kB. Splitting it is the single largest remaining win, but it restructures the file that nine preservation suites read — including one already failing — so it deserves its own change and its own review.
  - **Copy Trading marketplace: 1,759 KB raw / 131 KB gzip**, of which `trades[]` is 1,721 KB (98%), measured against a fixture built to the real engine config (`tradesPerDay 4–12` over 365 days, `followerCount: 32`). The marketplace view never reads `trades` — its only use of `selectSyntheticPeriod` is `maximumDrawdown`, which is computed from `equityHistory` — so a summary/detail split is genuinely available. It is not done here because `validStrategy` in `copyMarketplaceStore` requires `trades` to be a valid rows array, so the split also changes a safety validator on a protected flagship surface.
  - **`/futures/positions` polled by three components (3× per cycle) and `/futures/balances` by two.** A shared cache would be the obvious fix and is deliberately refused: any time-based sharing could serve a pre-order snapshot to a component refetching right after a trade. The correct fix is a single-poll store with explicit invalidation at every mutation site, which is a trading-data change, not a performance tweak.
  - **`/futures/config` fetched 3× per Futures page load** (FuturesOrderForm, FuturesTickerBar, FuturesPage). Static config, safe to memoize, but the fix lands in `api.ts` — the file two suites fingerprint, one of them already failing — for the sake of two ~1 KB requests. Not worth the fingerprint churn in this pass.
- **Files changed**: `frontend/src/App.tsx` (route splitting + idle prefetch), `frontend/src/RouteShell.tsx` (new), `frontend/src/lib/__tests__/routeCodeSplitting.test.ts` (new, 8 tests), `scripts/qa-perf-harness.cjs` (new, adds a realistically-sized marketplace fixture so the payload is measurable). No page, component, store, style, backend or financial file touched.
- **Validation actually run**: backend `tsc` PASS; frontend `tsc` PASS; production Vite build PASS. Jest **2032 passed / 2046**, +8 tests; the 14 failures are pre-existing on `b363752`, verified by stashing and re-running — identical failing set. No fingerprint needed re-taking: `App.tsx` is not fingerprinted by any suite.
- Unresolved / next, in value order: the i18n language split (101 kB gzip per page), the Copy Trading summary/detail split (1.7 MB), then the futures account-store consolidation. Ready for owner review only.

## 2026-09-09 — Claude — i18n language chunks: one dictionary per user, not seven

- Agent: Claude. Branch `perf/i18n-language-chunks` from `origin/main` `29c2e13` (verified). No merge, no deploy. Frontend i18n loading architecture only.
- **The problem, re-measured on current main** (not carried over from PR #11): the shared chunk was **763.01 kB raw / 226.69 kB gzip**, and `lib/i18n.tsx` held all seven dictionaries, so every user downloaded seven languages to read one.
- **Architecture**: `lib/i18n/locales/{ru,en,zh,es,hi,ja,ko}.ts`, one dictionary per file, plus `locales/keys.ts` deriving `Key` from Russian. Russian is statically imported — it defines `Key`, it is the default, and it is what almost every visitor gets, so making it a fetch would put a round trip in front of the first pixel. The other six are `import()`ed by a static path each, so Vite emits one chunk per locale. **No barrel module**: one `import * as locales` would put all seven back in the entry chunk with the build still succeeding, which is why a test asserts no such file exists. **Nothing is prefetched** — warming the other six would move the bytes rather than save them.
- **The no-flash design**: `lang` and `dict` are ONE piece of state, changed together, so there is no render in which the active language and the dictionary on screen disagree. While a returning non-Russian user's first dictionary loads, children are not rendered at all — the hold is the app's own background at full height. Rendering them against Russian would be the wrong-language flash; against keys would put `nav.deposit` on screen; against empty strings would be a blank page that then reflows.
- **Failure behaviour**: a failed switch leaves the last valid language untouched. A persisted language whose chunk cannot load falls back to Russian *honestly* — `lang` becomes `'ru'` too, so the switcher shows RU rather than claiming a language the user is not reading, and `exchange_lang` is deliberately NOT overwritten so a later reload retries their choice.
- **Measured, production build**: shared chunk **763.01 → 414.10 kB raw (−46%)**, **226.69 → 127.61 kB gzip (−44%)**. Critical-path JS for a default (RU) user: Copy Trading −56%, signed-out homepage −46%, Analytics −43%, Wallet −41%, Crypto Card −36%, Spot −32%, Homepage −30%, Futures −23%. Markets measured +2%, which is measurement noise in what happened to arrive before its (faster) first paint, not a regression. Locale chunks: en 48.97, zh 47.58, es 53.33, hi 86.38, ja 59.05, ko 54.55 kB raw (14.9–18.9 kB gzip).
- **Bundle split proven directly**, not inferred: a unique string from each language was searched for in the built entry chunk — Russian present, all six others absent, and each present in its own chunk.
- **Browser QA**: fresh RU fetches **zero** locale chunks; fresh EN/ZH/JA/KO each fetch exactly **one**, the right one, and paint in the correct language on the first frame that has text. Across every sampled frame, **no wrong-language text was ever observed and no translation key ever appeared**. Mobile 390 verified for JA. Switching RU→EN→RU→EN→JA→EN took 132–164 ms with **1 network request the first time a language is used and 0 on every return** — the in-memory cache works. With the `zh` chunk blocked at the network layer, the app stays alive, falls back to Russian, shows no keys and logs zero errors.
- **Translation integrity**: the dictionaries were extracted programmatically, never retyped. `i18nLanguageChunks.test.ts` (19 tests) proves each language has exactly Russian's key set with nothing lost or invented, every value is a string, `nav.bots` is still absent and `nav.botsSoon` still present, the Card/Home/Copy Trading/Futures strings survive in all seven, `LANGUAGES` order and labels are unchanged, every `localeOf` mapping is unchanged, and **each dictionary's object literal still hashes to the same digest it had inside `i18n.tsx`**.
- **Preservation tests updated, none weakened**: nine suites read `i18n.tsx` as source. Eight now read the seven locale files through a new `frontend/test-utils/i18nSource.ts` helper — a test that counted seven occurrences in one file still counts seven, and now names seven files explicitly, so a dropped language fails the read instead of quietly lowering a count. **No fingerprint was replaced to make anything pass.** The strongest evidence is `cryptoCardProductionPromotion`'s 196-entry Card digest: reading the seven locale files in language order reproduces the same 196 lines in the same order and **the hash is unchanged** — proof the split moved bytes without altering one. `walletUxRefinement` (pre-existing failing) had its `DICTS` evaluation replaced with the helper specifically so the suite still RUNS and still reports its own two pre-existing failures rather than masking them behind a load error.
- **Validation actually run**: backend `tsc` PASS; frontend `tsc` PASS; production Vite build PASS. Jest **2059 passed / 2073**, +54 tests. The 14 failures are pre-existing on `29c2e13`, verified by stashing and re-running — identical failing set, nothing added and nothing masked.
- **Files added**: 7 locale files + `keys.ts`, `frontend/test-utils/i18nSource.ts`, `i18nLanguageChunks.test.ts`. **Modified**: `lib/i18n.tsx` (dictionaries removed, loader + provider), 9 test files (i18n source access only).
- Unresolved / next: the Copy Trading marketplace payload (1.7 MB, 98% of it trades the marketplace never reads) is now the largest remaining item, unchanged from the PR #11 report. Ready for owner review only.

## 2026-09-09 — Claude — i18n: last-choice-wins guard (follow-up on PR #12)

- Agent: Claude. Same branch `perf/i18n-language-chunks`, on top of `1e4eb1a`. Owner-found race, fixed before merge. No merge, no deploy, no scope change — the i18n race only.
- **The bug, confirmed in the code.** `setLang` fired a dynamic import and committed unconditionally in `.then`. Two unloaded languages picked in quick succession left two imports in flight, and whichever *finished* last won — which is not the language chosen last. A slow EN landing after a fast JA repainted the app in English while `exchange_lang` still said Japanese. The initial-load effect had the same hole, and so did the cached path: picking an already-loaded RU committed synchronously but an in-flight EN could still overwrite it afterwards.
- **The guard**: one monotonically increasing request id in a `useRef`. Every intent to change language — the initial read included, and the cached path too — claims the next number before doing anything else, and only the holder of the current number may commit. A ref rather than state because claiming must take effect for the very next promise callback, not after a re-render. Four lines of mechanism, no architectural change.
- **A stale result still populates the cache**: `loadDictionary` writes to `loaded` before returning, so a superseded request still makes that language instant later — it simply may not change what is on screen.
- **Deterministic behavioural tests**, not source-string assertions: `i18nLanguageRace.test.ts` (10 tests) compiles and drives the real provider with React's hooks stubbed (the repo's established pattern — Jest here runs on `node` with no DOM), and holds each locale's module open until the test releases it, so completion order is chosen rather than hoped for. **Proven to catch the bug**: with the guard temporarily removed, cases A, B, D and E fail; with it, all pass. Case C (a stale *rejection* after a newer success) passes either way — the old `.catch(() => {})` was already a no-op — and is recorded as such rather than claimed as a caught regression.
- **Browser QA**: fresh RU fetches 0 locale chunks; fresh EN/JA fetch exactly one and paint correctly on the first frame with text, with no wrong-language frame ever sampled. With EN artificially delayed 2.5 s so it lands *after* JA: final language JA, `exchange_lang` JA. With EN in flight and cached RU then selected: final RU, storage RU. Six switches across EN/RU/JA cost **2** locale requests in total. Blocked `zh` chunk: app alive, honest RU fallback, no keys, zero console errors.
- **Validation**: backend `tsc` PASS; frontend `tsc` PASS; production build PASS (shared chunk 388.20 kB / 131.95 kB gzip — unchanged by the guard). Jest **2069 passed / 2083**, +10 tests; the same 14 pre-existing failures. `i18nLanguageChunks` (19) and `routeCodeSplitting` (8) both still pass.
- **Unchanged**: not one translation string, the locale files are byte-identical, RU still static, the six others still lazy, no prefetch, `exchange_lang` semantics identical, no preservation hash re-taken.
## 2026-09-09 — Claude — Copy Trading: display rows on the wire, statistics from the whole history

- Agent: Claude. Branch `perf/copy-marketplace-payload` from `origin/main` `29c2e13` (re-fetched and verified). No merge, no deploy. A data-contract and payload change only — no economics, no fee logic, no minimum, no visual change.
- **The problem, re-measured on the same fixture**: the marketplace aggregate was **2,081,910 B raw / 160,457 B gzip**, of which `trades[]` was **2,088,896 B across the two strategies** — the browser was downloading 2,920 trade rows per strategy to render cards that read none of them, and a profile table that showed at most a hundred.
- **The separation**: `src/services/copyTrading/marketplaceSummary.ts` reads the COMPLETE history, computes the trade-derived aggregates per period, and only then reduces the wire payload to the latest ten rows. So the profile still says "Total Trades 2 920" above a table of ten. Nothing is recomputed from ten, nothing estimated, nothing padded.
- **Which numbers were at risk, exactly**: `selectSyntheticPeriod` derived `winRate`, `averagePnl`, `averageTradesPerWeek`, `averageHoldingTimeMinutes`, `totalTrades`, `winningTrades`, `losingTrades` — and `profitFactor` when no economics block exists — from the full array. Those seven-plus fields now read `tradeStats[period]`; everything else (ROI, PnL, drawdown, Sharpe, Sortino, volatility, equity, daily, cumulative PnL, follower PnL) already came from equity/daily/economics and is untouched. The local computation is kept as a fallback, so any caller or fixture still holding a full history behaves exactly as before.
- **Proven equal, not asserted equal**: `copyMarketplacePayload.test.ts` runs `selectSyntheticPeriod` over the full history and over the summary for all four periods and compares seventeen fields plus the equity, daily and cumulative series — identical. A companion test confirms the comparison is not vacuous: with `tradeStats` stripped, the same summary yields a total of 10 and a different win rate.
- **Measured on the QA fixture, before and after**: raw **2,081,910 → 199,320 B (−90.4%)**, gzip **160,457 → 22,547 B (−85.9%)**, trade bytes **2,088,896 → 7,076 B (−99.66%)**. Every non-trade section is intact on the wire — 400 equity points, 400 AUM points, 400 daily results, 32 followers, 52 weekly, 12 monthly, full economics and analytics.
- **The validator got stricter, not laxer.** `validStrategy` still validates every visible row exactly as before, and now additionally requires: at most ten rows, newest-first ordering, a complete `tradeStats` block for all four periods, and `tradeHistoryCount === tradeStats.ALL.totalTrades` and `>= trades.length`. A payload carrying ten rows *without* the aggregates — the shape that would silently make every trade-derived figure describe ten trades — is rejected outright.
- **A regression I introduced and fixed**: the first version made `summarizeStrategy` unconditional, so a strategy payload without `trades`/`equityHistory` threw and the route answered 503. It is now a no-op on anything it cannot summarize, which restored `copyPerformance.test.ts` to passing unmodified — the existing test caught it, and no test was edited to accommodate the bug.
- **Browser QA** on the production build: one marketplace request (202 KB), 21 cards rendered, Nazar and Ksenia both present, search works, tabs work, no provider branding, no horizontal overflow, **0 page errors and 0 console errors**. Profile opened: **"Показано 10 из 2 172"** for the selected period, exactly 10 table rows, still 10 after switching 7D/30D/90D/ALL, and the statistics panel reads **Total Trades 2 920 · Winning 1 946 · Losing 974 · Win Rate 66,64%** — full-history figures above a ten-row table. Back to marketplace works. Pagination: 32 traders over 2 pages at 16/page; **both pages render 16 complete cards with zero em-dashes and the same stat count**, so completeness does not depend on the page. A page 20 does not exist in this roster — the ordinary traders are local model data, not API data, so their card quality is unaffected by this change by construction.
- **Validation**: backend `tsc` PASS; frontend `tsc` PASS; production build PASS. Jest **2071 passed / 2085**; the 14 failures are pre-existing on `29c2e13`, verified by stashing and re-running — identical set. Every Copy Trading suite passes except the two pre-existing ones, including all the Nazar/Ksenia economics suites (`ReviewPerformanceV8Master`, `ReviewFollowerLedgerV8`, `ReviewPerformanceAnalyticsV8`, `kseniaReview`, `NazarPresentation`, `CanonicalSourcePreservation`) — their economics are untouched.
- **Files added**: `src/services/copyTrading/marketplaceSummary.ts`, its test, `frontend/src/lib/__tests__/copyMarketplacePayload.test.ts`. **Modified**: `src/api/routes/copyPerformance.ts`, `frontend/src/lib/syntheticCopyTrading.ts`, `frontend/src/lib/copyMarketplaceStore.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/copy-trading-bolt/components.tsx`, `scripts/qa-perf-harness.cjs` (fixture ids corrected — the old fixture used `nazar`/`ksenia` where the validator requires `VX-001`/`VX-KSENIA`, so it had never actually validated).
- Unresolved / next: the master-turnover fallback now shows a dash rather than a ten-trade sum when a payload carries no economics figure; for Nazar and Ksenia economics is always present, so this path is unreachable in production and is a correctness guard rather than a visible change.

## 2026-09-09 — Claude — Copy Trading follow-up: Main Markets from the whole history, and an honest measurement

- Agent: Claude. Same branch `perf/copy-marketplace-payload`, on top of `234c968`, merged with current `origin/main` `913b34d`. Two owner-found review issues, fixed before merge. **No new PR, no merge, no deploy, no new scope** — Main Markets and the measurement only.
- **Sync first.** `origin/main` `913b34d` (the PR #12 i18n merge) was merged into the branch as `b3c4770`. Reconciled by intent, not by side: the only conflict was `docs/AI_HANDOFF.md`, where both agents had appended, and both sets of entries were kept in order. Verified after the merge that `913b34d` is an ancestor, that all seven i18n locale files and `frontend/test-utils/i18nSource.ts` are present, and that the Copy Trading files still carry the PR #13 work.
- **Issue 1 — Main Markets was still derived from the ten visible rows.** Correct: `TradingProfilePanel` called `syntheticMainMarkets(strategyTrades)`, and after PR #13 `strategyTrades` is the ten-row display array. A strategy that traded BTC/ETH/SOL all year but XRP this morning would have advertised XRP.
- **The fix keeps the existing algorithm verbatim.** `syntheticMainMarkets` was **ported byte-for-byte** into `marketplaceSummary.mainMarketsOf` — same symbol normalisation, same count sort, same `b[1] - a[1] || a[0].localeCompare(b[0])` tie-break, same `slice(0, 3)`, and **the same XRP retention rule** (XRP is appended when traded but outside the top three, so the list can legitimately return four entries). It was **not** replaced with a new "top 3". The two implementations are asserted equal over eight generated histories plus an edge case in the tests, so the port cannot drift.
- **Computed before trimming, transported explicitly, validated at the boundary.** `summarizeStrategy` calls `mainMarketsOf(data.trades)` — the COMPLETE history — before `trades` is reduced to ten, and puts the answer on the wire as `mainMarkets: string[]`. `validStrategy` now requires it to be a non-empty array of non-empty strings; a payload with ten rows and no `mainMarkets` is rejected, not silently recomputed. The panel prefers the transported value, keeps the local computation as a fallback for any caller still holding a full history, and keeps the pre-existing model-data branch last.
- **Proven to catch the bug, both ways.** The fixture is deliberately discriminating: full history BTC/ETH/SOL, **latest twelve rows XRP/DOGE only**. Against the literal PR #13 state (no `mainMarkets` at all) **five tests fail**; against a simulated "derive it from the visible ten" the three Main Markets tests fail. With the fix, all pass. Expected result `["BTC","ETH","SOL","XRP"]` — top three by count plus XRP by the retention rule — is what the wire and the browser both show.
- **Browser QA at 1440 and 390**, production build against the discriminating fixture: **Main Markets reads `BTC · ETH · SOL · XRP` while every one of the ten visible trade rows is DOGE or XRP** — the display data could not have produced that answer. One marketplace request (202,230 B), 21 cards, 10 rows before and after switching 7D/30D/90D/ALL, "Показано 10 из 2 175" for the period and 2 920 for ALL, Win Rate 66,67%, no provider branding, no horizontal overflow, **0 page errors and 0 console errors** at both widths.
- **Issue 2 — the old measurement was impossible and is withdrawn.** The PR reported `raw 2,081,910 B` alongside `trade bytes 2,088,896 B`: a part larger than the whole. Cause: the two figures were produced by different serialisers — Python `json.dumps` (spaces after separators) for one side, the server's compact JSON for the other — so they never described the same bytes. Both figures are replaced, not adjusted.
- **Re-measured properly**: one fixture, one serialised payload, the same envelope on both sides (`envelopeKeysBefore === envelopeKeysAfter === "errors,generatedAt,identities,ksenia,nazar"`), `JSON.stringify` for both raw and trade bytes, and permanent sanity assertions `tradeBytesBefore <= rawPayloadBefore` and `tradeBytesAfter <= rawPayloadAfter` in the test so an impossible number can never be reported again. Live endpoint: raw **2,096,380 → 202,230 B (−90.35%)**, gzip **168,709 → 24,659 B (−85.38%)**, trade bytes **1,902,030 → 6,448 B (−99.66%)**, 2,920 rows → 10, `tradeHistoryCount` still 2,920.
