## 2026-09-09 — Claude — Futures: one shared authenticated account store

- Agent: Claude. Branch `perf/futures-account-store` from `origin/main` `cbe066e` (fetched and verified against GitHub). Audit first, then implementation, because the audit found real duplication. Not merged, not deployed.
- **The audit, traced not guessed.** Five `setInterval`s over three authenticated endpoints, because three components each fetched what they personally needed: `/futures/positions` from `FuturesAccountSummary` (5s), `FuturesOrderForm` (5s) and `FuturesPositionsPanel` (4s); `/futures/balances` from the summary (5s) and the form (5s), plus `AssetsPanel` (4s) whenever the Assets tab was open. `/futures/orders/me` had one caller. `/futures/positions/history` was correctly fetched on tab open only. Measured in a browser against a fixture harness: **75 account requests per idle minute**, 7 on initial load.
- **Worse than the count**: the three copies of `positions` were fetched at different instants, so the margin summary, the order form's exposure preview and the positions table could each describe a different snapshot of the same account at the same moment.
- **A correctness bug found during the audit, and fixed.** `FuturesAccountSummary` initialised its balance to `{available: 0, locked: 0}` and swallowed failures, so a failed `/futures/balances` rendered **Маржинальный баланс 0.00 USDT, Доступная маржа 0.00 USDT, 0.00% / 0.00%** — reproduced in the browser against an injected 503. That is the fake zero VOLTEX forbids, on the most sensitive figures on the page. Unknown is now `null` and renders as a dash; a real 0 is still 0.
- **The architecture**: `lib/futuresAccountStore.ts`, a deliberate copy of the proven `marketDataStore` shape — one timer and one in-flight request **per resource**, reference-counted, polling at the fastest cadence any live subscriber asked for. **Not over-consolidated**: the four resources stay four endpoints behind one store, because a balances failure must not blank the positions table and history does not need polling. No Redis, no new service, no backend change at all.
- **Event-driven refresh** replaces waiting for the next poll: order placed → balances + positions + orders; position closed → positions + history + balances (a close releases margin, which used to lag up to 5s); transfer completed → balances, from both the terminal modal and the Wallet page.
- **Session safety**, which is the whole risk of a module-level account cache: a logout here is a route change, not a reload, so nothing unmounts. `api.ts` gained an `onSessionChange` notifier (`setToken`/`clearToken` broadcast; no request, URL, header or token handling changed). The store resets on every session change, and every response is committed only if BOTH its session generation and its captured token still match — so a request issued under user A cannot land in user B's state however slow it is. A token changed with no notification at all (another tab) is still caught by the token comparison.
- **Measured, same harness and scenarios, before → after**: initial load **7 → 4**; 60s idle **75 → 39 (−48%)**; 5min idle **375 → 195 (−48%)**; contract switch **6 → 3**; order placement **6 → 3**; transfer **9 → 5**; Assets tab open for 20s **25 → 13 (−48%)**. Position close went **4 → 5**: one request more, because it now also refreshes history and balances instead of leaving the released margin stale. Reported as the correctness trade it is, not hidden.
- **Failure behaviour, observed rather than asserted**: balances 503 → margin balance and available margin show a dash while PnL from positions still shows (partial failure stays partial); positions 503 → PnL and both margin percentages dash while balances still show; 15s of failing refreshes over healthy data → last-good values stay on screen, nothing blanks, nothing zeroes; recovery restores fresh data.
- **Tests**: `futuresAccountStore.test.ts`, 22 deterministic tests — dedup, cadence, teardown, mid-flight join, a 60s request budget, resources-not-merged, event refresh, and seven session/no-fake-zero cases. **Proven to catch the bug**: with both commit guards removed, the two cross-session tests fail; with them, all pass.
- **Financial logic unchanged, and proven so.** `futuresFinalPolish`'s 24 behavioural tests — every leverage cap, every order payload, the high-leverage confirmation, the exposure projection and the Spot/Futures balance separation — pass **unmodified** after the test's module shim was taught about the new import. No margin, liquidation, funding, tier, preflight, reconciliation or settlement code was touched; `src/` has no change at all.
- **Preservation fingerprints re-taken: four, each documented in place with exactly what differs** — `FuturesOrderForm.tsx`, `FuturesAccountSummary.tsx`, `lib/api.ts` (in `futuresTickerHeader`, +35/−0), `wallet-v3/TransferModal.tsx` (+4/−0). **None weakened, and no assertion removed.** The two hashes already failing on `cbe066e` (`DepositModal.tsx`, `api.ts` in `walletUxRefinement`) were deliberately left untouched.
- **Validation**: backend `tsc` PASS; frontend `tsc` PASS; production Vite build PASS. Jest **2 136 passed / 2 150** (+22 tests). The 14 failures are **identical to `cbe066e`** — verified by running the full suite in a worktree at that commit: same names, same count, nothing added and nothing masked.
- **Browser QA** at 1440 and 390 on the production build: user A's figures byte-identical to before the change (10 250.00 / 10 000.00 / +11.75 / 5.07% / 0.25%), user B sees only user B's, SPA logout→login leaks nothing, reload costs 4 account calls, Assets tab renders real rows, no horizontal overflow, **0 page errors**. The only console errors are the sandbox's blocked Kraken WebSocket, unrelated to this change.
- **Files added**: `frontend/src/lib/futuresAccountStore.ts`, `frontend/src/lib/useFuturesAccount.ts`, its test, `scripts/qa-futures-account-harness.cjs` (a SEPARATE harness — `qa-perf-harness.cjs` stays GET-only, its safety guarantee intact). **Modified**: `lib/api.ts`, `FuturesAccountSummary.tsx`, `FuturesOrderForm.tsx`, `FuturesPositionsPanel.tsx`, `AssetsPanel.tsx`, `FuturesTransferModal.tsx`, `wallet-v3/TransferModal.tsx`, three test files.
- Unresolved / next: `PriceChart` polls the SPOT `/orders/me?status=PENDING_TRIGGER` every 4s on the Futures terminal (97 requests in the QA run) for conditional-order lines that are a spot concept. Left untouched deliberately — the component is shared with the Spot terminal, so changing it risks Spot for a non-account resource. Reported, not fixed.

## 2026-09-09 — Claude — Futures account store: review follow-up (same PR #14)

- Agent: Claude. Same branch `perf/futures-account-store`, on top of `8ca24c7`. Three owner-found review findings, fixed before merge. No new PR, no merge, no deploy, no redesign — the store architecture is unchanged.
- **Finding 1 — position history WAS being polled, and my own comment and PR text said it was not.** The panel read `useFuturesAccount(tab === 'open' ? { positions: 4000 } : { positionHistory: 60_000 })`. A cadence of any value creates a timer; `60_000` is still a timer. The claim "still not polled at all" in the PR body was wrong, and the code contradicted the comment three lines above it. Now: `{}` for the history tab — an absent key is what tells the store never to create a timer — plus one explicit `refreshFuturesAccount(['positionHistory'])` on tab activation, and the existing refreshes on close and `refreshKey`. **Measured**: holding the history tab open for 60 s cost **1** history request before and **0** after. The accidental poll was 1/minute per open tab — real, small, and now gone.
- **Finding 2 — `data ?? []` turned unknown into empty.** A failed or not-yet-answered positions request rendered "Нет открытых позиций", and history rendered "История позиций пуста". Both are claims about someone's account that nobody made. The panel now distinguishes three states: `null` + loading → "Загрузка...", `null` + failed → "Не удалось загрузить позиции", `[]` → the real empty state. Existing keys and the exact pattern `CfdPositionsPanel` already uses — **no i18n key added, no locale file touched**. Last-good rows still survive a failed refresh.
- **Finding 3 — the same coercion in the order form, in the OPTIMISTIC direction.** `positions ?? []` and `activeOrders ?? []` fed `projectFuturesExposureNotional`, so an account whose existing position had not been fetched projected as if it held none — which yields the *highest* leverage tier. Now `effectiveMaxLeverage` is `null` while those inputs are genuinely needed and unknown, which suspends the leverage slider and the submit guard until the account state is known. **The projection is needed only when `!reduceOnly && notional > 0`**: reduce-only and zero-notional short-circuit to a REAL 0 before either resource is read, so the initial paint is untouched and risk-*reducing* orders stay available during an outage — which is when a trader needs them most.
- **Formulas untouched**: `projectFuturesExposureNotional`, `getLeverageTier`, the tier table, `previewLiquidationPrice` and the order payload are byte-unchanged. `src/` still has no change at all.
- **Tests**: `futuresAccountUnknownState.test.ts`, 20 new behavioural tests driving the real TSX (no-cadence-for-history, one load on activation, zero loads over 60 s, close-refreshes-history, A/B/C/D unknown-vs-empty for both resources, stale-last-good, and A–E for the exposure preview including the exact-formula equality and the payload). Plus 2 store-level tests that an empty `wants` creates no timer and that history still loads on demand for a subscriber that polls nothing. **Each fix proven load-bearing by reverting it in isolation**: finding 1 → 1 test fails, finding 2 → 3 fail, finding 3 → 4 fail.
- **A test fixture was incomplete, not weakened.** `futuresFinalPolish`'s `leverageForm()` never stubbed `getFuturesPositions`/`getMyFuturesOrders` — it did not have to, because the old code coerced the unanswered requests to `[]`. They now return a REAL empty account (`[]`), which projects exactly the candidate's own notional, the same number the coercion produced. **All 24 assertions are unchanged and pass.**
- **One fingerprint re-taken**: `FuturesOrderForm.tsx`, documented in place with exactly what differs. No assertion removed, nothing weakened, and the pre-existing failures on `cbe066e` left untouched.
- **Request counts unchanged elsewhere**, re-measured on the same harness against the previous PR head: initial load 4, 60 s idle 39, 5 min idle 195, contract switch 3, order placement 3, position close 5, transfer 5, Assets tab 13, reload 4 — all identical. History tab open 3 → 3 (one history load either way); history tab held 60 s **37 → 36**. **No increase anywhere.**
- **Validation**: backend `tsc` PASS; frontend `tsc` PASS; production Vite build PASS. Jest **2 158 passed / 2 172** (+22 on this follow-up, +44 over main). The 14 failures are identical to `cbe066e`.
- **Browser QA** at 1440 and 390: positions 503 → "Не удалось загрузить позиции" and the leverage slider **absent**; a genuinely empty account → "Нет открытых позиций" and the slider present at its real ceiling; history 503 → error, not "пуста"; genuinely empty history → "пуста"; orders 503 → slider **absent**; healthy → slider present. **0 page errors** throughout.
- **Deliberately NOT touched**: the `PriceChart` Spot `/orders/me?status=PENDING_TRIGGER` poll on the Futures terminal remains a separate future task, documented only.

## 2026-09-09 — Claude — PriceChart: spot conditional orders stay on spot

- Agent: Claude. Branch `perf/pricechart-market-orders` from `origin/main` `af978e7` (fetched and verified against GitHub; PR #14 merged). Not merged, not deployed. A removal, not a replacement — no futures substitute was invented.
- **The exact cause**: `PriceChart`'s conditional-order poll had dependencies `[pair]` and no market gate at all, so the effect ran identically on both terminals. `market` existed as a prop but only namespaced saved drawings and gated the spot axis refinement. Three further consumers were equally ungated: the post-drag refetch, `api.updateOrderTrigger`, and the SVG line overlay. `/orders/me?status=PENDING_TRIGGER` is the **spot** order book; there is no futures conditional-order contract behind it.
- **Measured on main before the change** (production build, fixture harness, real browser): the futures terminal issued **2** conditional-order requests on load, **15 per idle minute**, **3** more across three contract changes — and **drew 1 spot SL/TP line on the futures chart**. Identical at 1440 and 390.
- **The fix** is one derived boolean, `const spotConditionalOrders = market === 'spot'`, applied at four points: the poll effect returns before fetching or timing anything (and clears any lines a previous spot context left), `startDrag` refuses before `preventDefault`, the post-drag refetch refuses to commit, and the overlay does not render. The poll's deps gained the gate, so leaving spot re-runs the effect and its existing `cancelled` cleanup drops a request already in the air — correct without relying on the router remounting.
- **Measured after**: futures **0 / 0 / 0** conditional-order requests and **0** `updateOrderTrigger`, at both widths, with **0 lines drawn**. Spot is byte-identical: **2** on load, **15** per idle minute, **2** per pair change, 1 line still drawn, and the separate `OpenOrdersPanel` poll (`PENDING_TRIGGER,OPEN,PARTIALLY_FILLED` — a different consumer this task did not touch) unchanged at 2 / 15 / 2.
- **Tests**: `priceChartMarketOrders.test.ts`, 19 deterministic tests driving the REAL `PriceChart` through a hook-stub harness with a fake `lightweight-charts` complete enough that `priceToCoordinate` answers. That last part matters: without a working chart the overlay renders no line for *either* market, and the futures "no lines" assertion would have passed for the wrong reason. Spot genuinely draws a line in these tests, futures genuinely does not. Covers A–I plus a full drag round-trip asserting the exact `updateOrderTrigger` payload (trigger, and execution price moved by the original trigger-to-execution gap). **Proven to catch the bug**: with the gate removed, 8 tests fail — and all 11 spot tests still pass, which is the signature that spot is untouched in both directions.
- **One structural assertion updated, no fingerprint re-taken.** `chartDrawings` pins the literal `'</g> {conditionalOrders.map'` to prove the conditional lines sit outside the hidden drawing group. It now pins `'</g> {spotConditionalOrders && conditionalOrders.map'` — the same property, strengthened, since it additionally pins the gate to that exact position. No hash anywhere was re-taken.
- **Spot preserved**: cadence, pair filter, line rendering, drag, `updateOrderTrigger`, OCO, failure behaviour, drawing tools, indicators, candle fetching, drawing persistence, price formatting and the toolbar are all untouched. `src/` has no change at all — no backend, no financial logic, no futures account store.
- **Browser QA** at 1440 and 390 on the production build: both charts render, candles load, the drawing rail is present on both, contract/pair changes work, futures shows no spot conditional lines and no spot order traffic, spot still shows its line. **0 page errors and 0 console errors** on every run.
- **A QA-harness bug I introduced and fixed**: my first `/market/external/rankings` fixture returned `{gainers, losers}` where the real contract is `{source, rankings}`, which made a consumer map over `undefined` and log a render error on *both* builds. Corrected; both builds then reported 0 console errors. The harness (`qa-futures-account-harness.cjs`) was extended to serve both terminals — spot conditional orders, `PATCH /orders/:id/trigger`, and the market fixtures a real chart needs.
- **Validation**: backend `tsc` PASS; frontend `tsc` PASS; production Vite build PASS. Jest **2 177 passed / 2 191** (+19). The 14 failures are identical to `af978e7`, verified by running the full suite in a worktree at that commit.
- **Files**: `frontend/src/components/PriceChart.tsx` (+43/−7), `frontend/src/lib/__tests__/priceChartMarketOrders.test.ts` (new), `frontend/src/lib/__tests__/chartDrawings.test.ts` (one literal), `scripts/qa-futures-account-harness.cjs` (QA only).
- Unresolved / next: a real futures trigger-order chart feature remains possible only if a futures backend contract for it is built first. Nothing here presumes one.

## 2026-09-09 — Claude — Futures: professional order panel

- Agent: Claude. Branch `feat/futures-order-panel` from `origin/main` `8e60efb` (fetched and verified against GitHub; PR #15 merged). Not merged, not deployed. A frontend UX change: the execution and risk contract is untouched and `src/` has no change at all.
- **The UX problem**: margin mode had a full-width segmented toggle, leverage had its own slider with its own presets, and position size had a third control. Three stacked blocks, two of them sliders.
- **The change**: one compact `[ Изолированная · 10x ▾ ]` trigger opens a popover carrying margin mode and a leverage **numeric stepper with chips** — no second slider. The panel now has exactly ONE persistent slider and it is position size, with the owner-approved 10/25/50/75/100 presets. **Measured**: the form is **613 → 502 px** tall (−18%) and the panel **1004 → 893 px** at 1440, **979 → 867 px** at 390; `input[type=range]` inside the panel **1 → 0**, percent track stays 1.
- **A different UI over the same values.** `FuturesMarginLeverage` computes nothing: `min` is `config.minLeverage`, `max` is the live `effectiveMaxLeverage`, `warningThreshold` is `config.highLeverageWarningThreshold`. Chips never exceed the live ceiling and the ceiling itself is always offered — a tier max of 20x shows 1/5/10/20 and no actionable 50x. `getLeverageTier`, `projectFuturesExposureNotional` and `previewLiquidationPrice` are byte-unchanged.
- **PR #14's rules preserved**: `max` is `null` when the exposure inputs are unknown, and the control renders disabled showing a dash rather than a ceiling it cannot justify; an unknown balance still sizes nothing; reduce-only still needs no exposure data.
- **FEE AUDIT — no authoritative futures fee exists.** No fee rate in `src/futures/`, none in `src/config/futuresConfig.ts`, and **no fee column anywhere in `prisma/schema.prisma`**. The only `feeRate` in the codebase is Copy Trading's PERFORMANCE fee, a different concept that does not apply to an order. The row that read **`0.00 USDT (0%)` was therefore fabricated** — not a real zero, a number nobody computed. It now renders `—`. No maker/taker rate was invented and no external venue's fee was copied.
- **A second fabricated zero, found and fixed**: Order Value and Required Margin printed `0.00 USDT` when the price was unknown (a MARKET order before the mark price lands), which reads as a free order rather than an unknown one. Both formulas are unchanged; only the rendering of an unknown is now a dash.
- **TP/SL AUDIT — no futures TP/SL support exists, and none was faked.** `grep` over `src/futures/` for `takeProfit|stopLoss|triggerPrice|PENDING_TRIGGER` returns **zero** hits. `FuturesOrder` has no trigger column and its `status` is `OPEN | PARTIALLY_FILLED | FILLED | CANCELLED` — no `PENDING_TRIGGER`. The verified `POST /futures/orders` Zod schema accepts exactly `symbol, side, type, price?, quantity, leverage, marginType, reduceOnly?`. No TP/SL controls were added, and PR #15's removal of Spot conditional orders from Futures was not undone — the panel references no `PENDING_TRIGGER`, `updateOrderTrigger`, `getMyOrders`, `ocoGroupId` or `triggerPrice`, asserted in tests.
- **What real Futures TP/SL would require** (a separate task): a `triggerPrice` + `triggerDirection` column on `FuturesOrder` and a `PENDING_TRIGGER` status; a mark-price watcher that arms and fires those orders against the index/mark feed rather than the spot book; trigger validation in `placeOrder` (trigger vs mark on the correct side, reduce-only interaction, margin reservation semantics for a resting trigger); cancellation and OCO grouping; liquidation-engine interaction so a trigger cannot race a liquidation; and route + client contract additions. None of it exists today.
- **Payload identical**: browser QA captured the real `POST /futures/orders` bodies for LIMIT Long, LIMIT Short, MARKET Long, MARKET Short and Reduce Only — each carries exactly the eight contract fields, MARKET omits `price`, and no field was added.
- **Spot untouched**: `PercentSlider` gained an optional `presets` prop defaulting to the existing `[0,25,50,75,100]`, and the track still snaps to 0, so any other caller behaves identically. `LeverageSlider` and `MarginTypeToggle` are unchanged and `LeverageSlider` is still used by CFD.
- **Tests**: `futuresOrderPanel.test.ts`, 31 deterministic tests driving the real components (A one slider, B bounds incl. a 37x non-round ceiling and an unknown ceiling, C formulas unchanged across selections, D presets, E–H the exact payload for LIMIT/MARKET/CROSS/SHORT/ReduceOnly, I–K unknown balance/exposure/fee, L no spot TP/SL surface, plus popover semantics and CSS scoping).
- **Existing suites taught the new control, never weakened**: `futuresFinalPolish`'s leverage tests now read `FuturesMarginLeverage`'s `min`/`max`/`leverage` and drive its real chips — **every asserted value is unchanged** (same ceilings 100/50/20/10/5, same payloads, same confirmation rule) and all 24 pass. `futuresAccountUnknownState` follows the same rename. One fingerprint (`FuturesOrderForm.tsx` in `futuresUiPolish`) re-taken with the difference documented in place; no other hash touched.

Unknown balance → `—` and the preset sizes nothing; unknown positions or orders → the control is disabled and shows `—`.

## 2026-09-09 — Claude — Hotfix: the homepage owns its Tailwind utilities

- Agent: Claude. Branch `fix/homepage-tailwind-utilities` from `origin/main` `a35e7cc` (fetched and verified; the PR #16 merge). Not merged, not deployed. A CSS ownership/build fix only — no design, no logic, no backend.
- **Root cause, confirmed exactly as reported.** `HomePage.tsx` and its sections are written in Tailwind utilities, and `tailwind.config.js` has always scanned `./src/pages/home/**`, so the utilities were *generated*. What was missing is where they are **emitted**: Tailwind writes the layer into whichever stylesheet carries `@tailwind utilities`, and only two files did — `settings-arctic/tailwind-utilities.css` (imported only by the lazy `SettingsPage`) and `crypto-card-final/crypto-card.css` (imported only by the lazy `CardPage`). While the app shipped one monolithic stylesheet that was invisible: the homepage was silently borrowing another route's utilities. Route splitting moved both into lazy chunks `/` never loads, and the homepage — the eager landing route with no CSS chunk of its own — was left with `.vx-home` chrome and no layout.
- **Verified on the production build BEFORE the fix**: `/` requested exactly one stylesheet, `index-O79_81Wl.css`, containing **zero** occurrences of `flex-col`, `gap-5`, `pb-7`, `grid-cols-1`, `md:` or `text-home-muted`; those strings lived only in `CardPage-*.css` and `SettingsPage-*.css`. In a clean browser context that visited only `/`: `.flex-col` computed `display: block / flex-direction: row`, `.gap-5` computed `gap: normal`, `.pb-7` computed `0px`, `.grid` computed `block`, the responsive grid computed `grid-template-columns: none`, and `text-home-muted` inherited `rgb(230,234,241)` instead of its configured `#8b97a8`.
- **The fix**: a new `frontend/src/pages/home/home-tailwind-utilities.css` containing `@tailwind utilities;`, imported by `HomePage.tsx` **after** `./home.css`. Two lines of product change.
- **Why a separate file rather than appending to home.css.** It mirrors `settings-arctic/tailwind-utilities.css`, the strongest existing precedent, and it keeps `home.css` **byte-for-byte unchanged** — that file is fingerprinted by two preservation suites, and a build-plumbing fix has no business perturbing the hash that exists to prove the approved design has not drifted. I appended to `home.css` first, saw `cryptoCardVisualConsistency`'s homepage-CSS hash go red, and moved the directive to its own file rather than re-take that hash. **No fingerprint was re-taken.**
- **Import order is load-bearing**: `home.css`'s `:where(.vx-home)` block is the homepage's zero-specificity stand-in for the preflight that is deliberately off, but its ordinary `.vx-home` rules score 0,1,0 — the same as a utility. CSS is emitted in import order, so the utilities layer must come second to win those ties. Pinned by a test.
- **Verified AFTER**: `.flex-col` → `flex/column`, `.gap-5` → `20px`, `.pb-7` → `28px`, `.grid` → `grid`, responsive grid → `489.594px 870.406px` at 1440 and `342px` at 390 (the `md:` breakpoint ships), `text-home-muted` → `rgb(139,151,168)` = the configured `#8b97a8`. `<main>` went `block/row/normal/0px` → `flex/column/20px/28px`. Per-section heights: hero 3227→541, ticker 663→64, market overview 1618→237, Crypto Card 2059→757, markets 466→582, FAQ 164→271, header 109→58, footer 458→334. **CTA buttons went from 0 found to 3**, gold `rgb(224,169,63)` at 6px radius. Document height 8764→2989 px as stacked blocks collapsed into the real layout.
- **Preflight is still off**, asserted against the built CSS: no `*,::before,::after{box-sizing:border-box`, no `html{line-height:1.5`. `corePlugins: { preflight: false }` untouched.
- **Route splitting untouched**: `App.tsx` is unmodified, HomePage stays eager, every other page stays lazy, and Settings/Crypto Card keep their own directives — the fix ADDS one, it does not move one. Asserted.
- **The cost, measured and reported**: the eager `index-*.css` grows **49.34 → 93.28 kB raw, 11.22 → 19.59 kB gzip** (+43.9 kB / +8.4 kB gzip), because Tailwind emits the union of all scanned utilities into every file carrying the directive — the same duplication Settings and Crypto Card already carry. That is the price of the homepage no longer depending on another route's chunk; a per-entry Tailwind config could trim it later, and would be a much larger change than a production hotfix should make.
- **Tests**: `homepageTailwindUtilities.test.ts`, 13 tests covering all seven required properties plus import order, `home.css` staying free of `@tailwind`, the preflight stand-in surviving, and that `crypto-card.css`/`tailwind-utilities.css` remain reachable ONLY from their lazy pages. **Proven load-bearing**: removing the directive and rebuilding fails 3, including the build-output assertion. Two of my own assertions were wrong first — one forbade the homepage reusing Crypto Card *components* (legitimate; those files import no CSS) and one matched `tailwind-utilities.css` as a substring of `home-tailwind-utilities.css`, silently weakening itself. Both corrected.
- **Validation**: backend `tsc` PASS; frontend `tsc` PASS; production build PASS. Jest **2 230 passed / 2 244** (+13). The 14 failures are identical to `a35e7cc`, verified in a worktree at that commit.
- **Browser QA at 1440 and 390** on the production build, in a context that visited only `/` and never Settings, Wallet, Card, Trade or Futures: exactly one CSS request, full approved layout restored (header, hero, CTAs, terminal preview, ticker strip, market overview, Crypto Card, markets, FAQ, footer), no horizontal overflow, **0 page errors and 0 console errors**.
- Unresolved / next: `tailwind.config.js` also scans `./src/pages/register/**`, `./src/pages/WalletPage.tsx` and `./src/pages/wallet-v3/**`, and **none of those owns a `@tailwind utilities` directive either** — the same latent bug. They are lazy routes, so whether they break depends on which chunk happens to carry the layer; worth auditing next, and deliberately out of scope for this hotfix.

## 2026-09-10 — Claude — Audit: who owns the Tailwind utilities Register and Wallet use

- Agent: Claude. Branch `audit/register-wallet-tailwind-ownership` from `origin/main` `42a154691af173d755eab2810f8ad09dd48feab8` (fetched and verified as the PR #17 merge). Not merged, not deployed. **No product code changed** — this is the audit the previous entry left as "worth auditing next", and it deliberately stops short of the build restructuring it identifies.
- **Verdict — `/register` is A, `/wallet` is B. Neither is C.** Full evidence in `docs/REGISTER_WALLET_TAILWIND_AUDIT.md`.
- **`/register` owns everything it renders.** It uses **no Tailwind utility at all**: all 17 class names under `src/pages/register/**` are `vx-auth-*`, hand-written in `auth-shell/auth-shell.css`, which `AuthShell.tsx` declares and `RegisterPage.tsx` imports — ownership through the component graph, so the stylesheet travels in whatever chunk pulls the page. The card column on `/register` is `RegistrationCardVisual` (inline SVG); `HomeCryptoCard`, which *does* use utilities, is the `/login` variant.
- **`/wallet` does not own them.** `wallet-v3/wallet.css` carries no directive, and the built `WalletPage-*.css` (2.50 kB) contains **0** of the 293 utilities the page uses. All 293 are emitted only in `index-*.css` and `SettingsPage-*.css`. It is styled today solely because HomePage is eager, so PR #17's utilities layer — which Tailwind fills with the union of everything the shared config scans, Wallet included — is in `index-*.css` on every route. 194 of those 293 classes are not used by the homepage at all.
- **Proven by isolation, not inference.** The 291 plain-class utility rules Wallet uses were deleted from the **shipped** `index-*.css` (93 275 → 80 225 bytes) with nothing else touched and no rebuild. A cold `/wallet` on that build collapsed exactly like the pre-PR-#17 homepage: `.flex` → `block`, `.gap-3` → `normal`, `.rounded-wlg` → `0px`, `.bg-panel` → transparent, `.text-ink-3` → inherited, holdings grid → `none`. A cold `/register` on the same stripped build was identical to normal on every probe.
- **Cold-route browser QA at 1440 and 390**, fresh context per test, visiting only the route under test — never `/`, Settings, Crypto Card, Trade or Futures. Both routes render fully today: `/register` requests one stylesheet and shows the complete two-column shell; `/wallet` lays out correctly (grid `962px 390px` at 1440, single `358px` column at 390) with unknown data honestly rendered as `—`. **0 page errors, 0 console errors, no horizontal overflow on either, at either width.**
- **Cost measured before proposing anything.** Union utilities layer = **+43.94 kB raw / +8.37 kB gzip** (PR #17's delta). By page, plain-class rules only: union 706 rules / 36.34 kB, home 376 / 19.36 kB, wallet 291 / 13.05 kB, settings 275 / 12.78 kB. Adding a bare `@tailwind utilities;` to `wallet.css` would take `WalletPage-*.css` from 2.50 to ~46.4 kB raw — the whole union, 459 of whose 752 classes Wallet never uses, all of it duplicating the eager stylesheet. A scoped `@config` (the mechanism `crypto-card.css` already uses, which is why `CardPage-*.css` carries none of the union) would land ~16–18 kB raw with no dead weight. **Neither implemented**: no current user-facing failure, and doing option 2 properly means narrowing the shared config and re-verifying Home/Settings/Register — the restructuring this task ruled out.
- **What ships instead**: `frontend/src/lib/__tests__/registerWalletTailwindOwnership.test.ts`, 11 tests, so the coupling fails loudly instead of silently. **Proven load-bearing** by mutation: adding `mt-4 flex gap-2` to `RegisterPanel.tsx` fails the "register uses no utility" test naming all three classes; converting HomePage to `lazy(…)` in `App.tsx` fails the "HomePage must stay eager" test; pointing the build assertion at the stripped `dist` fails it listing the 262 missing classes. The build assertion checks the eager stylesheet **∪** `WalletPage-*.css`, so it keeps passing if Wallet is later given its own layer and fails the moment neither supplies them.
- **Preserved from Codex and from earlier Claude work**: `corePlugins.preflight` stays `false`; route code splitting untouched (`App.tsx` unmodified); Settings and Crypto Card keep their own directives; `tailwind.config.js` unmodified and its `cryptoCardProductionPromotion` fingerprint intact; no preservation fingerprint re-taken anywhere.
- **Separate finding, reported not fixed — `shadow-panel` renders a WHITE shadow.** Tailwind builds `shadow-*` from both `theme.boxShadow` and `theme.colors`, and `panel` is in both (`boxShadow.panel` = `0 1px 2px 0 rgba(16,24,40,0.04)`, `colors.panel.DEFAULT` = `#ffffff`). `.shadow-panel` is emitted twice and the shadow-COLOUR rule is emitted second, so live on `/wallet` all four panels compute `box-shadow: rgb(255,255,255) 0px 1px 2px 0px` — invisible on a white panel over the `#f6f7f9` workspace. The specified panel edge has never rendered. `panel` is the only key present in both scales. Minimal fix: rename the box-shadow key to `wpanel` (matching the `w`/`wsm`/`wlg` discipline the neighbouring `borderRadius` block already documents) and update five call sites in `wallet-v3/`. **Not applied** — it edits the fingerprinted `tailwind.config.js` and changes what Wallet looks like, so it needs an explicit yes.
- **Validation**: backend `tsc` PASS; frontend `tsc` PASS; production build PASS and **byte-identical** to the build audited (`index-B45Crrs2.css`, same sha256), so every figure above is reproducible. Jest **2 241 passed / 2 255** (+11). The 14 failures are identical to `42a1546` — verified by running the full suite with and without the new file and diffing the failing-test names: none added, none hidden.
- Unresolved / next: (1) the `shadow-panel` collision above, awaiting a decision; (2) if Wallet is ever to stand on its own, the scoped-`@config` route with the shared `content` narrowed to the homepage — a build change, not a hotfix; (3) the 14 pre-existing preservation failures on `main` are untouched and still red.

## 2026-09-10 — Claude — Real Futures TP/SL (DB → mark-price trigger engine → reduce-only execution → API → UI)

- Agent: Claude. Branch `feat/futures-real-tpsl` from `origin/main` `9adea27b246094ba59d487da1d3939541dc78e6d` (fetched, and verified through the GitHub API as the PR #18 merge). Not merged, not deployed.
- **Architecture: a dedicated model, not extra columns on `FuturesOrder` (option B).** `FuturesBookTransaction.load()` rebuilds the executable futures book from every `FuturesOrder` row in `OPEN | PARTIALLY_FILLED` and **throws** on anything that is not a priced LIMIT, so an armed trigger stored there would either corrupt the book or force a filter into the hottest safety-critical loader in the stack. `FuturesPositionProtection` cannot reach the order book at all — **an untriggered TP/SL is not an order and never enters matching.** Migration `20260910120000_futures_position_protection`, purely additive: one new table, one unique index `(positionId, kind)`, two lookup indexes, two FKs. No existing table altered, no row created/updated/deleted.
- **No quantity is stored, structurally.** The row has no size column, so there is nothing a stale size could live in. The size is read from the position **after** the claim, immediately before the close. Opened 2 → manually closed 1.25 → the stop closes exactly 0.75 (tested).
- **Trigger state machine**: `PENDING → TRIGGERING → EXECUTED`, with `→ FAILED` (execution threw, position still open — re-claimed on a later sweep, protection is never silently dropped), `→ CANCELLED` (position gone, or the OCO sibling won), and `TRIGGERING → PENDING` both for an orphaned claim and for the case where a concurrent increase left a remainder (re-arm rather than report a position as protected-and-closed when it is neither).
- **Idempotency is a conditional write, not a boolean.** The claim is `updateMany({ where: { id, status: { in: ['PENDING','FAILED'] } }, data: { status: 'TRIGGERING', … } })` — one `UPDATE … WHERE id = ? AND status IN (…)`; PostgreSQL row-locks and re-evaluates the predicate against the newest committed version, so exactly one caller sees `count === 1`, across ticks **and** across processes.
- **Mark price is the only authority.** `MarkPriceService.getMarkPrice` (index + smoothed basis) — the same figure liquidation and unrealized PnL already use. `null` means unknown: nothing triggers, nothing is cancelled, **zero is never substituted**. LONG TP `mark >= level` / SL `mark <= level`; SHORT mirrored; inclusive at the level.
- **Execution reuses the trusted path and nothing else.** One `placeOrder(… type: 'MARKET', reduceOnly: true)` call site, asserted by test to be the only one. A reduce-only order can only shrink an existing opposing position: never increases exposure, never reverses, never opens. No second accounting engine — the file contains no `futuresBalance`, no `insuranceFund`, no `realizedPnl`. The dependency is one-way: `FuturesPositionService.ts` contains no reference to protection at all, asserted by test.
- **OCO**: the winner is recorded and the sibling CANCELLED in one transaction. That is the intended path but not the safety argument — even a sibling that somehow fired would be a reduce-only order against a closed position, which the engine cancels without settling. Belt and braces, both tested.
- **ONE change outside the new code, and it was required.** `LiquidationEngine.liquidatePosition` claimed its position with a snapshot read plus a JS check. That transaction runs at READ COMMITTED and holds none of the `futures-book` advisory lock that serializes order placement, so a reduce-only close committing in between left the sweep settling a position somebody else had already settled — **PnL realized twice, the same margin released twice**. Pre-existing (the manual Close button could already hit it), but a stop loss makes it routine, because a stop and a liquidation are triggered by the same mark price moving the same way moments apart. The guard is now the same conditional write, and the row is re-read **after** the claim so a concurrent partial reduce is not settled at a stale size. **The liquidation trigger condition, the maintenance-margin and liquidation-price formulas, the total forfeiture of locked margin and the insurance-fund settlement are byte-for-byte unchanged.** Proven load-bearing: restoring the old guard fails 2 of the new race tests and passes all 6 original ones.
- **API**: `GET`/`PUT`/`DELETE /futures/positions/:positionId/protection`. PUT replaces the whole protection, so `null`/omitted removes that side. Ownership is checked on the POSITION and another user's position answers **404 identical to a position that does not exist**, so the status code cannot probe for other accounts. Additionally `GET /futures/positions` now carries `protection` per row — **so the UI needs no new endpoint and no new timer**; one indexed lookup for the whole list.
- **UI**: a `TP/SL` cell on each open-position row opening a compact editor (take profit, stop loss, save, remove, cancel). **It shows only server state** — an unsaved draft never changes the chips, and a failed save keeps the editor open with the reason while the chips stay on what the server last said. `null` protection renders `—` (unknown) rather than "not set". Refresh goes through `refreshFuturesAccount(['positions'])`; the component owns no timer and no fetch loop. 14 new keys in all seven locales, translated.
- **A real mobile defect found and fixed in QA**: the editor was absolutely positioned inside the positions table's `overflow: auto` box, so at 390 px it was clipped and ran past the fold — part of it unreachable. It is now `position: fixed`, flipped above the trigger when there is no room below, clamped to a 12 px gutter, and **re-anchored** on scroll/resize rather than closed (closing on scroll made it unopenable on a phone, because momentum scrolling keeps firing after the tap). Measured after the fix: fully inside the viewport with every field and the Save button reachable at both 1440 and 390.
- **Entry-order (bracket) TP/SL is DEFERRED**, as instructed. This ships protection for an EXISTING OPEN position only. Persistent bracket semantics need a resting trigger to survive a partial entry fill and to be cancelled with its parent, which is a second lifecycle on top of this one; nothing was faked in the meantime and no control implies it exists.
- **Tests, +84**: `FuturesProtectionService.test.ts` (38) — LONG/SHORT × TP/SL thresholds, TP-only, SL-only, TP+SL, both-crossed, OCO both ways, two concurrent sweeps placing ONE order, a second sweep doing nothing, manual close before and *during*, liquidation race, retryable failure staying armed, partial position, mid-flight increase, unavailable mark price (and that zero is not substituted), restart persistence, orphaned-claim reclaim, a live claim not stolen, validation, ownership, and the futures-only separation. `futuresProtection.test.ts` (17) — the HTTP contract through the real router and real auth. `LiquidationEngine.test.ts` +5 races. `futuresPositionProtection.test.ts` (21) — the real component. The fakes model the two properties the design leans on: `updateMany` is a conditional write with no gap between check and write, and every other call yields a microtask so concurrent sweeps genuinely interleave.
- **Three fingerprints re-taken, each with the reason written in place and the claim verified by diff, none weakened**: `frontend/src/lib/api.ts` (**+46/−0**, not one line removed or edited), `src/api/routes/futures.ts` (**+74/−1**, the single deletion being `marketRegistry: FuturesMarketRegistry` gaining a trailing comma before a sixth parameter), and the seven i18n dictionary digests (**+14/−0 each**, `git diff` over the locales directory contains no deletion at all). Every read those suites exist to protect — mark price, index price, open interest, funding, the order book, `request()`, the auth header, every spot method — is untouched. The i18n suite was **strengthened** at the same time: the 14 keys are now asserted by name in all seven languages, and each is asserted to differ from the Russian, so those digests cannot be advanced again for some unrelated change.
- **Confirmations.** Financial formulas unchanged: `marginMath.ts`, `exposureRisk.ts`, `FuturesMarketExecution.ts`, `FuturesPositionService.ts`, `futuresConfig`'s tiers/MMR/`maintenanceAmount`, `FundingRateService`, `InsuranceFundService` — none modified (`futuresConfig.ts` gained only two new interval constants, +16/−0). Spot unchanged: no file under `src/services/OrderService.ts`, spot routes or the spot conditional stack was touched, and the browser run recorded **zero** requests to `/api/v1/orders*`, zero `PENDING_TRIGGER`, zero `/orders/:id/trigger`. PriceChart unchanged: not modified, and PR #15's separation is re-asserted by test. Route splitting, `corePlugins.preflight: false` and the eager stylesheet are untouched — the built `index-B45Crrs2.css` is byte-identical to main's.
- **Validation**: backend `tsc` PASS; frontend `tsc` PASS; `prisma validate` PASS; production Vite build PASS. Jest **2 325 passed / 2 339** (+84). The 14 failures are identical to `9adea27` — verified by running the full suite before and after and diffing the failing-test names: **none added, none hidden.**
- **Browser QA at 1440 and 390, identical at both**: create TP only → chips `TP 110000`; reopen seeded from the server; add SL → `TP 110000, SL 99000`; edit → `TP 115000`; an invalid level → the server's reason shown, editor stays open, chips unchanged; an abandoned draft → chips unchanged, **and still unchanged after a full page reload**, which is the proof the row shows server state; remove one → `SL 99000`; remove all → back to the `TP/SL` label. No horizontal overflow, **0 page errors, 0 console errors**, and zero spot-conditional or spot-order requests in the whole run. `scripts/qa-futures-account-harness.cjs` gained the protection surface (loopback-only fixture, same server validation rules).
- Unresolved / next: (1) entry-order bracket TP/SL, deferred above; (2) the liquidation sweep still holds no `futures-book` advisory lock — the atomic claim makes that safe, but serializing the two would be the stronger design if liquidation ever grows beyond a status flip; (3) the 14 pre-existing failures on `main` are untouched and still red.

## 2026-09-10 — Claude — Futures TP/SL review follow-up: four safety fixes on PR #19

- Agent: Claude. Same branch `feat/futures-real-tpsl`, on top of `04f9cefba9a14b326385c49c9eb67cbedbf38ac4` (verified as PR #19's open head; main still `9adea27`). No new PR, not merged, not deployed. The dedicated-model architecture was approved and is unchanged — these are the four reported correctness findings and nothing else.
- **1. A stale scan can no longer fire an edited trigger.** The claim was `id + status`, so a sweep that decided against "SL 95000" could claim the row the trader had since edited to 90000 and close at a level that never crossed. The claim is now a **compare-and-swap** on `id + status + revision + triggerPrice`. `revision` is a new column, bumped by every mutation; `triggerPrice` rides along so a write that somehow failed to bump revision still cannot resurrect a stale decision. Tests A/B/C inject the edit inside `getMarkPrice`, which the sweep calls **after** capturing the armed rows and **before** claiming any — exactly the window. C is the sharp one: the replacement level (94500) has ALSO crossed at mark 94000, so a price-only check would wave it through; the CAS token is what makes it decidable, and the next sweep — which did evaluate the replacement — fires it correctly.
- **2. PUT/DELETE can no longer lie over a live claim.** `refuseWhileTriggering` throws `Conflict` when either leg is TRIGGERING, so an edit or a cancel during execution is refused rather than reported as done. Ownership **and** OPEN are re-checked **inside** the mutation transaction, under the lock, not only before it. Clearing deliberately does NOT require OPEN — tidying stale protection off a closed position arms nothing and must stay possible.
- **My own test caught a hole in this fix.** With the mutation under the futures-book lock but the CLAIM outside it, a mutation could read "nothing is TRIGGERING", the sweep could claim, and the mutation could then overwrite a trigger whose close was already on its way. The claim now runs inside its own short transaction that takes the same lock — and **releases it before `placeOrder`**, which opens its own transaction on another pooled connection and would otherwise deadlock against us. Asserted on the code's own ordering.
- **3. Liquidation joins the futures-book lock.** `LiquidationEngine.liquidatePosition` now takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended('futures-book', 0))` — the **same literal key**, asserted by test against `FuturesBookTransaction`'s and against the number PostgreSQL derives — as the first statement in its transaction, before the claim. The conditional `OPEN -> LIQUIDATED` claim is **kept** as defence in depth. Ordering is now deterministic in both directions rather than a question of snapshot interleaving.
- **4. Arming requires an authoritative mark price.** A PUT carrying any non-null leg with no mark price is refused with `MarkPriceUnavailable` and writes nothing. DELETE never reads a mark price at all (asserted: the service is not even called), and a PUT that only clears is not blocked — refusing to let a trader REMOVE a stop because the feed is down would be the wrong failure direction. Zero is still never substituted.
- **HTTP contract**: `404 NOT_FOUND` (unchanged, still identical for another user's position and one that does not exist), **`409 PROTECTION_TRIGGERING`**, **`503 MARK_PRICE_UNAVAILABLE`**, `400 INVALID_PROTECTION`. All four codes asserted distinct so a client can branch on them. The UI branches on the **code**, never on message text, and both new states get their own translated sentence in all seven locales.
- **NATIVE PostgreSQL tests, as asked.** `src/futures/__tests__/futuresBookLock.pg.test.ts`, 12 tests against a real PostgreSQL 16.13: the lock is transaction-scoped and a second taker genuinely **blocks** (measured — the waiting test takes ~260 ms because it really waits), a ROLLBACK releases it, the key is byte-identical across all three call sites, G/H/I close-vs-liquidation in both orders and started together, two concurrent liquidations, a partial reduce settled at its real size, the CAS claim against a concurrent edit, `DECIMAL(36,18)` comparing by VALUE (the stored form is `95000.000000000000000000`, the claim passes `95000`, and it matches), 8 concurrent claims of which exactly one wins, and the unique `(positionId, kind)` pair enforced by the database. The full migration chain was also applied to a fresh database and the resulting table inspected.
- **A false-green I caught and fixed in my own test.** The first version returned early inside each test when no database was reachable, so the suite reported **12 passed while verifying nothing** — and it did exactly that once, after the local server was killed mid-session. It is now `describe.skip` when `VOLTEX_PG_TEST_URL` is unset (Jest reports 12 SKIPPED, never passed) and **fails loudly** when the variable is set but the database is unusable. It reads only `VOLTEX_PG_TEST_URL`, never `DATABASE_URL`, because it writes rows.
- **Financial formulas unchanged.** `marginMath.ts`, `exposureRisk.ts`, `FuturesMarketExecution.ts`, `FuturesPositionService.ts`, `FundingRateService`, `InsuranceFundService`, the tier table / MMR / `maintenanceAmount` — none modified in this follow-up. In `LiquidationEngine` only the lock line was added; the trigger condition, liquidation-price and maintenance-margin formulas, margin forfeiture and insurance-fund settlement are untouched.
- **Spot unchanged**: nothing under `OrderService`, spot routes or the spot conditional stack touched; the browser run recorded **zero** `/api/v1/orders*` requests. **PriceChart unchanged.** Scope held: no bracket TP/SL, no chart lines, no drag, no fee economics, no Wallet/Tailwind work.
- **Three fingerprints advanced again, each verified by diff first**: `api.ts` **+49/−0** (still no deletion; the addition is the `revision` CAS token on the trigger type), `src/api/routes/futures.ts` **+96/−2** (the second deletion is `import { Router }` gaining `Response` for the shared error mapper), and the seven i18n digests **+16/−0 each** with no deletion anywhere in the locales directory. The i18n key assertion grew from 14 to 16 named keys, so the digests still cannot be advanced for an unrelated change.
- **Validation**: backend `tsc` PASS; frontend `tsc` PASS; `prisma validate` PASS; production Vite build PASS. Jest **2 356 passed / 2 382 with 12 skipped** without a database, and **2 368 / 2 382 with 0 skipped** against PostgreSQL. The 14 failures are identical to `9adea27` in both runs — **none added, none hidden**.
- **Browser QA at 1440 and 390, identical**: the full create/edit/remove lifecycle still green (including the abandoned draft surviving a page reload); a 409 during execution shows the translated "a trigger is executing" line with the editor open and **the chips unmoved**, for an edit *and* for a remove — no false cancellation; a 503 shows the translated mark-price line with chips unmoved; and **removing protection still succeeds with the feed down**, clearing the chips. No overflow, **0 page errors, 0 console errors**, zero spot requests. The QA harness gained the same 409/503 rules behind `/__qa/protection-state`.
- Unresolved / next: unchanged from the previous entry — entry-order bracket TP/SL is still deferred, and the 14 pre-existing failures on `main` are still red.

## 2026-09-10 — Claude — Shared authenticated header collapsed on /trade and /futures (CSS ownership)

- Agent: Claude. Branch `fix/shared-header-desktop-layout` from `origin/main` `c7c2200d324963194d3061b3aa39608ed9c57299` (fetched and verified as the PR #19 merge). New PR, not merged, not deployed. Nothing from PR #19 is touched here.
- **Root cause, proven in the browser, not inferred from source.** The winning rule for `.global-header`'s padding and `.nav-item`'s padding/margin was `.trade-terminal *, .trade-terminal *::before, .trade-terminal *::after { margin: 0; padding: 0; box-sizing: border-box; }` from the lazy `TradeTerminal-*.css`. The shared `<header class="global-header top-nav-bar">` renders **inside** `div.trade-terminal` on /futures (`.futures-terminal`) and /trade (`.spot-terminal`, `.cfd-terminal`) but not on /markets (`div.markets-bolt-root`) or /wallet, so the reset reached it there and only there. `.trade-terminal *` and `.global-header` both score **0,1,0**, and the route chunk loads **after** the eager `index-*.css`, so it won every tie. `display`, `height`, `gap` and `font-size` are absent from the reset and still applied — which is exactly why the bar was the right height and colour with everything crushed into the corner.
- **Fix: one selector, in the file that owns the reset.** `.trade-terminal *:not(:where(.global-header, .global-header *))` (and the two pseudo-element rules). `:where()` contributes **zero specificity**, so the selector still scores exactly 0,1,0 and every rule inside the terminal cascades precisely as before — the reset is narrowed, not deleted or re-weighted. `box-sizing: border-box` is not lost for the header: `index.css` sets it globally on `*`. No `!important`, no `transform: scale`, no negative margins, no duplicated or route-specific header, no JS measurement, no hidden nav items, no change to any global reset. **26 lines changed in one CSS file, 20 of them the comment explaining why.**
- **Mandatory CSS load-order test.** Cold `/futures` versus real in-app clicks into `/futures` from `/markets`, `/trade`, `/wallet` and `/copy-trading` — four different stylesheet orders (e.g. `index, MarketsPage, TradeTerminal, TradePage, WalletPage, FuturesPage` vs `index, WalletPage, TradeTerminal, TradePage, FuturesPage`). Every source route and every post-navigation state returned identical metrics: `hdrPad 0px 28px`, `itemPad 0px 11px`, `itemMargin 8px 0px`, `firstItemX 176`, 9 visible items, 0 collisions. **The nav is correct because the eager stylesheet says so, never because a route chunk happened to load.**
- **Regression QA, 10 shared routes × cold and in-app**, at 1600 and 1440: `/markets /trade /futures /analytics /wallet /copy-trading /arbitrage /card /otc`, plus a single-context walk clicking through all of them in sequence — 20 cold loads and 10 walk steps, **zero deviations**, no horizontal overflow, correct active item on every route. `/settings` renders its own `ArcticTopNav`, not the shared Nav, on `c7c2200` as well — unchanged.
- **Preserved behaviours, measured on /futures and /trade (the affected routes) against /markets as control, all identical**: Trade dropdown (2 items, 210×167, `padding 12px 14px`, opens below the header), active-route underline (2 px, `rgb(240,196,63)`), Copy Trading prefetch (a real `/copy-trading/marketplace` request on hover, before any click), Deposit button → modal → close, language switcher, Profile dropdown (Settings link + Logout, inside the viewport), logout (token cleared, leaves the authenticated app), ticker present, admin-only Admin link (**absent** for a USER, and `padding 0px 11px; margin 8px 0px` when `/me` is patched to an admin), mobile burger + drawer (11 links) and BottomNav at 390.
- **Viewport QA on /futures** at 1600×958, 1440, 1280, 1024 and 390: header padding `0 28px → 0 16px → 0 10px` as the existing responsive rules intend, nav item 47 px tall at y=8 (vertically centred), no nav/actions overlap, no page overflow, **0 page errors and 0 console errors**. Two things look odd at narrow widths and are **pre-existing shared-header behaviour, byte-identical on /markets and /wallet which were never affected**: a 3 px `.main-nav` overhang at 1280 (`761` vs `758`), and Crypto Card + OTC being deliberately `display: none` below 1050 px.
- **Regression test added**: `frontend/src/lib/__tests__/sharedHeaderStylesheetOwnership.test.ts`, 10 tests, both directions of the invariant. (1) No page-root blanket reset outside the eager `index.css` may set a property that moves a box unless it excludes the header — this is the one that fails on the defect, naming `.trade-terminal *`, `*::before` and `*::after`. The other six page-root resets in the repo (`.vx-wallet`, `.markets-bolt-root`, `.copytrading-bolt-root`, `:where(.crypto-card-page)`, `.vx-home`, `.vx-auth`) set only `box-sizing`/border/`--tw-*` and stay green untouched. (2) The header may not **depend** on a lazy stylesheet either: every class it renders is declared in `index.css`, `main.tsx` imports it, and no header component imports a stylesheet of its own. Plus the exact `:not(:where(…))` form (so specificity cannot be raised back), the nesting fact that makes it matter, and the same two checks against the **built** chunks. **Verified by reverting the fix in both the source and the shipped chunk: 3 of the 10 fail, and pass again when restored.**
- **Two documented leftovers recorded rather than silently allowed** (the test pins the list, so it cannot grow unnoticed): `CopyTradingBolt.css` hides `.mobile-menu` — the ported archive's own burger — and re-shows the real one with `display: grid !important` in its narrow media query (verified at 390 px: the burger opens the shared drawer on /copy-trading exactly as elsewhere); and `auth-shell.css` styles `.header-icon` under `.vx-auth-work`, which is the login shell and renders no shared Nav.
- **One fingerprint re-taken, with the reason written in place and strengthened, not weakened.** `spotOrdersPresentation.test.ts` digests the shared section of `TradeTerminal.css`; that section is exactly what this fix edits. The new digest is accompanied by an assertion pinning the `:not(:where(.global-header, .global-header *))` selector itself, so the digest cannot be advanced again to cover a different edit without also deleting that line.
- **Scope held.** No backend file, no Prisma schema or migration, no futures financial logic (`FuturesPositionService`, `FuturesProtectionService`, `LiquidationEngine`, `marginMath`, `exposureRisk`, tiers/MMR, funding, insurance fund, market execution), no Spot logic, no `Nav.tsx`, no landing-page `HomeHeader`, no Crypto Card visuals or assets. The whole change is 3 files: one CSS selector, one new test file, one re-taken fingerprint.
- **Validation**: frontend `tsc` PASS; production Vite build PASS. Full Jest **2 366 passed / 2 392 with 12 skipped** (the PostgreSQL lock suite, no database configured), against **2 356 / 2 382** on `c7c2200` — the +10 are the new suite, and the **14 failures in 8 suites are identical to main**, verified by running the full suite on the stashed tree and diffing the failing names: none added, none hidden.
- Unresolved / next: (1) the 14 pre-existing failures on `main` are still red and untouched; (2) the 3 px nav overhang at 1280 and the Crypto Card/OTC hiding below 1050 px are shared-header behaviour that predates this fix — worth a separate look if the owner wants the nav to fit without clipping at 1280; (3) `.copytrading-bolt-root .mobile-menu { display: none }` neutralised by an `!important` re-show is fragile and could be pruned from the archive CSS in its own change.

The two earlier regressions (homepage Tailwind, TradeTerminal resetting the Nav) have no surviving siblings.

A `display: grid !important` put it back, but only inside the archive's `max-width: 760px` block — leaving a 100px band where `.main-nav` was hidden by the media query AND the burger was hidden by the route chunk. Proven from the live cascade: at 800px the burger computed `none` on /copy-trading and `grid` on /markets, same viewport, same shared component; at 740px it computed `grid` again. What was unreachable there: the drawer's Deposit, CFD, Futures, Analytics, Copy Trading, Arbitrage, Crypto Card, OTC, language switcher and **Logout** — BottomNav offers only Wallet, Markets, Trade, Profile.

Both archive rules are gone, so `index.css` owns the shared burger on this route exactly as it does everywhere else — and the `!important` that existed only to undo the hide went with them. **Net −2 rules, one `!important` removed, none added.** The comment was corrected in place, including a stale claim that this stylesheet is eager (App.tsx lazy-imports CopyTradingPage; the CSS ships in its own chunk).
- **Proven surgical.** Same fixtures, pre-fix build vs post-fix build, full computed-style digest: `/copy-trading` @800 and @768 differ in exactly **3 nodes** — the burger (`display: none → grid`), its svg, and `.header-left` widening 111px → 153px to hold it. `/copy-trading` at 1600, 1024 and 390, and `/markets`, `/futures`, `/wallet` at all five widths: **0 differences**. The drawer opens at 800 and 768 with all 11 links plus Deposit and Logout, inside the viewport, 0 page errors.

That exception is deleted — the allowed list shrinks from 3 entries to 2 — and a new assertion is added: nothing outside the eager stylesheet may set `display` on `.mobile-menu`, `.nav-burger`, `.main-nav` or `.nav-mobile-menu`, **including with `!important`**, which is what made the gap between the two media queries invisible in review.
