## 2026-09-10 — Claude — One shared read of /futures/config (request dedup)

- Agent: Claude. Branch `perf/futures-config-dedup` from `origin/main` `f7f2d187cfeba01501cb9cfb70d4284c9a672693` (fetched and verified as the PR #21 merge). Not merged, not deployed. Frontend request-dedup and state ownership only.
- **Root cause of the three requests.** `/futures/config` is static, and three components each fetched it from their own mount effect with no cache between them: `FuturesPage.tsx:72` (the listed contracts), `FuturesOrderForm.tsx:71` (leverage bounds and the tier table) and `FuturesTickerBar.tsx:62` (the funding interval). Measured on the shipped build against `scripts/qa-perf-harness.cjs`: **3 requests on a cold /futures with a 0–1 ms spread**, and **8 more across two /markets↔/futures round trips**. Two further call sites did the same once each on `/markets` (`markets-bolt/components.tsx:145`) and the homepage (`home/useHomeMarket.ts:139`).
- **Design: a tiny dedicated shared store, option 2 of the three offered.** Option 1 was rejected on inspection: `futuresAccountStore` is for AUTHENTICATED, POLLED, SESSION-SCOPED resources — one timer per resource, a session generation stamped on every request, state dropped on logout. `/futures/config` is none of those things, and putting it there would have given a static public value a timer and a session lifetime it does not want. New file `frontend/src/lib/futuresConfigStore.ts` (~190 lines, most of it the note explaining the above): one in-flight promise every caller joins, the resolved value reused with no network, `useFuturesConfig()` for the four component consumers and `futuresConfigStore.load()` for the homepage, which needs the value inside a larger effect and keeps its own `futuresStatus` error state.
- **No session isolation, and here is why.** `/futures/config` is the ONE futures route with no auth middleware (`src/api/routes/futures.ts`: every other route there carries `requireAuthOrApiKey`, this one carries nothing). Its body is `marketRegistry.list()` plus module constants; not one field is derived from the caller, so two users and a logged-out visitor get byte-identical responses. Verified in the browser: across a logout and a re-login as the other fixture identity **inside one document**, every response the fixture ever returned was byte-identical (`distinct payloads=1`), the page rendered correctly, and no further request was needed. There is no user state to drop.
- **No timer, no polling loop.** Nothing in the store schedules anything — asserted by a test that greps the module for `setInterval`/`setTimeout`/`requestAnimationFrame`. The only re-read path is `STALE_AFTER_MS` (5 min), evaluated lazily when a consumer mounts, so the contract listing — the one field derived from live market data — cannot stay pinned for the life of a long-lived SPA session. Measured: **1 request while sitting on /futures for 40 s** (before: 3).
- **Failure stays failure.** A failed read never produces a default: no built-in tier table, no leverage bounds, no funding interval. `config` stays `null` (unknown) or keeps the last good answer flagged `failed`. `load()` REJECTS, for every joined caller and not just the first, so the homepage's error state still fires. Retry is explicit and mount-driven, never timed. Verified against a forced 500: the DOM is **identical to before** — the funding countdown renders `—`, the tier table is absent, and the pair list falls back to the same documented `CORE_SYMBOLS` first-paint placeholder it always did.
- **Same values, same consumers — proven with a distinctive payload, not the fixture's.** `/futures/config` was answered with `symbols ['AAA','BBB','CCC']`, `minLeverage 2`, `maxLeverage 37`, `fundingIntervalHours 3`, `highLeverageWarningThreshold 11` and three unmistakable tiers, then every consumer was read back off the DOM on BOTH builds: pair list `AAA/BBB/CCC` with `AAA/USDT` selected (FuturesPage), tier rows `12,345 | 37x | 1.23%` / `67,890 | 19x | 4.56%` / `∞ | 3x | 7.89%` (FuturesOrderForm), and a countdown inside a **3-hour** boundary rather than the fixture's 8 (FuturesTickerBar). **Byte-identical before and after**, at 3 requests and at 1.
- **Measured result** (cold /futures, shipped build, 1440×900 and 390×844, both identical): **3 → 1** on cold load; **8 → 0** across two /markets↔/futures round trips; **3 → 1** while idle for 40 s; **3 → 1** on the failure path. A hard reload still re-reads (module state dies with the document), so there is no stale config after reload. 0 page errors, 0 console errors in every scenario.
- **Tests.** New `futuresConfigDedup.test.ts`, 13 tests: three concurrent `ensure()` calls produce ONE fetch; a mid-flight caller joins rather than races; a later caller is served from memory; every field arrives named and unchanged; object identity survives a re-read of an unchanged payload but a genuinely changed one is adopted; a failure never substitutes a default, rejects for every joined caller, retries only on the next mount, and keeps the last good answer flagged stale. Plus the assertion that actually protects this a year from now: **`api.getFuturesConfig(` has exactly one caller in the whole frontend** — `src/lib/futuresConfigStore.ts` — and no file reaches the URL by hand. Verified by reverting the consumers: that assertion fails and names all five old call sites.
- **Four existing suites needed their harness wired, and none was weakened.** `futuresOrderPanel`, `futuresAccountUnknownState`, `futuresFinalPolish` and `futuresTickerHeader` mount the real TSX through the repo's hook-stub harness, which maps each dependency module by name. Each now maps `../lib/futuresConfigStore` to a stub that resolves **the same `api.getFuturesConfig` stub the test already defines**, on the same tick the old mount effect settled on — so every assertion runs against exactly the config it always did, from exactly the render it always did. No test's config value was edited. All 40 + 29 + 24 + 20 of their behavioural assertions pass unmodified.
- **Three fingerprints re-taken, each with the reason written in place, and two of them strengthened.** `futuresTickerHeader`'s reads-block digest now also asserts, in code, that the funding interval comes from `useFuturesConfig()`, that the block contains no `getFuturesConfig` of its own, and that `getFuturesMarkPrice` and `getFuturesFundingRate` are still there — so the digest cannot be advanced again for an unrelated edit. `futuresUiPolish`'s semantic digests for `FuturesOrderForm.tsx` and `FuturesPage.tsx` were re-taken with a note naming the one difference in each. `marketUniverseScale`'s "symbols come from the backend listing, never from a local list" was rewritten rather than deleted: it now pins the shared-store read, pins `setSymbols(listed)`, asserts the page no longer calls `getFuturesConfig` itself, and asserts the ONLY local symbol array left in the file is the documented first-paint placeholder.
- **Nothing financial moved.** No backend file, no response shape, no `futuresConfig` value, no leverage tier, no MMR, no margin, liquidation, exposure, order-validation, TP/SL, funding, market-execution, Spot or Copy Trading code. The five changed frontend files move the same bytes from the same endpoint to the same consumers.
- **Validation**: frontend `tsc` PASS; production Vite build PASS. Full Jest **2 380 passed / 2 406 with 12 skipped**, against **2 367 / 2 393** on `f7f2d18` — the +13 are the new suite, and the **14 failures in 8 suites are identical to main**, verified by running the full suite on the stashed tree and diffing the failing names: none added, none hidden.
- Unresolved / next: the 14 pre-existing failures on `main` are still red and untouched; and the other two findings from the post-route-splitting audit (the shared header inheriting typography/colour from its page wrapper, and the missing fallback message when the TradingView widget script cannot be fetched) are still open.

## 2026-09-10 — Claude — PR #22 review follow-up: load() now respects the freshness window

- Agent: Claude. Same branch `perf/futures-config-dedup`, on top of the reviewed head `ac50dd7705c0b866fbd48e5293a32663b79ccd75`. No new PR, not merged, not deployed. One reported cache-semantics hole, nothing else.
- **The hole, as reported and confirmed.** `ensure()` tested freshness; `load()` did not — it joined an in-flight request and otherwise went straight to the network. So a second imperative read inside the five-minute window issued a request for an answer the tab already held, contradicting the store's own contract ("the resolved value is reused by every later consumer, with no network at all"). The original suite missed it because its "later consumer" test called `ensure()` after the first load, never `load()` twice.
- **Fix: one private `fetch()`, three public callers.** `fetch()` is now the only place the request is issued and owns the in-flight join. `ensure()` returns early when `isFresh()`. `load()` joins an in-flight request, otherwise returns `Promise.resolve(cachedConfig)` — the exact object, not a copy — when fresh, and only otherwise calls `fetch()`. `refresh()` calls `fetch()` directly, so it is still DEFINED to hit the network and cannot be turned into a no-op by the cache; it still joins an in-flight request rather than duplicating one. The old `refresh()` trick of zeroing `fetchedAt` and delegating to `load()` is gone — with a cache-aware `load()` that would have been a sentinel holding the seam together. No timer, no polling, unchanged.
- **Failure semantics unchanged and now asserted directly.** A first failure leaves `config` null, so nothing is ever "fresh" and every asker retries. A failed *refresh* leaves the last good value servable — `fetchedAt` only moves on success, so the window it was fetched under is intact — with `failed` still set for a view that wants to mark it stale; once that window really passes, the next read goes to the network again.
- **THE SEQUENCE IN THE REVIEW CANNOT ACTUALLY HAPPEN, and it is worth writing down.** `/` renders HomePage only for a logged-out visitor: `App.tsx:115` is `getToken() ? <Navigate to={defaultTradingPath()} replace /> : <HomePage />`. With a token, navigating to `/` redirects to the trading path and `useHomeMarket` never mounts, so the imperative read is never issued. Measured on both builds: **cold /futures → home → /markets → /futures is 1 request on the reviewed head as well as on the fix**, at 1440 and 390. The mechanism the review described is real; that particular route sequence is not the one that reaches it.
- **Where it IS reachable, and the measured before/after.** The public side: land on `/`, go elsewhere, come back inside the window — HomePage remounts, `useHomeMarket` runs again, `load()` is called again. Logged out, `/` → `/login` → `/` → `/legal/terms` → `/`, then once more past the window:
  - reviewed head `ac50dd7`: **4** requests (cold 1, each in-window return **+1**, past-window +1)
  - with the fix: **2** (cold 1, both in-window returns **+0**, past-window +1)
  - identical at 1440×900 and 390×844, homepage and its Futures tab rendering correctly, 0 page errors, 0 console errors in every run.
- **Authenticated browser QA, unchanged and re-measured on the new build** (1440 and 390, identical): cold `/futures` → home → `/markets` → `/futures` = **1 total**; homepage cold = 1 and → `/futures` within the window still **1 total**; a hard reload is a new document and reads again (**+1**), so there is no stale config after one; and with the page clock wound past the window (`Date.now` overridden before any app script runs, so no test waits five minutes) a navigation back to `/futures` reads again (**+1**) — the same path `refresh()` forces, exercised through the real store in a real browser.
- **Tests, +10 (13 → 23).** A: two awaited `load()`s inside the window make exactly ONE request. B: `load()` after a hook-loaded value returns the cache with no request. C: the real route sequence — three `ensure()` mounts, then an imperative read a minute later, then two more navigations — costs one request. D: `refresh()` re-reads a perfectly fresh value. D2: `refresh()` joins an in-flight request instead of duplicating it. E: at exactly `STALE_AFTER_MS` the value is still fresh, one millisecond later it is not. F: a cached load resolves with the very same object AND makes no second call. Plus: a cache hit emits nothing, so no consumer re-renders for it; G: a failed read never poisons the cache and `load()` keeps retrying; G2: a failed refresh leaves the last good value servable with the window intact, and reads again once it passes. The clock is hand-driven via `Date.now`, so nothing waits.
- **A flaw in my own first draft of these tests, caught and fixed.** B and C originally awaited two `Promise.resolve()`s after `ensure()`. That is not enough for the request's `.then().catch().finally()` chain to clear `inFlight`, so the later `load()` joined the still-pending request and looked cached whether or not it respected freshness — both tests PASSED against the unfixed store. They now drain the microtask queue with `setImmediate` before the second read. Verified against the reviewed head's store: **7 of the 23 fail** (A, B, C, E, F, the emit check and G2) and all 23 pass with the fix.
- **Validation**: frontend `tsc` PASS; production Vite build PASS. Full Jest **2 390 passed / 2 416 with 12 skipped**, against **2 367 / 2 393** on `f7f2d18` — the +23 are this suite, and the **14 failures in 8 suites are identical to main**: none added, none hidden.
- Unresolved / next: unchanged — the 14 pre-existing failures on `main`, and the two open findings from the post-route-splitting audit (the shared header inheriting typography/colour from its page wrapper; no fallback message when the TradingView widget script cannot be fetched).

## 2026-09-10 — Claude — CFD chart: explicit TradingView failure fallback and a retry that can actually retry

- Agent: Claude. Branch `fix/cfd-tradingview-fallback` from `origin/main` `47ba6f5c932c5ebaba8ed46eb3f8078cbf63c59b` (fetched and verified as the PR #22 merge). New PR, not merged, not deployed. Error handling only — no chart redesign, no second data path.
- **Root cause, two parts, and the second is what made it unrecoverable.** (1) `loadTradingViewScript().then(...)` had no `.catch()`, so a CDN failure escaped as an UNCAUGHT page error and no failure UI existed — the chart area simply sat empty. (2) The module-level `tvScriptPromise` retained the REJECTED promise, so every later attempt (a retry, a symbol change, a remount) re-read that same old failure and issued no request at all. A Retry button on top of that would have been a lie: the chart could not come back without a full page reload.
- **Fix.** `onerror` now clears `tvScriptPromise` **before** delivering the rejection and removes the dead `<script>` node, so the next caller starts exactly one new request; a load already under way is still joined, so concurrent retries cannot race two tags; an existing `window.TradingView` is reused with no tag inserted at all. The component gained an explicit `loading | ready | error` status and an `attempt` counter that is an effect dependency, so Retry re-runs the same load path rather than needing a second one. The `.catch()` covers both a failed script and a throwing widget constructor.
- **The happy path is byte-identical, and that is measured, not asserted.** `TV_SYMBOL_BY_CFD`, `TV_LOCALE`, `interval: '60'`, `autosize`, `theme`, `style`, `timezone`, `toolbar_bg`, `hide_side_toolbar`, `allow_symbol_change` and `withdateranges` are untouched. Browser-measured with TradingView served: canvas `{x:281,y:200,w:848,h:496}` and chart area `{x:281,y:200,w:848,h:528}` at 1440 — **the same boxes as main** — one script tag, one request, widget constructed with the exact config, disclaimer present, 0 page and 0 console errors. **No loading state is painted at all**, deliberately: covering the container first would change what a working chart looks like.
- **Deliberately no alternate chart.** The failure branch renders a message. No candles are generated, no Spot or Futures candles are reused, no Twelve Data time-series poll is added, no provider, no proxy, no key. A test prints the component with comments stripped and asserts it contains no `candles|orderBook|volume|funding|openInterest|time_series|twelvedata|api.` at all.
- **Measured before/after, forced failure at 1440:**
  - main: canvas present but **empty**, dead `<script>` still in the DOM (`tvScriptTags=1`), no fallback, no retry, and an **uncaught page error** — `Failed to load TradingView widget script`.
  - with the fix: no empty canvas, fallback occupying the canvas's exact slot (`848×496`), Retry visible, dead tag removed (`tvScriptTags=0`), disclaimer present, instrument list / ticker / order panel all still usable, **0 page errors**.
- **Retry, measured:** blocked → fallback → CDN restored → click Retry → **exactly one** new `tv.js` request, widget constructed with the same exact config, fallback gone, canvas back at `{281,200,848,496}`, and a sentinel set on `window` before the click survives it — **no page reload**. Identical at 390×844 (`390×378` canvas/fallback slot). 0 page and 0 console errors in every scenario.
- **A layout reading I chased down rather than reported.** One QA pass showed the chart area at `y=433` with TradingView working and `y=415` with it blocked at 390. Re-measuring twice more gave `433/410` for BOTH states: the difference is something above the chart area not having settled at the sampling instant, not the fallback. The chart area's own size is `410` in every reading, at every viewport, in both states.
- **Tests, +16** in `cfdChartFallback.test.ts`, driving the real component against a fake document whose script tags are countable and whose `onload`/`onerror` the test fires: A successful load initialises the chart with the exact config and no fallback; B a failed script shows the fallback, keeps the disclaimer and produces **no unhandled rejection** (asserted through a `process.on('unhandledRejection')` probe); C the rejected promise is not kept — Retry issues a NEW request and the chart recovers; D three more Retry clicks while that request is in flight still make ONE request; E an existing `window.TradingView` inserts no script; F unmount during a pending load creates no widget; G a symbol change uses the right TradingView symbol and needs no second script; H five languages map to the right TradingView locale; I a throwing widget constructor shows the fallback instead of crashing. Plus: the component invents no data, the `onerror` handler clears the promise before rejecting, and all three strings exist in seven distinct translations. **Verified against main's component: B, C, D, I and the `onerror` ordering assertion fail; A, E, F, G, H pass on both, which is itself the evidence that the happy path is unchanged.**
- **Translations**: `trade.cfdChartUnavailable`, `trade.cfdChartUnavailableHint`, `trade.cfdChartRetry` in all seven locales, each written in its own language — no locale serves the Russian string. `git diff --numstat` over the locales directory reports `3  0` for every file: **no deletion anywhere**.
- **One fingerprint re-taken and one assertion updated, both documented, neither weakened.** `i18nLanguageChunks`'s seven dictionary digests were advanced with the +3/−0 accounting written in place, and a new named-key test asserts the three keys exist in seven DISTINCT translations, so the digests cannot be advanced again for something else. `cfdTerminal`'s `[symbol, lang, containerId]` dependency assertion became `[symbol, lang, containerId, attempt]` with a note that the retry counter is the only addition — its CSS-scoping assertion passed untouched, confirming the four new selectors are all `.cfd-terminal`-scoped.
- **Scope held**: no backend, no Prisma, no Spot chart, no Futures chart, no futures financial logic, no TP/SL, no futures config store, no `marginMath`, no `exposureRisk`, no `LiquidationEngine`, no `FuturesPositionService`, no Copy Trading economics, no Crypto Card assets, no homepage copy. Four files plus one new test: `CfdChart.tsx`, `CfdTerminal.css` (+5 scoped selectors), the seven locale files, and two existing test files.

## 2026-09-10 — Claude — PR #23 review follow-up: a script that loads but publishes nothing

- Agent: Claude. Same branch `fix/cfd-tradingview-fallback`, on top of the reviewed head `4f372f6a23ca48b1f383f8a552f87aa8212ff328`. No new PR, not merged, not deployed. One reported retry edge case, nothing else.
- **The hole, as reported and confirmed.** `script.onload = () => resolve()` treated a `load` event as success. But a classic external script can finish loading while its own execution throws, or while it simply never publishes `window.TradingView` — and then `tvScriptPromise` was cached PERMANENTLY RESOLVED. Retry was handed that resolved promise, found no global, and fell straight back to the error state **without ever issuing a request**. Unrecoverable without a full page reload — the same poisoning as the rejected-promise case the first round fixed, through the opposite door.
- **Fix.** The two failure paths now share one `fail()` closure so they cannot drift: forget the promise FIRST, drop the dead tag, then reject. `onload` decides on the global, not on the event: `if (window.TradingView) resolve(); else fail();`. `onerror = fail`. Nothing about which of the two happened reaches the UI — the user is told the chart is unavailable, and that is all. TradingView's configuration, the normal path and the existing `onerror` behaviour are untouched.
- **Browser-measured, both viewports, serving a tv.js that loads cleanly and defines nothing:**
  - reviewed head `4f372f6`: fallback shown, but the useless `<script>` stays in the DOM, and Retry produces **0 new insertions and 0 new requests** — `window.TradingView` still undefined, fallback still there. Stuck.
  - with the fix: dead tag removed, Retry produces **exactly one** new insertion and one new request, `window.TradingView` becomes an object, the widget paints, the fallback clears — and a `window` sentinel set before the click survives it, so no reload. **Two extra Retry clicks fired immediately after the first added nothing**: total insertions 2, not 4.
  - 0 page errors and 0 console errors in every run, on both builds.
- **Two artefacts in my own QA harness, found and fixed rather than reported as results.** The first run said Retry caused 0 new requests even WITH the fix — Chromium was reusing the cached tv.js, so the "CDN recovered" body was never delivered; `Network.setCacheDisabled` plus `cache-control: no-store` fixed it, and the run then showed the real recovery. The second was an init script that reached for `document.head` before `<head>` existed and threw — which showed up as a page error the application never caused; hooking `Node.prototype.appendChild` instead made the error count meaningful again. Both readings in the report come from the corrected harness.
- **Test J**, +1 (16 → 17): the script fires `onload` with no global → fallback, disclaimer kept, **no unhandled rejection** (asserted through a `process.on('unhandledRejection')` probe), neither promise nor tag retained; Retry issues ONE new request; three more clicks while it is in flight add nothing; the second attempt publishes the global, the widget constructs and the fallback clears; **exactly two attempts in total**, counted by a cumulative `createElement` counter rather than by the live tag list, which loses entries when a dead tag is removed. The source-pinning test now also asserts both handlers route through `fail()` and that `onload` resolves only on the global. **Verified against the reviewed head: J and that pinning test fail; the other fifteen pass on both, which is the evidence the normal and network-failure paths did not move.**
- **Validation**: frontend `tsc` PASS; production Vite build PASS. `cfdChartFallback` 17/17, and `cfdTerminal` + `i18nLanguageChunks` + `chartDrawings` 150/150 together. Full Jest **2 408 passed / 2 434 with 12 skipped**, against **2 390 / 2 416** on `47ba6f5` — the +18 are this suite plus the new i18n test, and the **14 failures in 8 suites are identical to main**: none added, none hidden.
- Unresolved / next: unchanged — the 14 pre-existing failures on `main`, and the last open audit finding (the shared header inheriting typography and colour from whatever page wrapper it renders inside).

## 2026-09-10 — Claude — futures: a TP/SL that wins its claim now takes the bucket's resting entries with it

- Agent: Claude. New branch `fix/futures-protection-cancels-bucket-entries` from `origin/main` `97a1fd350d072ee5a2ee40969cb59a214fa9a431` (the PR #23 merge). New PR, **not merged, not deployed**. Backend futures only. This fixes the "reopen after protection wins" race and nothing else — entry-order TP/SL is still unimplemented and unstarted.
- **The defect, and it is in shipped `main`, not in anything proposed.** `fire()` claimed a trigger and closed the position it was keyed to, and stopped there. A non-reduce-only order of the SAME user resting in the SAME `(userId, symbol, marginType)` bucket outlived that close. When it later filled it re-opened the exact exposure the stop had just closed — and unprotected, because the `FuturesPositionProtection` rows are keyed to a position id and were resolved along with the position they belonged to. Found while reviewing the entry-order TP/SL architecture; it needed fixing first, on its own, so the bracket work is not built on top of it.
- **The fix is an ordering guarantee, not an extra cleanup step.** The CAS claim and the cancellations now COMMIT TOGETHER, inside one `FuturesBookTransaction.run` — the same literal `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended('futures-book', 0))` the hand-rolled `lockFuturesBook` took here before, plus the staged book and the commit verification. Exact ordering inside that one transaction: take the lock → CAS claim (`id + status ∈ CLAIMABLE + revision + triggerPrice`) → `count === 0` means lost, return having changed nothing → re-read the position, `status !== 'OPEN'` means nothing to protect → select the bucket's non-reduce-only `OPEN|PARTIALLY_FILLED` orders → cancel each in the staged book and in the DB and release its reserve once → COMMIT, which publishes the staged book and releases the lock → and only THEN the existing reduce-only MARKET protection path runs, unchanged. A watcher that loses the CAS cancels nothing and refunds nothing.
- **No financial formula was rewritten or duplicated.** `cancelOrder`'s body was EXTRACTED verbatim into `FuturesPositionService.cancelRestingOrderWithin(tx, session, order)` and `cancelOrder` now calls it; the bucket clear calls the same method in a loop. The margin release is still `computeInitialMargin(remaining × price, leverage)` moved `locked → available` through the same `adjustBalance`, from the one place it has always lived. `marginMath`, `exposureRisk`, leverage tiers, MMR, the liquidation formula, funding, the insurance fund, MARKET reconciliation, the TP/SL trigger thresholds, Spot and Copy Trading are all untouched.
- **The lock is never nested.** The owner's rule was not to call public `placeOrder()`/`cancelOrder()` from inside a transaction already holding the futures-book lock — `FuturesBookTransaction.run` keeps an in-process queue per engine, so a nested `run` deadlocks in-process, not in PostgreSQL. `FuturesProtectionService` therefore reaches the transaction through `positionService.withFuturesBook(work)`, a thin bound wrapper over `run` on the service that already owns the `MatchingEngine`; it never constructs one, never nests a `run`, and `placeOrder` is still called only AFTER the commit, which a source-ordering test pins.
- **A regression I introduced and then fixed, caught by the real database.** `FuturesBookTransaction.run` is SERIALIZABLE; the claim previously ran at READ COMMITTED, where a losing writer waited on the row lock and then saw `count === 0`. Moving it inside made 40001 reachable: PostgreSQL now ABORTS the loser instead. `checkAndTrigger` has no per-trigger catch, so that would have killed the rest of the sweep. `isSerializationFailure` (`P2034`/`40001`/`40P01`, and nothing else) plus a `claimAndClearBucket` wrapper turn exactly that abort into an ordinary lost race — an aborted transaction changed nothing, so the trigger stays claimable — and let every other error propagate. **This was discovered by the native PostgreSQL test failing, not by reasoning about it.**
- **Failure safety, tested rather than asserted**: an error anywhere after the cancellations rolls the WHOLE transaction back — the trigger returns to `PENDING`, the order is still `OPEN`, and the reserve was not returned. No half-cancelled bucket, no double refund, no falsely claimed ownership.
- **Tests, +13 in `FuturesProtectionService.test.ts` (58 → 72, all passing).** 1 a winning TP cancels the resting entry, refunds ONCE and closes; 2 the same for the stop-loss side; 3 two resting entries, both cancelled, each refunded once; 4 another SYMBOL untouched; 5 another MARGIN TYPE untouched; 6 another USER untouched; 7 a REDUCE-ONLY resting order survives; 8 a sweep that LOSES the CAS cancels and refunds nothing; 9 a fill and the trigger are ordered by the lock, never interleaved; 10 two watcher workers → one cancellation, one refund, one close; 11 a serialization abort is a lost race and the rest of the sweep still fires; 12 an aborted claim cancels nothing and refunds nothing; 13 the catch is narrow — a real failure still propagates; plus a unit test that only serialization SQLSTATEs count. **Verified against `main`'s `fire()`: 1, 2, 3, 9, 10, 11, 12, 13, the ordering test and the predicate test fail, while 4, 5, 6, 7 and 8 pass on both — which is the evidence the fix does not over-reach beyond the one bucket.**
- **+3 native PostgreSQL tests in `futuresBookLock.pg.test.ts` (12 → 15, all passing against a real server** via `VOLTEX_PG_TEST_URL`; the production `DATABASE_URL` was never used**)**: real SQL predicates cancel only the bucket and refund `1 × 50000 / 10 = 5000` exactly once while the other-symbol, other-margin-type and reduce-only rows stay `OPEN`; an abort after the cancellations takes the claim back with them; and two workers with SEPARATE engines — so they can only serialize through the database — racing one trigger produce one cancellation, one refund, and `attempts === 1`.
- **Validation**: `npx tsc` PASS, `npm run build` PASS. Full Jest **2 422 passed / 2 451 with 15 skipped**; the **14 failures in 8 suites are identical to `main`** (`DepositService`, `TronDepositVerifier` and six frontend copy/presentation suites) — none added, none hidden. Every futures suite passes, `FuturesPositionService`'s included, which is what covers the extracted `cancelOrder` path.
- **Preserved from Codex**: nothing in this area was Codex's; `FuturesBookTransaction`, `LiquidationEngine`, `MarkPriceService`, `FuturesMarketExecution` and the matching engine were read but not modified. Two files changed plus two test files.
- Unresolved / next: **entry-order TP/SL is deliberately NOT implemented** — it waits on this review and on the owner's two outstanding decisions. Also still open: the 14 pre-existing failures on `main`, and the shared header inheriting typography and colour from its page wrapper.

## 2026-09-10 — Claude — PR #24 review follow-up: the bucket admits no new exposure while its stop is closing

- Agent: Claude. Same branch `fix/futures-protection-cancels-bucket-entries`, on top of the reviewed head `018098c5e10370286cfa2c9ebd97145debe57935`. **No new PR, not merged, not deployed.** One reported race, nothing else. Entry-order TP/SL is still unimplemented.
- **The remaining half of the hole, as reported and confirmed.** Cancelling the bucket's resting entries inside the claim transaction closes only half of it. That transaction COMMITS and releases the futures-book lock before the reduce-only close runs — it must, or the close would deadlock against itself taking the same lock. In that window a user could place a **brand new** non-reduceOnly order into the same `(userId, symbol, marginType)` bucket. It was never there to be cancelled, it outlived the close, and on filling it re-opened the exposure the stop had just closed. `placeOrder` had no admission guard against a `TRIGGERING` protection in the bucket.
- **Fix: admission refuses new exposure into a bucket whose close is in flight.** In `placeOrder`, under the futures-book lock AND the bucket's own `futures-exposure:{userId}:{symbol}:{marginType}` advisory lock already taken there, and **before** the MARKET estimate, the exposure/tier checks, the margin reservation, the `FuturesOrder` row and matching: read every `OPEN` position in the bucket, and if any has a `TRIGGERING` protection, throw. A rejected admission writes nothing at all — no order row, no reservation, no book change. Two queries, both plain `where` predicates.
- **Read from the database, never from memory.** The whole point is that another PROCESS may hold the claim; an in-memory flag would be invisible to it. A test drives a second `FuturesPositionService` with its own `MatchingEngine` — which knows nothing about the claim except what the DB tells it — and it still refuses.
- **`reduceOnly` stays admitted**, because the protection close itself is a reduce-only MARKET through this very method; blocking it would block the thing being protected. Reduce-only cannot open or increase exposure, which is the only thing this guard exists to prevent. Other users, other symbols, other margin types and `PENDING`/`EXECUTED`/`CANCELLED`/`FAILED` protections are all untouched — and a `TRIGGERING` claim pointing at an already-CLOSED position blocks nothing, because the bucket match is against an OPEN position.
- **Not a permanent bucket lock.** A claim resolves to `EXECUTED`/`CANCELLED`/`FAILED`, and one orphaned by a dead backend is reclaimed after `PROTECTION_STALE_CLAIM_MS`; admission is normal again either way. Tested on both the fake and the real database.
- **No circular dependency.** The guard is two Prisma reads inside the existing admission transaction — no import of, and no reference to, `FuturesProtectionService` from the order path.
- **One existing invariant test rewritten, with the reason recorded in the test.** `the dependency is one-way` asserted the order path names nothing about TP/SL at all. That is no longer the truth to pin: admission must ask the protection table whether a close is in flight. Reading a row is not a dependency; CALLING the service would be. So it was **narrowed, not dropped, and to something stricter than "no imports"**: the only protection identifier the executable code may name is `futuresPositionProtection` (asserted as an exact set over `/\w*[Pp]rotection\w*/`), the only thing it may know about that model is `status: 'TRIGGERING'`, and no trigger semantics (`takeProfit`, `stopLoss`, `triggerPrice`, `TAKE_PROFIT`, `STOP_LOSS`, `hasCrossed`) may cross into the engine. Prose is exempt on purpose — `cancelRestingOrderWithin`'s doc comment names the caller it exists for, and pinning the invariant to the comment would only pressure the comment into being dishonest.
- **Tests, +13 in `FuturesPositionService.test.ts` (79 → 92, all passing).** 1 a non-reduceOnly LIMIT is rejected with no order row, no margin locked, no book entry and the position untouched; 2 the same for MARKET **with 5 BTC of liquidity waiting**, so it is admission refusing it and not the book; 3 the reduce-only close is still admitted and still closes the position; 4 another user unaffected; 5 another symbol unaffected; 6 another margin type unaffected; 7 `PENDING` is an ARMED stop, not a closing one — admission normal; 8 `EXECUTED`/`CANCELLED`/`FAILED` block nothing (three cases); 10 the guard is temporary — refused mid-close, admitted once resolved; plus the cross-process DB-state test and the CLOSED-position case. The fake Prisma gained a `futuresPositionProtection` model and `futuresPosition.findMany`, and protections now roll back with everything else.
- **+2 native PostgreSQL tests (15 → 17, all passing** via `VOLTEX_PG_TEST_URL`; the production `DATABASE_URL` was never used**)**: T1's claim + bucket clear commits, then T2 — a second service with its own engine — attempts a new non-reduceOnly LIMIT in that exact window and is refused, with no resting entry left that could reopen the position and the balance unchanged from the post-refund `9000/1000`; and after the trigger is resolved to `EXECUTED` the very same order is admitted, reserving `1 × 40000 / 10` to leave `5000/5000`.
- **Discrimination, with only the guard removed:** in the Jest suite tests 1, 2, 10 and the cross-process test **fail** while 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8 and the CLOSED-position case **pass on both** — the evidence the guard does not over-reach past the one bucket. Both new PostgreSQL tests fail without it.
- **Validation**: `npx tsc` PASS, `npm run build` PASS. Full Jest **2 435 passed / 2 466 with 17 skipped**, against **2 422 / 2 451** at the reviewed head — the +13 are the new admission tests, and the **14 failures in 8 suites are identical to `main`**: none added, none hidden.
- Scope held: no `marginMath`, no `exposureRisk`, no leverage tiers, no MMR, no liquidation, no funding, no insurance fund, no MARKET reconciliation, no Spot, no Copy Trading. Two product files touched in total across the whole PR plus three test files.
- Unresolved / next: unchanged — **entry-order TP/SL is deliberately NOT implemented**, awaiting review and the owner's two outstanding decisions; the 14 pre-existing failures on `main`; and the shared header inheriting typography and colour from its page wrapper.

## 2026-09-11 — Claude — ф'ючерси: напрямок угоди став кнопкою, а не режимом

- Agent: Claude. Гілка `feat/futures-long-short-buttons` від `main`. Новий PR, **не злито, не задеплоєно**. Тільки фронтенд, тільки ф'ючерсна форма.
- **Що було.** `side` був вкладкою над формою: трейдер мусив оголосити напрямок до того, як торкнувся хоч одного поля, а розворот означав перемикання назад і втрату з екрана чисел протилежного боку. Власник показав скріншот Bybit, де форма одна, а внизу дві кнопки — «Лонг» і «Шорт».
- **Що зроблено.** Блок `fo-sideTabs` прибрано. Внизу дві кнопки `submit-btn buy` / `submit-btn sell` — ті самі класи й ті самі кольори, що вже мала єдина CTA. Ціна ліквідації тепер рахується для **обох** напрямків і показується парою `лонг / шорт` у кольорах кнопок, бо обраного боку більше немає. `previewLiquidationPrice` викликається з тими самими аргументами, формула не змінена.
- **Баг, який я сам створив і сам спіймав до коміту.** Першою редакцією кнопки були `type="submit"` з `onClick={() => setSide(...)}`. Це не працює: заплановане React-оновлення стану **не видно** обробнику сабміту в тій самій події, тому пішов би ПОПЕРЕДНІЙ напрямок. Виправлено тим, що `submitOrder(orderSide)` приймає бік **аргументом**, а не читає зі стану; кнопки стали `type="button"`. `handleSubmit` лишився й делегує в `place(side)`, тому Enter у полі досі відправляє ордер — той, який трейдер натискав востаннє.
- **Тести, +5** у `futuresOrderPanel.test.ts` (40 → 45): свіжа форма надсилає SHORT, коли натиснуто Шорт, без жодного попереднього кліку; те саме для LONG; **розворот надсилає НОВИЙ напрямок, а не попередній**; обидві кнопки підкоряються одному guard; жодна не `type="submit"`, щоб випадковий Enter не обирав напрямок за трейдера. **Дискримінація: з повернутим багом падають 3 тести** — два нові й наявний payload-тест G.
- **Три наявні тести оновлено, жоден не послаблено.** `futuresUiPolish` більше не пінить `aria-pressed={side === 'BUY'}` — такого контролу не має існувати; замість нього пін суворіший: `fo-sideTab` відсутній, обидві кнопки присутні з власними сторонами, `type="submit"` немає жодного, а бік їде аргументом (`submitOrder(orderSide)`, `side: orderSide`). `aria-pressed` для ТИПУ ордера лишився — тип і далі є обраним режимом. Відбиток `FuturesOrderForm.tsx` перезнято з переліком усіх відмінностей просто в тесті. `futuresFinalPolish` більше не «обирає» Short перед сабмітом — натискання Short і є сабмітом; очікуваний payload не змінився ні на байт.
- **Свідомо НЕ зроблено**, хоча є на скріншоті Bybit: TP/SL при відкритті — це «entry-order TP/SL», який власник велів не реалізовувати до рев'ю; його передумова — PR #24 — була змержена власником 2026-09-10 (`74cdbee`), але сама фіча і далі чекає на рішення; `postOnly` і `timeInForce` — таких полів немає у схемі Prisma, це бекенд і матчинг, а не UI; ставка комісії — у VOLTEX **немає джерела комісії за ф'ючерси**, тому там лишається прочерк, а не вигаданий відсоток; калькулятор — окрема фіча.
- **Перевірено**: `tsc -b` PASS, продакшн-збірка Vite PASS. Фронтенд-Jest **1063 passed / 1078, 6 skipped**, проти **1048 / 1073** на main — +5 нові тести, а **6 суїтів, що падають, ідентичні main**: жодного не додано, жодного не приховано.
- Незакрите: решта пунктів дизайн-аудиту (≈970 унікальних кольорів замість токенів, «Админка» в публічній навігації, щільність, дубльована панель «Информация о рынке»); аудит швидкості (картка на 1.2 МБ на екрані входу, 14.67 МБ незапитуваних зображень); **entry-order TP/SL і далі свідомо не реалізовано**.

## 2026-09-12 — Codex — PR #31 staging SSE reconnect semantics

- Re-fetched GitHub before editing: current `main` was `14b6869c6b767a17cd2153d3c2ca9cf9fb3c4a97`; PR #31 head was `098b23a813e9f8be23b71390bc3987f37a8bf5ba`. Continued the explicitly requested PR branch without rebasing, merging or deploying.

- Root-cause evidence excludes an application backpressure/cleanup close and the verifier's own timer: backend reported `peer_close` at 899387 ms with no backpressure, while the client received EOF at 899765 ms before its 900000 ms deadline. The exact external layer is not proven; official Render documentation allows HTTP responses up to 100 minutes, and Cloudflare documents a 900-second idle keepalive rather than a 15-minute active SSE lifetime.
- The verifier now follows the production client's reconnect semantics within one total observation deadline: an early EOF triggers a same-endpoint reconnect with a 10-second snapshot grace, preserves the same Tracker, and keeps revision, frame, universe and silence checks active. Failed reconnects and invalid post-reconnect continuity still fail; deadline completion still passes.
- Validation: `StagingVerifier.test.ts` plus `LiveTransport.test.ts` passed 60/60; backend TypeScript build, frontend TypeScript/Vite production build, verifier syntax and diff checks passed. No real staging soak was run because no secure token was used in this task. Next: deploy this PR candidate only to both staging services under separate authorization, then rerun the existing full 15-minute verifier with the token injected locally and review both service logs/metrics. Production remains untouched.

## 2026-09-15 — Claude — admin console opens on Пользователи, and that table scrolls (direct to `main`)

- Cherry-picked from `claude/github-repo-setup-029sd4` `e8a8817` onto fresh `main` `304ab20`. **Deliberately ONLY this commit**: that branch is 26 further commits of Futures/NativeDemo integration the owner has not reviewed, and `main` auto-deploys to production, so carrying it wholesale would have shipped all of it. Every file applied cleanly; only this handoff log conflicted (the branch carries entries `main` does not), resolved by keeping `main`'s history and appending this entry.
- **The clipped last row was a bug.** `adminStyles.table` set `overflow: 'hidden'` and then only restored X with `overflowX: 'auto'` — in a style object that leaves `overflow-y: hidden`, so the `maxHeight: '68vh'` cap cut the bottom rows off with no scrollbar. Measured before the fix: `scrollHeight 771` vs `clientHeight 678`. Now `overflowY: 'auto'` + `overscrollBehavior: 'contain'`; the rounded corners the `hidden` served are kept with `isolation: 'isolate'`. This is the SHARED `styles.table`, so Пользователи, Пополнения, Выводы, KYC and Журнал действий all gain the scroll.
- **Обзор removed.** Dropped from `SECTIONS` in `AdminLayout`; `activeSection` no longer needs its `/admin` exact-match case; `/admin` is now `<Navigate to="users" replace />`. `AdminOverviewPage.tsx` deleted with its lazy import and route — it was reachable only from that tab, and its data (newest users, incoming transfers, pending KYC) lives on the pages the sidebar still links.
- Tests assert the absence rather than dropping assertions: `adminDepositRails` checks `App.tsx` has no `AdminOverviewPage`, the nav has no `label: 'Обзор'`, the file does not exist, and the index route is the redirect; `routeCodeSplitting` drops it from the lazy list; the three `adminConsoleInteractions` tests that mounted the page were removed with it (diff-verified that only those three went).
- Verified on this exact tree: frontend `tsc` PASS, Vite production build PASS, the three affected suites PASS. Browser QA at 1440×1000 on an isolated PostgreSQL with 15 seeded users and a real login: `/admin` lands on `/admin/users`, sidebar has no Обзор link, table `overflow-y: auto` with `canScroll true`, last row not fully visible before scrolling and fully visible after, 0 page errors.
- No Futures, trading, wallet or backend change is included.

## 2026-09-15 — Claude — Futures order-size validation fixed across the whole market universe (PR #83, `codex/private-cross-demo-bybit-model`)

- Continues PR #83 head `6d6b959`; no reset, no force, no merge, PR #83's own branch untouched. Committed and pushed to the designated branch `claude/github-repo-setup-029sd4`, which was a strict ancestor of that head, so the push is a fast-forward and loses nothing. Material files: `frontend/src/lib/futuresMath.ts`, `frontend/src/components/FuturesOrderForm.tsx`, `frontend/src/lib/i18n/locales/{ru,en,zh,es,hi,ja,ko}.ts`, new `src/futures/__tests__/orderSizing.test.ts`, `frontend/src/lib/__tests__/futuresOrderPanel.test.ts`. TradingView Bar Replay deliberately not started — deferred by the owner.
- **Reproduced first, in the real engine, before any code changed.** Two independent defects, both in the CLIENT's sizing — the backend's validation (`FuturesPositionService`, `exposureRisk`, `getLeverageTier`, the `LEVERAGE_TIERS` table and its `maintenanceAmount` continuity) was checked and is correct; nothing in `src/futures` or `src/api/routes/futures.ts` was changed.
- **Defect 1 — sizing ignored that leverage is a function of size.** `applyPercent` computed `margin × leverage`; the tier ceiling then clamped the leverage underneath a quantity already sized for the higher one. 100% of margin at 100x asked for `margin × 100`, which falls in a 50x / 20x / 10x tier and needs two, five or ten times the margin the account holds — the server refused it with `Insufficient USDT margin balance`. Measured: 1 000 USDT → 100 000 notional needs 2 000; 5 000 → 500 000 needs 25 000; 10 000 → 1 000 000 needs 100 000. Only 50 000 survived, because it is exactly tier 1's cap.
- **Defect 2 — `toFixed(8)` rounds to NEAREST.** 10 000 / 60 000 becomes 0.16666667, fractionally more than the margin that bought it. Measured across a 12-contract universe at 1x/5x/10x/20x and 1 000/10 000/100 000 USDT: **4–7 of 12 contracts rejected a 100% slider order in every combination**, at every notional, not just large ones. Flooring at 8dp overshoots in 0 of 216 cases.
- **Fix.** New `maxAffordableNotional` in `futuresMath.ts` solves the size/leverage fixed point: `tier.maxLeverage` is non-increasing in notional, so one pass over the tiers — each offering `min(selected, tier.maxLeverage)` and having room to its own cap — gives the exact maximum. 10 000 USDT at 100x is 250 000 at 50x, not 1 000 000 at 100x. `floorToDecimals` truncates on the decimal text rather than scaling by 1e8 (1e11 units × 1e8 exceeds 2^53 at the low-priced end, where `Math.floor` is a no-op), plus a 1e-12 relative shave that swamps double error without being visible.
- Also in the panel: the leverage clamp was a one-way ratchet (`setLeverage(ceiling)` in an effect), so one large order permanently rewrote a 100x selection and every later size was quoted at the lower figure. Leverage is now DERIVED — `min(requestedLeverage, effectiveMaxLeverage)` — and rises again by itself when the size comes down. Changing leverage re-sizes a percentage-chosen order and leaves a hand-typed quantity alone. Reduce-only sizes against the position it can close, not the free balance. An order the account cannot margin now disables both buttons and says so (new key `futures.insufficientMargin`, all 7 locales) instead of making a round trip for a 400. The unknown-vs-empty rules from PR #14 are preserved everywhere: a `null` balance, position list or order list still suppresses sizing and blocks nothing.
- Preserved from Codex: the entire futures engine, the tier table, the exposure projection, the advisory-lock admission path, the margin lock/refund arithmetic, the `/futures/config` payload, the order payload shape, and every existing assertion in `futuresOrderPanel.test.ts` (61/61 pass, including the 8 that pin the leverage bounds).
- Verified: backend `tsc` PASS (after `npm install` — `@types/ws` was declared but missing locally), frontend `tsc` PASS, Vite production build PASS. New `orderSizing.test.ts` **95/95**: it sizes with the panel's own functions and places through the real route + `FuturesPositionService` + matching engine over 8 contracts (60 000 down to 0.000 008 2 USDT) × 50k/100k/500k/1M and 1x–100x, requiring 201, margin within budget, and MAXIMALITY — a millionth more is refused. Confirmed the suite is load-bearing: restoring the pre-fix sizing fails it on exactly the 100k/500k/1M cases across every contract plus the rounding cases. `futuresOrderPanel.test.ts` 61/61 with 9 new panel tests. Full Jest: **failing-suite set byte-identical to the same tree without these changes — 32 suites, all pre-existing on this branch** (CFD/deposit/provider network suites and stale frontend fingerprints); zero new failures.
- Not verified: browser QA of the panel, the Render preview, GitHub CI. Not merged, not deployed.

## 2026-09-15 — Claude — owner trades the ORIGINAL Futures terminal via an account/execution adapter (PR #83, `codex/private-cross-demo-bybit-model`)

- Fresh fetch: `main` `304ab20`, PR #83 `6d6b959`, my branch `add3b71` (exactly 1 commit ahead of #83, #83 a strict ancestor — no newer #83 commits existed, so nothing to preserve beyond a fast-forward). No reset, no force. Order-sizing commit `add3b71` carried into #83 by fast-forward.
- **The integration that was missing.** `FuturesPage` chose `native.requested ? NativeDemoTicket : FuturesOrderForm` and swapped the whole bottom panel for `NativeDemoPanel`. Both alternatives are now gone from the page: there is ONE `FuturesOrderForm`, one `FuturesPositionsPanel`, one `FuturesOrdersPanel`, one `AssetsPanel`, and they render for every account. What differs for the owner is the SOURCE of account state and the DESTINATION of an order.
- **New seam.** `lib/futuresExecution.tsx` (`FuturesExecution` + provider + `REAL_FUTURES_EXECUTION`, whose implementation is the `api` call each component used to make inline, so an ordinary account's path is unchanged by construction); `lib/futuresAccountSource.ts` (a tiny context so `useFuturesAccount` can read a replacement state without importing back into the execution module); `lib/nativeFuturesAdapter.ts` (pure projection of `NativeState` into `FuturesAccountState` + the native draft); `lib/useNativeFuturesExecution.ts` (binds `useNativeDemo` to that interface). `FuturesOrderForm`, `FuturesPositionsPanel`, `FuturesOrdersPanel` and `FuturesPositionProtectionCell` now write through the adapter instead of `api.*` directly.
- Identity/authorization untouched: the same `PRIVATE_TRADING_OWNER_ID` + ADMIN + session gate decides, via the existing `/private-trading/access` verdict. No second `User`, no balance reset, no re-credit, no separate demo onboarding, no email-substring rule, no all-admins rule. Admin Panel, Spot, CFD, Wallet, Banking, Card and Copy Trading untouched.
- Isolation: `ready` is false while the verdict or the native state is unknown AND when either failed — trading is blocked, never a fallback to the real engine. The real account store is not subscribed at all for that account (`useFuturesAccount(nativeExecution?{}:{…})`), so the real endpoints are not polled for it. Margin mode is reported as **Cross**, which is what the engine settles in; the toggle is locked rather than offering a mode that does nothing. Other users' mode is untouched.
- **Chart trading.** New `ChartTradingToggle` ("Торговля с графика") on the original chart — a TOOL switch. On: a bar can be picked and the pick prices the ORDINARY order form. Off: the pickers are hidden and an unsent pick is dropped (`onCancelSelection`) so the next ordinary order cannot open in the past; positions, orders, balance and history are not touched. Bar Replay deliberately NOT started. `?privateTrading=1` is inert — `FuturesRoute` keys off `?card=` alone, so the legacy link lands on the ordinary terminal (pinned by a test).
- **Order sizing on the NATIVE path — the actual bug.** The real-engine suite could not prove this: `FuturesPositionService` enforces no quantity step, no contract ceiling and no minimum order value. Reproduced two rejections. (1) `INVALID_QUANTITY_STEP`: the slider's `toFixed(8)` produced 0.83333333 BTC on a contract whose step is 0.001 — every slider-sized order on every contract, at every notional. (2) `INVALID_ORDER_SIZE`: a MARKET order is bound by `maxMarketOrderQty` (120 BTC), far below a 500k/1M notional on many contracts, and the old message named neither the limit nor its value.
- Fix, both halves. Client: new `fitQuantityToContract` + `stepDecimals` in `futuresMath` floor onto the contract's step and clamp to its ceiling (down only, so a size that fitted the margin still fits), refuse below `minOrderQty`/`minNotionalValue` rather than sizing up, and the form blocks and names the rule before a round trip. New authenticated `GET /private-trading/native/contracts/:symbol` publishes the same rules `validateContractOrder` enforces. Server: `ContractRuleError` carries `{limit, allowed, actual}`; the compound size check is split into its three real rules so a refusal says WHICH one bound and what it allows. No real margin/contract/risk limit was removed or relaxed. Historical trades are still priced by the server from the bar the client names.
- Preserved from Codex: the whole native engine and its Cross/fees/funding/TP/SL/liquidation/partial-fill/averaging/persistence/idempotency math, the custom funding model (Long −0.001 / Short +0.004 per 8h — unchanged), the P&L card at 1080×1215 with the compact modal and enlarged bags, the frozen snapshots, the route guards, and every existing assertion in the futures suites.
- Verified: backend `tsc` PASS, frontend `tsc` PASS, Vite production build PASS. New `src/private-trading/__tests__/nativeOrderSizing.test.ts` **70/70** — sizes with the panel's own functions, through the adapter, into the native engine's own `validateContractOrder`, over 6 contracts (60000 → 0.0082 USDT, steps 0.001 → 10) × 50k/100k/500k/1M, and opens a real position in the engine. New `frontend/src/lib/__tests__/nativeFuturesTerminal.test.ts` **22/22** pins the single-terminal integration and the projection. `futuresOrderPanel` 61/61, `orderSizing` 95/95. Full Jest: **failing-suite set byte-identical to the branch baseline (32, all pre-existing)** — zero new failures.
- Browser QA on an isolated PostgreSQL (`voltex_qa`, `prisma db push`), real ADMIN seeded with an existing DemoBalance, **real login through the real form** (no forged token, no fake `/me`): `/futures` renders the ORIGINAL interface at 1440×1000 and 390×844 — terminal shell, chart, order book, pair list, ticker, the original order form with its leverage control, size slider and both Long/Short buttons, and the original 5 bottom tabs (Открытые ордера / Позиции / История ордеров / История позиций / Активы). No alternative ticket or panel in the DOM, no horizontal page scroll, zero page errors.

## 2026-09-15 — Claude — reference parity: equal entry fields, reference positions row, account summary (PR #83, `codex/private-cross-demo-bybit-model`)

- **1. Price and quantity are one field shape, used twice.** They were structurally different — the price caption sat INSIDE the field (absolute) while the quantity caption sat ABOVE it, which is why the heights differed. Both are now `fo-field` > `fo-fieldCaption` + `fo-fieldRow` > `fo-input` + `fo-fieldTrailing`. Every box property (height, min-height, width, box-sizing, padding, background, border, radius) is declared ONCE on the shared class; the input carries no box of its own (`border: 0`, `flex: 1 1 0`, `min-width: 0`) and the trailing slot is `flex: 0 0 auto`, so "Последняя" and the asset unit take space from the input and never from the box. "Доступная маржа" left the quantity label — it was the one thing that could stretch one field relative to the other — and is now the summary's line. One `PercentSlider` with the 0/25/50/75/100 presets under the quantity. Long/Short share `flex: 1 1 0` and one explicit 40px height. Messages are siblings of both fields, never children.
- **2. Positions row rebuilt to the reference column set:** contract with side · Cross · leverage under it; quantity with its unit; position value in the quote asset; entry; mark; liquidation; unrealized with ROI under it; realized as its OWN column; TP/SL (the existing server-state control); close by «Лимитный» and «Рынок»; a compact P&L-card icon button. Position value is size × MARK — the two figures the row already shows — and `—` when the mark is unknown, never silently valued at entry. **Unrealized and realized are never summed**: the size that banked the realized figure is out of the size carrying the unrealized one, and the fees/funding already inside the realized figure would otherwise be counted twice. «Лимитный» is not a dead button: it hands the position to the ORDINARY order form as a reduce-only LIMIT ticket (`onLimitClose` → `closeTicket`), which the trader then prices and submits; where no such handler exists it is not rendered. The card button renders only where a card service exists (`execution.showPnlCard`). Horizontal scroll is on `.futures-positions-scroll` with the table at `min-width: max-content`, so a large P&L widens the table and the container scrolls — the page never does, and no cell truncates. Tab counters already came from `account.positions.data`/`account.orders.data`; the auto-collapse already requires BOTH to be verified-empty, so an open position cannot be hidden by it (both now pinned by tests). No invented loans, instruments or dead tabs.
- Backend: open positions now report `realizedPnl` (the column already on the Prisma row) so the new column is real for the real engine too; the adapter maps the native engine's own `realizedPnl` and deliberately does NOT use `netPnl` for an open row, because that is the closed result and already contains the fees and funding.

It reads the same single `useFuturesAccount` the form, slider and tables read, so the adapter can replace all four at once and they cannot disagree. Unknown stays `—` and a real zero stays `0`. It reads ONLY the quote asset's own balance row: nothing values BTC/ETH into USDT or counts them as collateral (pinned by a test).

## 2026-09-15 — Claude — one `/futures` terminal, phase 1: the four PR #83 QA defects and localized refusals (`claude/futures-owner-terminal`)

- Branch cut from `main` `7e83003` (fresh fetch; the owner's stated `a8d9c51` was stale) and merged with `origin/codex/private-cross-demo-bybit-model` at `59126a0` — Codex's cross-demo engine work is preserved whole, nothing reverted, no force-push. The Admin console fix already in production (PR #86) is NOT duplicated and NOT touched here: `Обзор`, admin scrolling and every admin page are untouched on this branch.
- **20.4 — a reduce-only LIMIT close stays a LIMIT.** It used to be rewritten into a `CLOSE` command, which market-closed the position at whatever the book was, ignoring the price the trader had just typed. `NativeCommand.OPEN` now carries `reduceOnly` + `positionId` (zod refuses `reduceOnly` without a position), the client draft carries them, and `useNativeFuturesExecution` splits the path: MARKET reduces still take the `CLOSE` command, a LIMIT reduce rests at its own price.
- **20.2 / 20.3 — the owner no longer polls the real account, and never falls back to it.** `useNativeDemo` held a two-state `allowed` flag that a failed access check silently cleared, which both let the real `/api/v1/futures/{balances,positions,orders/me}` endpoints be polled for the owner and made a simulation outage degrade into the REAL account. It is now a sticky tri-state `binding` — `'unknown' | 'owner' | 'ordinary'`: unknown trades nowhere and polls nothing, an error revokes the session data but never demotes an established `'owner'`, and only a session change resets it to unknown. Fail-closed by construction, not by a caught exception.
- **20.1 — maintenance margin is the engine's own figure, not a second derivation.** `FuturesAccountSummary` recomputed it from the REAL leverage-tier table applied to positions the simulation engine had priced under its own tiers, which is why it read 0.00%. The engine now publishes `account_aggregate` (wallet, equity, UPL, initial margin, maintenance margin, order reserve, available, maintenance ratio, liquidatable) through the execution seam, and the card DISPLAYS it. A real account publishes no aggregate and keeps the previous derivation untouched.
- **13 — a refusal names its own cause, in the interface language.** The reported defect was an order rejected for margin coming back as `max MARKET quantity …`, sending the trader to shrink a size that broke no limit. Server side: `INSUFFICIENT_DEMO_MARGIN` now reads «Недостаточно средств для размещения этого ордера.» (and no longer calls the owner's account a demo account), an unmapped engine code answers a plain sentence and is `console.warn`ed instead of being printed at the trader, and a contract-rule refusal carries `code` + `limit` + `allowed` + `actual`. Client side: `PrivateTradingError` carries `code`/`detail`, and the new `frontend/src/lib/futuresOrderErrors.ts` maps them to 35 new i18n keys in **all seven languages** — the named limit wins over the code, because `INVALID_ORDER_SIZE` alone cannot say whether a size was too small, too large, or too large for a MARKET order. Used by the order form, the position close and the TP/SL editor. Raw codes reach the console and the tests only.
- `PrivateTradingError` moved to its own `frontend/src/lib/privateTradingError.ts`; `privateTradingApi.ts` re-exports it, so no import site changed. The reason is that `privateTradingApi.ts` builds a client from `import.meta.env` at import time, which is not loadable outside Vite — the localizer and its suite would not have run.
- Verified: backend `tsc` PASS, frontend `tsc` PASS, Vite production build PASS. New `frontend/src/lib/__tests__/futuresOrderErrors.test.ts` **11/11** and `src/private-trading/__tests__/nativeErrorMessages.test.ts` **6/6** (the latter drives the real router through supertest). `src/private-trading` + `src/futures` **697/697**. Full Jest before and after this phase: **25 failing suites / 88 failing tests, identical — zero new failures**, +2 suites and +17 tests passing. Four pre-existing suites were updated, not weakened, where they asserted the old source text (`nativeDemoFrontend` now pins the tri-state binding; `futuresReferenceRow` now pins both the aggregate display AND the unchanged real-account branch), and six harnesses gained stubs for the two new modules.
- **NOT verified.** No browser QA in this phase — this sandbox still cannot reach `api.bybit.com` (curl exit 000), so there is no live native account to refuse an order and read the sentence back from. The localized text is proven by the router test and the unit suite, not by a live session. Remaining brief items (multi-asset wallet collateral, the owner adjustment ledger, centralized money math, collateral pricing, Cross liquidation on equity, the fee/funding audit, the single execution layer, P&L card snapshot, wallet consistency, atomicity/idempotency) are NOT done in this phase.

## 2026-09-15 — Claude — phase 2: the Cross collateral base is the whole wallet, priced or named (`claude/futures-owner-terminal`)

- Continues `3d6049c` on the same branch. `main` untouched, Admin untouched, Codex's engine work untouched.
- **A new pure module, `src/private-trading/native/collateral.ts`**, answers one question: what is a multi-asset wallet worth as Cross collateral. Its whole reason to exist is the rule that **an unavailable price is not a price of zero**. A holding whose quote cannot be fetched comes back `status: 'UNPRICED'` with `price`/`value` `null`, is named in `unpriced`, and leaves `complete: false` — it is never valued at 0 (which would shrink the collateral and liquidate a solvent account), never at a guess, never at a stale or neighbouring figure. An explicit `null` price, a missing map entry, a `0` quote and a negative quote are all the SAME answer and are reported identically. `priced` is therefore not a wallet valuation on its own: it is the part that is known, and `complete` says whether that is all of it. Locked quantity counts — it still backs the account. Holding zero of an unpriced asset is not an unknown. `asOf` is the STALEST priced input, because a valuation is only as fresh as its oldest price. All arithmetic is 36 dp BigNumber on a private clone; a wallet large enough to lose cents in a double is asserted to the last decimal.
- **The repository can read the whole wallet**: `NativeRepository.holdings(actor)` returns every `DemoBalance` row (both columns), not the USDT row alone. `available()` is unchanged, so the existing initialization path is untouched.
- **`NativeDemoService.collateral(actor)`** prices each non-settle holding from the SAME market data the terminal trades on — one price source, so the collateral and the positions it backs cannot drift apart — and at the **mark**, for the same reason. A contract that does not exist and a provider that is down produce the identical answer: we do not know. The settle asset needs no quote and can never be unpriced, so a total provider outage still values the USDT the owner holds. **No total is written into the code**: the figure is whatever the owner's rows and the live quotes make it, and a test changes the wallet and asserts the figure changes with it.
- Exposed as `GET /private-trading/native/collateral` and as `nativeDemoApi.collateral()` with the `null`-preserving `NativeCollateral` types on the client.
- Verified: backend `tsc` PASS, frontend `tsc` PASS, Vite build PASS. New `src/private-trading/__tests__/collateral.test.ts` **12/12**; `nativeDemoService` **13/13** (4 new, driving the real service against a failing quote). `src/private-trading` + `src/futures` **719/719**. Frontend lib suites: **18 failing suites / 71 failing tests, identical to the branch baseline — zero new failures.**
- **NOT done in this phase**, and still open from the brief: the adjustment/simulation ledger view, centralized server-side money math, Cross liquidation on account equity, the fee/funding audit, the single execution layer, the P&L card snapshot, Wallet-UI consistency with this valuation, atomicity/idempotency review, the browser QA walkthrough and the isolated preview. No browser QA in this phase — this sandbox still cannot reach `api.bybit.com`.
- Not merged to `main`, not deployed to production.

Merged into the branch as `3c4a356`; **no conflicts** — the warm-cache work and the engine work touched `FuturesPage.tsx` but not the same lines. One assertion in `terminalAccountPanel` had to follow a rename it already described: the real account's polling is suppressed by the ENGINE SEAM being non-null rather than by a separate access flag, which also makes a still-undecided binding fail-closed.
- **Re-verified after the sync**, all passing: margin shortfall reads as a margin shortfall and not `max MARKET quantity`; contract-size refusals stay separate errors with their own limit and value; a reduce-only LIMIT close stays a LIMIT; the owner's account never falls back to the real futures account; maintenance margin comes from the engine aggregate; Cross collateral is computed from the whole wallet with no hardcoded total; an unknown asset price is `null`/UNPRICED and never 0.
- **The ledger (`src/private-trading/native/ledger.ts`).** The engine kept a balance but no explanation of it. This projects the engine's own event journal into one line per change — `INITIAL_COLLATERAL`, `OPENING_FEE`, `CLOSING_FEE`, `LIQUIDATION_FEE`, `REALIZED_PNL`, `FUNDING` — each with its source, position, price and the running balance after it. Fees and funding are their OWN lines, which is what makes the double-counting question answerable: a position's realized figure is the sum of its lines and each component appears exactly once. It is a projection, not a second bookkeeping system: the closing balance is compared against the engine's `walletBalance` on every read, so `reconciled: false` means the engine moved money without journaling it rather than being papered over. Events that move no money (CANCEL, LEVERAGE, PROTECTION) get no line.
- **The account (`src/private-trading/native/accountModel.ts`).** A Cross account is backed by the simulation ledger AND the rest of the wallet valued at mark. Adding them is not double counting: the collateral moved into the ledger at initialization was DEBITED from the wallet rows, so each unit of value is in exactly one of the two — proven in the browser run below, where the total is identical before and after initialization. **Liquidation is decided on that account equity**, not on a single settle row. And when the wallet cannot be fully valued the model refuses the liquidation question entirely — `liquidatable: null`, not `false` — because an incomplete valuation understates collateral and understated collateral makes a solvent account look liquidatable. The unpriced assets are named, and the terminal and the Wallet both say on screen that the figure is a floor.
- **One path.** Every response the terminal receives — state poll, order, close, refresh — now goes through a single `authoritative()` helper, so a poll and an order response can no longer disagree about available margin for a poll interval. The client passes the account through (`state?.account ?? null`) instead of rebuilding it field by field. `FuturesAccountAggregate` is a transcript of the server's object; the real account's old derivation survives only as the `:` branch behind the `aggregate ?` guard. Pinned structurally by `futuresSingleMoneySource.test.ts`, which also asserts the sanity-check total appears in NO source file.
- **The Wallet page** now renders the futures account from the futures endpoint (`WalletFuturesAccountCard`). It does no arithmetic at all — recomputing equity from the wallet rows would be a second answer to a question that already has one. It renders nothing for an account not bound to the simulation engine, so an ordinary user's wallet page is unchanged.

The cause: `walletBalance` was deliberately renamed to `settleBalance` when the account model started counting the wallet's OTHER assets as Cross collateral — one name was being used for two different quantities. No client reader was left behind (zero remaining readers outside the ledger's own reconciliation field). The test was pinning the old name. It now asserts the new contract AND the equivalent financial magnitude: `settleBalance` is still 10 000 000, `walletCollateral` is `0` because the demo row was DEBITED into the ledger, `collateral` is exactly 10 000 000, and the ledger reconciles. **The concurrency assertions are untouched** — two tabs still produce one revision, and the demo balance still drains to `0` exactly once.
  - That failure also exposed a **latent contract hazard**: because `view()` returned the engine's own account and `authoritative()` replaced it, every response's type was a UNION of two different account shapes, so a caller could not tell which it had and a field could go missing without anything failing to compile. `view()` now returns no account at all; `authoritative()` is the only place that builds one. There is exactly one account shape on the wire.
- **CI failure 1 — browser QA, `initialize: 503` in every scenario.** The root cause was NOT market data: the isolated review server's `ReviewRepository` did not implement the `holdings()` method the Cross collateral model added, and its catch-all error handler turned the resulting `TypeError` into a generic 503 with nothing in the logs. Fixed three ways, none of which weakens production logic:
  1. `ReviewRepository.holdings()` now reports the preview session's wallet exactly as the production repository reports the owner's `DemoBalance` rows — the settle row, which initialization debits into the ledger, plus a non-settle holding priced through the SAME collector path the positions are priced on. No account value is written in; the figure moves when the price moves.
  2. The review server's error handler now prints the underlying stack server-side (the response stays sanitized). A bare 503 with nothing behind it is what made a missing method look like a provider outage for a whole CI run.
  3. Two QA scripts followed the renamed contract (`usedMargin` → `initialMargin`, `walletBalance` → `settleBalance`) and the preview check gained assertions that the wallet collateral is priced, that collateral equals the settle ledger plus the wallet valuation, and that the ledger reconciles.

They now refresh through `execution.refresh(...)`, which routes to the native engine for the owner and to the same store as before for everyone else. This is the single authoritative path the brief asks for, and it is the fix for the last real leak in the owner's isolation.
- **A pre-existing QA script bug, unmasked.** `protection.takeProfit` was compared as a STRING against the submitted limit with a trailing `.0` stripped, assuming a normalization the engine does not do — it stores the trigger exactly as submitted. It only failed when mark × 2 landed on a whole tenth, and was never reached before because `initialize` 503'd first. It now compares the PRICE; a wrong trigger still fails.
