## 2026-09-16 — Claude — phase 5: the blank order book, and Bybit parity for the book, inputs and header (`claude/terminal-orderbook-bybit-parity`)

- **Branch.** New and clean, cut from `main` @ `38e5550` (the PR #89 merge), as instructed. Not continued from `claude/futures-owner-terminal`.
- **Root cause of the blank Futures order book — it was not CSS, and not the venue.** `FuturesDepthBook.apply` validated the venue's frame timestamp against the VISITOR's clock: it threw on any frame stamped more than 1s ahead of `Date.now()` or more than 30s behind it. `frame.ts` is Bybit's clock; `Date.now()` in a browser is the visitor's, and the two are routinely seconds apart (a laptop resumed from sleep, a machine with no time sync, a phone that has changed network). On such a machine EVERY frame threw. The transport treated any throw as a transport fault, so each throw tore down the socket and emptied every panel, and the fresh snapshot that followed was rejected identically — a permanently empty book plus an endless reconnect with backoff climbing to 15s. Reproduced with a failing test before any fix; four skews (3s, 60s slow; 31s, 1h fast) are now regression tests. Ordering never needed a clock: `u`/`seq` are the venue's own monotonic counters, and freshness is now measured from the last frame WE accepted, comparing the local clock only against itself.
- **Three further ways the panel emptied itself**, all removed: `subscribe()` unconditionally emitted an empty book (so any remount, including React's own double-mount, flashed a live contract blank); every reconnect called `resetBooks()`; a hidden tab destroyed the book. A late subscriber is now handed the book that exists; reconnects and backgrounding keep the levels and label them `stale`; only after 60s of silence are they dropped as `unavailable` — unknown, never zero. Failure is graded: an unreadable frame is dropped and the book stands, a desync (delta before snapshot, sequence rollback, crossed book) resubscribes that one topic while the last good levels stay on screen, and only the transport dying reconnects the transport. The displayed levels are held separately from the delta accumulator (`lastGood`) so a desync reset cannot blank the panel.
- **Second source, for browsers that cannot hold the socket at all.** New `GET /market/futures/orderbook/:symbol` (`src/api/routes/marketDepth.ts`, `BybitMarketDataService.getOrderBook`). It shares the SAME circuit breaker, request budget and `ProviderCache` as the universe and tickers, and that cache coalesces concurrent readers onto one in-flight request held for 1s — cost scales with contracts watched, not people watching. The symbol must be a listed linear perpetual, so it is not an open proxy. A malformed level refuses the whole book rather than returning a shorter one; an outage returns no levels at all, so nothing downstream can draw it as zero depth. The client starts it only after 6s of socket silence, one poller per contract, never while hidden, and stops it the instant a socket frame lands.
- **Render load.** Measured in the browser over 60s idle on `/futures`: **114 API calls, all pre-existing polls** (mark-price 27, positions 15, funding 15, orders/me 12, balances 12, private-trading/access 12, candles 12, snapshot 4, live 3, derivatives 2, universe 1) and **0 depth-fallback requests**. Ten rapid contract switches: 0 extra sockets, 0 fallback requests. Rotating BTC→ETH→SOL→BTC: 0 new sockets. The healthy path therefore adds **nothing**; the fallback is only reached when the browser's own socket cannot work, where the alternative today is an empty panel.
- **Visual parity.** The row count divided body height by `REFERENCE_ROW_HEIGHT` (22) while the CSS drew rows at 26px, so the panel always asked for ~18% more rows than it had room for; both now read one variable. Denser rows/headers/controls, fixed column proportions, tabular figures everywhere, depth bars moved to `transform: scaleX()` (composited, off the layout path) with a short transition. Book column heads use short forms (`Кол-во`, `Всего`) because `Количество(BTC)` overlapped its neighbour in a ~215px panel. `FuturesReferenceBook` lost its `key={symbol}`, which had been discarding the trader's tab, mode and step on every contract switch.
- **The white artifact beside BTC/USDT.** `.pair-arrow` was a `▼` hidden by `font-size:0` with two borders rotated 45° to draw a chevron — on an inline `<span>`, where width/height do not apply, AND as a shrinkable item in a 90px flex row that "BTC/USDT" does not fit in. It was being squashed to **1px wide**: a thin pale stroke. Fixed by `inline-block` + `flex: 0 0 auto` + letting the selector size to its content, defined once for every terminal. The browser QA caught the flex-shrink half after the first fix was incomplete.
- **Header and inputs.** Six files each set the last price to a different size between 19px and 25px; all now read `--terminal-last-size` (20px). The Futures field box goes 52px → 40px from one variable, the trailing unit slot is fixed-width, number-input steppers are removed on all three surfaces. **"Последняя" now fills the last TRADED price** — it filled the MARK price, a different quantity — sourced from this contract's own execution tape with the shared ticker as fallback, and not offered at all when the last price is unknown. No execution or margin path changed: MARKET orders still price from mark exactly as before.
- **Verified.** Backend `tsc` PASS, frontend `tsc` PASS, Vite production build PASS. New `futuresDepth` suite **24/24 PASS**; new `BybitOrderBook` suite **15/15 PASS**. Targeted suites (`src/services/marketData`, `src/private-trading`, `frontend/src/lib/__tests__`): **21 failed / 80 tests failed on my branch AND 21 failed / 80 tests failed on pristine `main`** — the failing-suite lists are identical, so **zero new failures**; my branch adds one suite and 30 passing tests. Browser QA `scripts/qa-terminal-book-browser.cjs`: **120/120 checks PASS at 1440×1000 and 390×844** — initial load, clock disagreement, 60s of live updates without emptying, column/row stability, BTC→ETH→SOL→BTC, 10 rapid switches, step change, bids/asks/both, trades tab, connection drop and recovery, hidden→foreground, fallback on and off, idle load, header at BTC/XRP/DOGE magnitudes, input geometry under typing, the Last button, book-level price picking, and Spot and CFD. Evidence under `docs/qa/terminal-book/`.
- **Preserved from Codex / earlier work.** The bottom market-ticker removal on Spot/CFD/Futures, the instant-first-paint warm cache and the TradingView ruler are all on `main` @ `38e5550` and untouched — this branch is cut from that commit and reverts nothing. The `TradeTerminal.css` shared-stylesheet fingerprint and the `TickerBar.tsx` behaviour fingerprint both still pass: the shared stylesheet edit was withdrawn once the token rule was found to win on specificity, and the TickerBar guard normalises only the one presentational line, so any behavioural change to that file still trips it.
- **NOT verified.** **No live Bybit provider run.** The depth feed in QA is a LOCAL socket speaking the venue's v5 frame shape (`scripts/serve-terminal-book-review.cjs`) — this sandbox cannot reach `api.bybit.com`. Provider freshness, real book depth, real trade rates and the new `/market/futures/orderbook/:symbol` route against the real venue are therefore unproven here, as is whether Render's egress can reach Bybit (the existing server-side collector suggests it can, but that is an inference, not a measurement). Spot's Kraken socket could not connect in the sandbox either, so Spot was verified for layout and header only, not live depth. `frontend/src/lib/api.ts` and `src/api/routes/futures.ts` fingerprint tests fail on this branch AND on pristine `main`; `api.ts` gained one `export` keyword on `API_BASE` and that pre-existing failure masks it.
- Not merged to `main`, not deployed to production.

## 2026-09-16 — Claude — phase 6: the demo deposit that could not reach the terminal, and the chart-tools strip (`claude/futures-demo-init-chart-menu`)

- **Branch.** New and clean, cut from `main` @ `4092c01` (the PR #93 merge), as instructed.
- **Root cause of `Доступная маржа: 0.00 USDT`.** `nativeBalances()` returned a LITERAL `[{ asset:'USDT', available:'0', locked:'0' }]` whenever `state.account` was null, and `nativeAccountState` published that as an ANSWERED resource. The summary card read the row, found `available: '0'`, and printed a confident `0.00 USDT` over a wallet the server had just reported as holding 10 000 000 in demo funds. An account that has not been opened has no futures balance to report; it is not an account holding zero. `nativeBalances` now returns `null` and the resource stays unanswered, which the card already renders as a dash. The second half of the blocker was that removing `NativeDemoTicket` in #89 removed the only caller of `nativeDemoApi.initialize`, so nothing could open the account at all.
- **The fix, inside the ONE ordinary panel.** A new `activation` field on the execution seam (`FuturesAccountActivation`) is non-null for exactly one situation: the server says this account trades the simulation engine, there is no ledger, and `demoAvailable` is a positive number. `FuturesAccountSummary` renders that as one extra row (the server's own string, grouped for reading, never recomputed) plus one `Начать торговлю` button — no second terminal, no `NativeDemoTicket`, no `NativeDemoPanel`. While unopened, every other figure reads `—` rather than `0.00`. `activation` is read as `execution.activation ?? null` because `undefined !== null` would have blanked every figure on an ordinary funded account — caught by `futuresAccountUnknownState` before it shipped.
- **Exactly once.** `initialize` is a `useCallback` guarded by a synchronous `pending` ref (a double click cannot get two requests past it), sends a FIXED idempotency key `initialize-native-account` (a retry, a second tab or a reload that races the first is the same request and is deduplicated server-side), and the server still checks the owner binding and the accepted model. Nothing about the server's idempotency, authorization or owner binding was changed.
- **BEFORE → INITIALIZE → AFTER**, new DB regression tests (`nativeDemo.integration.test.ts`, run against a migrations-built database): before, `demoAvailable: '10000000'` with `account: null` and the wallet row intact; after, the row is `0`, `settleBalance` 10 000 000, `walletCollateral` `0`, `collateral` 10 000 000 — counted once, not twice — and the ledger reconciles. A fresh process then reads state and initializes again: revision stays 1 and the wallet row stays 0. A second test adds 2.5 BTC and proves the non-settle holding is valued at mark, added once, and MOVES with the price (125 000 → 150 000 at 50k → 60k) while the ledger side does not. A third proves a wallet with no demo funds is offered nothing.
- **The chart-tools strip.** `ChartTradingToggle` — a bordered bar above the chart in every session — is deleted. A double click on the chart opens `ChartTradingMenu` at the pointer: a compact non-native menu with the switch, `Выбрать вход на графике` (which turns the switch on with it, so the only action in the menu is never gated by a checkbox above it), and, while the picker is armed, the current state plus a cancel. It closes on `×`, Esc, an outside click (capture phase, so a dismissing click never also draws), after an action, on symbol change and on unmount. It is clamped into the viewport from a measured box. The double click opens a menu and does nothing else: no drawing, no zoom, no pick, no order — asserted in `nativeFuturesTerminal.test.ts`.
- **While the picker is armed, the chart keeps the pointer.** A double click's FIRST click already reaches the chart and picks, so opening the menu on the second would steal the gesture and report a state that had just changed. The double click is suppressed in that state and the compact trigger appears instead — for exactly as long as the picker is armed, in the chart's own corner, never a bar. The same trigger is the touch entry, shown by `(pointer: coarse)`.
- **Verified.** Backend `tsc` PASS · frontend `tsc` PASS · Vite build PASS. New `futuresDemoActivation` suite 9/9; `nativeFuturesTerminal` 25/25 including three new composition tests; `nativeDemo.integration` **8/8 against real PostgreSQL** with `PRIVATE_TRADING_DB_TESTS=1`. Targeted suites (`frontend/src/lib/__tests__`, `src/private-trading`): 19 failing suites on this branch and 19 on pristine `main`, identical lists, **zero new failures** — the two that did appear (`futuresAccountUnknownState`, `futuresReferenceRow`) were real defects of mine and are fixed, and `futuresUiPolish`'s Futures-scoping guard caught the unscoped menu CSS, now scoped. New browser QA `scripts/qa-futures-demo-activation.cjs`: **84/84 at 1440×1000 and 390×844** — BEFORE figures, one-press-from-a-double-press, AFTER figures matched to the server to the cent, the collateral identity, reload, the strip's absence with no empty band, single click inert, double click opens, Esc/outside/×/action/symbol-change close, picker armed and cancelled, touch entry, no order placed, and the kept no-real-account-requests assertion. The existing `scripts/qa-native-demo-browser.cjs` was migrated to the menu and passes **26/26** at both widths.
- **Render/Wallet.** No new polling and no new endpoint. Wallet was touched only in as much as the demo balance now reaches Futures honestly; no Wallet redesign, and Banking, Copy Trading, Admin, Card, CFD and Spot business logic are untouched.
- **NOT verified.** No live Bybit provider run: the review stand runs with `NATIVE_PREVIEW_FIXTURE=1` because this sandbox cannot reach `api.bybit.com`, so provider freshness and real marks are unproven here. With the fixture off, the stand's collateral valuation correctly reported `collateralComplete: false`, `unpricedAssets: ['BTC']` and `liquidatable: null` rather than valuing BTC at zero — the honest-unpriced path, verified as a side effect. Production login and first-run initialization against the real database were not exercised in a browser; the DB-level flow was.
- Not merged to `main`, not deployed to production.

## 2026-09-16 — Claude — phase 7: the Wallet becomes the Unified Trading Account (`claude/wallet-unified-account`)

- **Branch.** New and clean, cut from `main` @ `2665c40` (the PR #94 merge). Verified by ancestry (`git merge-base --is-ancestor` for both `4092c01` and `2665c40`) AND by content: `src/api/routes/marketDepth.ts` present (#93), `ChartTradingMenu.tsx` present and `ChartTradingToggle.tsx` gone (#94).
- **The second set of books, found and removed.** The Wallet's owner total did not come from any ledger. `src/services/AdminPortfolioProfile.ts` held a HARDCODED holdings list (271 BTC, 561 ETH, 1 200 000 XRP, 32 726 245 USDT, 1 200 000 USDC, 700 000 EUR) selected by email address, plus a generated equity curve with hardcoded anchor returns (+28% / +132% / +317% / +1926%). Valued live it produced the ~$58.45M the owner saw. Meanwhile `/futures` read the owner's REAL `DemoBalance` rows through the native Cross account model. Two independent accounting systems for one account, guaranteed to disagree the moment anything traded — and after initialization moved the settle row into the simulation ledger, the Wallet still listed that USDT as a spot holding. **The whole file and its test are deleted.** No balance is selected by identity anywhere in the app any more; `walletUnifiedAccount.test.ts` scans every `.ts`/`.tsx`/`.js` under `src/`, `frontend/src/` and the Tailwind config for the total, the quantities and the profile helpers, with comments stripped so a worked example in prose is not mistaken for a balance.
- **One authoritative account, asked for rather than derived.** New `NativeDemoService.wallet()` + `GET /private-trading/native/wallet` return the SAME `crossAccount()` object the terminal reads, the collateral valuation it was built from, and a per-asset row projection — all from ONE valuation pass (`authoritative()` now accepts a precomputed valuation, so the two cannot be taken at different prices). New `src/private-trading/native/walletRows.ts` is that projection and invents no rule: `total = wallet row + (settle asset only) trading balance`, `inUse = row locked + (settle only) initial margin + order reserve`, `available = total − inUse` floored at 0, `value = total × price` or `null`. Adding the wallet row to the trading balance is safe **because initialization debits the row it credits** — proven both directions in `unifiedWallet.test.ts` (same 5 000 000 before and after opening; if the debit were ever dropped the test reads 10 000 000).
- **Wallet ↔ Futures consistency.** `useWalletData` now returns one `UnifiedAccount`. For the owner every field is the server's: equity, available, unrealized PnL, initial margin, maintenance margin, order reserve, `collateralComplete`, `unpricedAssets`. There is no arithmetic on them — a guard test asserts no `a.x ± a.y` and no BigNumber call in that branch. For everyone else it is `/wallet/overview`'s valued ledger, with the margin fields **null (unknown), never 0**. The two branches are exclusive, so a Cross equity that already counts the whole wallet can never have a ledger subtotal added to it. `WalletFuturesAccountCard` is deleted: the header IS that card now, and a second panel of the same figures is a second chance to disagree.
- **Unknown is still not zero, and now says so.** `/wallet/overview` silently skipped an unpriced holding from its totals; it now also returns `valuationComplete` and `unpricedAssets`, so the interface can state that the total is a FLOOR — in the terminal's own sentence (`futures.collateralIncomplete`), deliberately not a second wording. The asset row shows `—` plus `Нет котировки` so a missing price cannot read as an empty balance, and the allocation ring excludes the asset and names it rather than drawing a zero slice.
- **Bybit-like, in VOLTEX's colours.** The workspace is now dark and terminal-compatible. Every wallet-only Tailwind token (`base`, `surface`, `panel`, `hair`, `pos`, `neg`, `ink DEFAULT/2/3/4`, `gold DEFAULT/light/deep/wash`, the three shadows) points at a CSS variable **with the previous light value as its fallback**, and the dark palette is one block in `wallet-v3/wallet.css` scoped to `.vx-wallet`. Nothing outside the Wallet can shift, because outside it the variables are not defined. The header is a compact account overview with a mode chip; the ledger gained an `В использовании` column (committed quantity is a fact on a margin account, not a footnote) and keeps search, hide-zero, sorting, icons and the mobile layout; Deposit/Withdraw/Transfer use the existing real flows, History scrolls to the existing section, and **Convert is rendered disabled with a reason** rather than wired to a flow that does not exist. No Bybit asset or logo was copied.
- **Request/load impact: no new recurring request.** The authoritative account is read ONCE per page load, plus on the existing refresh triggers and when the tab regains focus. It is deliberately NOT on the 8s balance poll, because its valuation costs one upstream mark per held asset. It REPLACES the one `/native/account` request the deleted card made, and carries the rows that would otherwise have needed a second call. Measured in the browser: 1 `native/wallet` and 1 `wallet/overview` per load, at both viewports, for both kinds of account; 40 rows still cost one request.
- **Verified.** Backend `tsc` PASS · frontend `tsc` PASS · Vite production build PASS. New `unifiedWallet` suite 8/8; new `walletUnifiedAccount` suite 12/12; `futuresSingleMoneySource` 13/13 (re-pointed from the deleted card to the hook and header, same assertions); `WalletPortfolioService` 24/24; `walletPortfolio` routes 6/6. **Full suite: 91 failed / 3782 passed on this branch and 91 failed / 3788 passed on pristine `main` @ 2665c40 — the 26 failing-suite lists are byte-identical, so zero new failures.** `walletUxRefinement`'s two remaining failures (`DepositModal.tsx`, `frontend/src/lib/api.ts` fingerprints) fail on pristine `main` too and are left untouched, as the suite's own note instructs. Its `useWalletData.ts` and `WalletPortfolioService.ts` fingerprints were deliberately re-taken with a written rationale, and its behavioural assertions were updated rather than relaxed — the column list is still exact, the masking still exhaustive, and its `t()` stub now interpolates so a sentence naming an asset is actually checked for that asset.
- **Browser QA: 70/70 at 1440×1000 and 390×844** (`scripts/qa-wallet-unified-account.cjs` against the real production bundle on `scripts/serve-wallet-review.cjs`) — dark surface, formatted total with no trace of the old observation, Cross vs Spot header and the metrics each one may show, the seven-column ledger with tabular numerals, the mobile ledger, search, hide-zero, no horizontal overflow, Deposit reachable, Convert disabled, the request census, the owner BEFORE the margin account exists (falls back to the ordinary ledger, invents no margin figures), and the unpriced case for both account kinds (labelled row, no `$0.00`, a note naming the asset and calling the total a lower bound). Screenshots under `/tmp/qa-wallet/`.

Banking, Copy Trading, Admin, Card, CFD and Spot business logic are untouched, as is Russian hosting.

## 2026-09-16 — Claude — phase 7b: the equity curve, restored on real history (`claude/wallet-unified-account`, PR #95)

- **What I got wrong in 7a.** Deleting `AdminPortfolioProfile` removed the generated curve that was the ONLY thing ever feeding the Wallet's performance block for the owner. The owner was right to reject it.

The cramped sparkline and its `Performance`/`Sparkline` helpers are gone from `PortfolioStrip`; an equity curve is a section, not an ornament beside the balance.
- **Fed only by data that already existed.** `/wallet/performance` → `PortfolioSnapshot` rows → `buildAdjustedSeries` (that day's deposits and withdrawals removed before the day counts as performance) → `computeAllPeriods`. Every plotted vertex is one stored day. The chart fetches nothing itself, and a guard test asserts the file contains no `fetch`/`api.`, no `Math.random`, and no `Math.exp`/`log`/`sin` — the shaping functions the deleted generator used.
- **Insufficient history keeps the card.** Two daily snapshots are needed before any return exists. Until then the plot frame, the period tabs and the card all stay, with an explanation INSIDE the plot area and no line drawn through points that do not exist. A window the series cannot cover is a disabled tab that says why, and the card opens on the WIDEST window the account can actually answer, so a young account is never parked on an empty 7D while its all-time curve exists.
- **Nothing hardcoded came back.** No holdings, no `ANCHORS`, no `futureStep`/`historicalLog`/`adminEquityIndex`/`adminPerformanceSeries`, no `PROFILE_*` day. A new test scans every source file for the five anchor multiples (1.28 / 2.32 / 4.17 / 20.26 / 22.15) and those six identifiers and fails on any hit. The authoritative balance/equity source is untouched: the header still reads the native `CrossAccount`, the chart reads the stored series, and neither feeds the other.
- **One real defect found on the way.** `registerWalletTailwindOwnership` went red: its `className={...}` parser reads quoted segments, and my multi-line template literal made it take the bare identifier `usable` for a utility class. Fixed at the source by hoisting the tab's three states into named constants (as `PortfolioStrip` already does) rather than by loosening the audit.
- **Verified.** Backend `tsc` PASS · frontend `tsc` PASS · Vite build PASS. `walletUnifiedAccount` **19/19** (7 of them new, covering the curve); `walletUxRefinement` unchanged at its two pre-existing failures; `registerWalletTailwindOwnership` 11/11. **Full suite: 91 failed / 3789 passed, against 91 failed / 3788 passed on pristine `main` @ 2665c40 — identical 26-suite failing list, zero new failures.** Browser QA **108/108** at 1440×1000 and 390×844: the chart present with >10 real vertices, >220px tall, real dates on the axis, real history age, disabled windows for periods the series cannot cover — and, at both viewports, a zero-history account keeping its card, plot frame, tabs and in-plot explanation with no line drawn. The review stand now serves the series through the REAL `PortfolioPerformanceEngine` from stored-snapshot-shaped rows including a deposit day, so flow removal is exercised rather than described.
- Not merged to `main`, not deployed to production.

No balance is hardcoded, no figure is recomputed in React, and the Wallet↔Futures single-source property and its double-counting proof are exactly as they were.

The reference prints `IM 0.05%` beside `MM 0.00%`, and an interface that divided `initialMargin` by `equity` itself would be the beginning of a second accounting system. Both ratios are `null` when equity is not positive, so an account with nothing in it prints `—` rather than a fabricated `0%`.

## 2026-09-16 — Claude — phase 8: Isolated margin becomes real, and the order panel calculates live (`claude/futures-margin-mode-order-panel`)

- **Branch.** Cut from `main` @ `5942a3d` (the PR #95 merge), verified by ancestry AND content: `walletRows.ts` and `WalletSideNav.tsx` present, `AdminPortfolioProfile.ts` gone. The owner's gate — "confirm #95 is in main, otherwise STOP" — was run first and initially FAILED (main was still `2665c40`); work began only after #95 merged at 15:47 UTC and the gate was re-run.
- **The audit's finding, and why the switch was locked.** The REAL futures engine (`src/futures/`) has always supported both modes — `MarginType = 'ISOLATED' | 'CROSS'`, per-bucket positions, a liquidation branch. The NATIVE engine had not: one shared `walletBalance`, one account-wide equity/IM/MM in `demoAccount()`, a liquidation sweep that closed EVERY open position at once, `marginMode: 'CROSS'` as a literal on the position view, and no `marginType` field on a position or an order at all. `useNativeFuturesExecution` pinned `marginType: 'CROSS'`, which was honest given that engine. So the engine was changed first and the switch unlocked second — per the owner's own rule, "do not enable Isolated in UI until all of that is real".
- **What Isolated now IS.** `DemoOrder`/`DemoPosition` carry `marginType`; an isolated position carries `isolatedMargin`, the margin MOVED OUT of `walletBalance` at fill and returned pro rata at close. `positionRisk()` selects the tier on the notional of the position's OWN bucket. `demoAccount()` splits: isolated margin, P&L and maintenance are reported separately and are absent from cross equity, `usedMargin`, `maintenanceMargin` and `liquidatable`. Each isolated position is liquidated alone; the account sweep takes only CROSS positions and CROSS orders. Funding on an isolated position moves its own post. A cross order cannot reduce an isolated position.
- **The bound that makes it true.** An isolated liquidation settles at the position's BANKRUPTCY price when the observed mark is beyond it. Without that, a mark gapping 50 000 → 30 000 billed the shared account **1501.65 against a 500 post** — the ring fence leaking, just later. With it the account pays only the closing fee (2.475 measured), and the post absorbs the loss. This is pinned by test.
- **State migration.** `DemoState` version 2, with `migrateDemoState()` applied at the repository read boundary so every stored snapshot AND every replay checkpoint is read forward in one place. A version-1 row is Cross with nothing posted — a fact about those rows, not a default — and reports the identical account it reported before (asserted).
- **Wallet consistency.** `crossAccount()` adds the posted margin back into `settleBalance` and counts it in `initialMargin`: it left the cross pool but it is still the owner's money, so ring-fencing a position does not shrink the Wallet total (asserted against a matching cross account). `available` excludes isolated P&L, which is not collateral for a new position until the ring-fenced one closes. No second balance calculation was added: the order panel still sizes from `nativeBalances()`, which projects the one authoritative `CrossAccount`.
- **The live block was already live** — `notional`, `requiredMargin`, `liqPreviewLong/Short` recompute on every keystroke, and `previewLiquidationPrice` already branched on margin type. What changed is the labels (`Стоимость позиции`, the reference's wording) and the shortfall sentence (`Вам не хватает средств…`, 7 locales), plus the three defects below.
- **Three real defects found by the browser QA and fixed at the source.** (a) The Cross liquidation preview printed a NEGATIVE price — `-100 948 106.00` — because a large balance behind a small order pushes the backstop term through zero; it now returns `null` for any result at or below zero and the panel shows a dash, which is what the server's own estimator already answered. (b) The shortfall message printed a literal `{asset}`: the i18n interpolator used `String.replace` with a string pattern, which substitutes only the FIRST occurrence, so any sentence naming one parameter twice lost the second — it now splits and joins, which also stops a value containing `$&` from being read as a replacement pattern. (c) `place()` read `config.highLeverageWarningThreshold` bare, and the field is nullable: `leverage >= null` is `leverage >= 0`, so EVERY order raised the high-leverage confirm, and a trader who dismissed it had the order silently dropped with no error. Two sibling call sites already guarded it (`?? Infinity`, `?? 20`); this one did not. The review stand was also serving `leverageTiers: []`, which silently disabled every tier-dependent control including the preview; it now projects the instrument's own risk tiers.
- **Verified.** Backend `tsc` PASS · frontend `tsc` PASS · Vite production build PASS. New `isolatedMargin` suite **17/17**; `nativeFuturesTerminal` 27/27 (two new cases: an isolated projection, an isolated draft); `futuresSingleMoneySource` 13/13 with its `liquidatable` pin re-taken on the new expression plus the settle-balance line; `nativeDemoEngine`/`nativeDemoReplay`/`crossAccount`/`unifiedWallet` unchanged and green — the Cross path is the engine it was. **Full suite: 91 failed / 3814 passed on this branch against 91 failed / 3795 passed on `main` @ 5942a3d with a matching fresh build — identical 26-suite failing list, zero new failures**, +19 passing from the new tests. (The baseline needed its own `npm run build` first: `registerWalletTailwindOwnership` audits BUILT CSS and fails on a stale `dist/`, which would otherwise have read as a suite my branch "fixed".)
- **Browser QA: 68/68 at 1440×1000 and 390×844** (`scripts/qa-futures-margin-mode.cjs`, new, against the real Futures page and the real engine). Covers: the mode offered rather than pinned; both modes present; position value = quantity × price; margin = value / leverage; value, margin and liquidation all moving live on quantity, price and leverage; leverage moving margin but NOT position value; isolated quoting a reachable long liquidation (45 250.00) where cross has none; all four combinations — Cross/Isolated × Limit/Market × Long/Short — **actually submitted** and reaching the engine with the right `marginType`; the engine storing both buckets; the isolated position carrying its own posted margin (49.8454) and the `ISOLATED_POSITION_ESTIMATE` basis; the account reporting isolated margin as in use; **a reload still showing `Кросс` and `Изолированная` on their own rows**; the percentage presets sizing monotonically and stopping at the CONTRACT's maximum rather than the budget; a sub-minimum quantity refused by its own rule and NOT as a funds message; a shortfall named as a shortfall with both figures and no unfilled placeholder; and an unfilled order showing dashes, never 0.00.
- **NOT verified.** (a) No live Bybit run — the stand uses a deterministic fixture market because this sandbox cannot reach `api.bybit.com`. (b) The HISTORICAL chart-entry flow was not exercised in the browser: the panel is shared and the draft carries the bar's identity rather than a price (unit-tested), but picking a candle and submitting from it was not driven here. (c) Reduce-only was not driven in the browser; its bucket rule is covered by an engine test. (d) Wallet↔Futures agreement after an open AND a close is covered by engine and account-model tests, not by a browser round trip across both pages. (e) Production login and a real owner session were not exercised.
- **Preserved.** PR #93's order book and depth work, PR #94's demo activation and chart menu, and PR #95's Wallet are untouched. The real `src/futures/` engine was audited and NOT modified — it already supported both modes. Banking, Copy Trading, Admin, Card, CFD and Spot are untouched, as is Russian hosting.
- **Branch note.** `AGENTS.md` nominates `integration/claude-codex` as the shared branch; the owner explicitly instructed "start a fresh branch from the current `main`", which that file's own rule allows ("unless the owner explicitly asks"), and which matches how #93/#94/#95 were run.
- Not merged to `main`, not deployed to production.

### 2026-09-16 — Claude — phase 8b: driving PR #100's `native` CI job to green (`claude/futures-margin-mode-order-panel`)

- **Why.** The `native` job on PR #100 was red: `scripts/qa-native-demo-browser.cjs` reported `5 QA checks failed`. Reproduced locally, then root-caused rather than re-run. Four distinct causes, all fixed at the source; no assertion was relaxed and no check was skipped.
- **(a) The panel defaulted to the wrong bucket — and the fix I first wrote was wrong too.** `account-summary-*` failed with `Owner margin mode is not Cross: "Тип маржи\nИзолированная"`. The native account is ONE unified pool whose Wallet header reads `Кросс-маржа`, so opening on Isolated misdescribed it. My first fix set the form's `useState` default to `'CROSS'` — which also changed the default of the **real** Futures engine, whose payload has always been `ISOLATED`. `futuresOrderPanel` and `futuresAccountUnknownState` caught that (`marginType: "ISOLATED"` → `"CROSS"`, byte-for-byte pins), and they were right. Corrected properly: `FuturesExecution` gains `defaultMarginType`, distinct from `marginType` (which PINS the mode and removes the choice). `REAL_FUTURES_EXECUTION` declares `'ISOLATED'` — its payload is unchanged, re-asserted by those same pins — and the native seam declares `'CROSS'`. The form's own state is now `'ISOLATED' | 'CROSS' | null`, where `null` means "the trader has not chosen": it is NOT seeded from the engine, because the engine binding resolves a render late and a seeded value would latch whichever engine happened to be bound first. Both test mocks hand-roll the seam and were given the field they now claim to model.
- **(b) A reduce-only order took its bucket from the form, not from the position.** `normal-owner-flow-*` failed with `CLOSE: 409 MARGIN_TYPE_MISMATCH`. A trader closing an isolated position while the panel happens to show Cross is closing THAT position; the engine rightly refuses an order that crosses buckets. `useNativeFuturesExecution.placeOrder` now resolves the position being reduced and carries ITS `marginMode` (the field is `marginMode` on `NativePosition`, not `marginType` — a `TS2339` caught this).
- **(c) A real race in the chart's double-click gesture.** `chart-tool-selection-390` failed in CI. Reproduced it in isolation, then ran the SAME scripted flow against a pristine `main` @ `cf37d87` built with the harness's own observation shim: main passed 4/4, this branch failed, and instrumenting the probe made the branch pass — the signature of a race, not a deterministic break. Cause: the handler read `native.interaction.selecting` from its render closure, but the gesture's own FIRST click picks a bar and sets `selecting` to `null`; whether the second click saw the old or new value depended on whether React had re-rendered in between. Under load it had not, and the gesture silently did nothing. The guard now reads `pickingAtGestureStart`, a ref recorded on the first press in the capture phase — so a double click is judged by the state the GESTURE started in, which is what the handler's own comment always claimed. Pinned by a new `nativeFuturesTerminal` case that also forbids reverting to the live read.
- **(d) The QA harness armed a tool it only meant to switch on.** `setChartTools(p, true)` closed the menu by clicking its primary action — which is "Выбрать вход на графике", and that ARMS the picker. Callers wanting only the switch were leaving a picker armed and then racing its first click. Split: `setChartTools` now uses Esc in both directions, and a new `armChartPicker()` does the arming for the one flow that wants it. `chartFlow`'s own assertions are unchanged.
- **Verified.** Frontend `tsc` PASS · Vite production build PASS · `scripts/qa-native-demo-browser.cjs` **26/26 at 1440×1000 and 390×844, zero browser runtime errors** (was 21/26). `futuresOrderPanel` + `futuresAccountUnknownState` 96/96; `nativeFuturesTerminal` 28/28 with the new gesture case. **Full suite: 26 failed / 3807 passed on this branch against 27 failed / 3794 passed on a freshly-built `main` @ `cf37d87` — no suite fails here that does not also fail on main.** `registerWalletTailwindOwnership` fails on that main baseline and passes here; this branch touches nothing in Wallet, so it is recorded as an artifact of the baseline worktree's build and explicitly NOT claimed as a fix.
- **NOT verified.** Everything listed as unverified in phase 8 still is — no live Bybit run, no production login, and the historical chart-entry submit and reduce-only paths are still covered by unit/engine tests rather than driven in a browser. CI on PR #100 had not re-run at the time of writing.
- **Preserved.** No change to the native engine, the account model, the store, the real `src/futures/` engine, Wallet, Copy Trading, Admin, Card, CFD or Spot. The real engine's order payload is byte-for-byte what it was.
- Not merged to `main`, not deployed to production.

### 2026-09-16 — Claude — phase 8c: reconciling with Codex on the same branch (`claude/futures-margin-mode-order-panel`)

- **What happened.** While phase 8b was being verified, Codex pushed three commits to this same branch (`c8f4ead`, `d08c35f`, `2fb3d0a`). The push was rejected; the remote was merged rather than overwritten, and reconciled by intent per `CLAUDE.md` — no `ours`/`theirs`, no force-push.
- **KEPT — `c8f4ead`, a real engine bug I had missed.** Replaying a market CLOSE against a book rebuilds the reduce-only order from the position, and that reconstruction carried everything EXCEPT `marginType`. It therefore defaulted to Cross, and the engine correctly refused to let a cross order reduce an isolated position — so replaying an ordinary close of an isolated position threw, which for a deterministic engine means the account could not be rebuilt at all. Codex's one-line fix (`marginType:p.marginType`) is correct and is kept. It arrived without a test, so two were added to `isolatedMargin.test.ts`: one drives the exact reconstruction replay performs and asserts the position closes and the post returns; the other asserts the SAME rebuild without the bucket still throws, so the fix cannot be silently undone. (Its trailing-newline removal on `replay.ts` was restored.)
- **DROPPED — `d08c35f` + `2fb3d0a`, the compatibility runner.** `scripts/qa-native-demo-browser-current.cjs` copied the QA harness at runtime and rewrote two things before executing it: it widened `Owner margin mode is not Cross` to accept Isolated as well, and it made `openChartMenu` click the compact `.chart-tools-trigger` instead of performing the double click. `.github/workflows/native-demo-qa.yml` was pointed at it. Those are the two checks phase 8b diagnosed, and both were real defects, not stale expectations: the panel genuinely opened on the wrong bucket over a unified Cross account, and the double-click guard genuinely raced its own first click. With 8b's fixes the original assertions pass AS WRITTEN — 26/26 at both viewports — so the runner would only have suppressed the gesture it is there to test. The script is removed and the workflow points back at `scripts/qa-native-demo-browser.cjs`. The intent Codex was serving (a green `native` job that does not block the margin-mode feature) is met; the method is not needed.
- **Note on the touch rationale.** The runner's comment reasons that touch viewports should use the compact trigger. That is true of a real touch device, and the product already does it — but the QA context at 390px is created without `hasTouch`, so the double click IS the gesture under test there and it now works deterministically.
- **Verified after the merge.** Backend `tsc` PASS · frontend `tsc` PASS · Vite production build PASS · `qa-native-demo-browser.cjs` **26/26 at 1440×1000 and 390×844, zero runtime errors** · `isolatedMargin` **19/19** (17 + the 2 new replay cases). **Full suite: 26 failed / 3809 passed against 27 failed / 3794 passed on a freshly-built `main` @ `cf37d87` — no suite fails here that does not also fail on main.**
- **NOT verified.** CI on PR #100 had not re-run at the time of writing. Everything listed as unverified in phases 8 and 8b still is.
- Not merged to `main`, not deployed to production.

## 2026-09-17 — Claude — Ksenia ROI 7D: what was actually wrong (`claude/ksenia-roi-7d-and-futures-layout`)

- **Branch.** Fresh-fetched and cut from `main` @ `21ce8df0` (the PR #106 merge), the SHA named in the brief.
- **FINDING 1 — on 2026-09-17 the logic is already correct, and the test proves it.** Driving the real canonical model (`advanceKseniaReview` → `kseniaReviewResponse` → `summarizeStrategy` → `withKseniaReportedTrade`) to `simulatedAt = 2026-09-17`, with a genuine canonical trade from the 17th already present: `economics.periods['7D'].roi` carries the reported **+7.8pp** exactly, `analytics.roi7` matches it, 7D `masterPnl` carries **+1,754**, and the 7D trade and win counts each gain one. `validStrategy` passes. Applying the overlay twice is a no-op, so it is not double-counted. **If production does not show this, the cause is the data or the deploy, not this code.**
- **FINDING 2 — a real bug, one week later, that breaks the whole card.** Measured across `simulatedAt` 2026-09-17…2026-09-28: from **2026-09-24** `validStrategy` returned **false** on every date, which drops Ksenia's section wholesale — card and profile fall back to skeleton / "Данные недоступны", taking ROI 7D with it. Cause: the strategy closes ~2 trades a day, so about a week after the 16th the reported row is no longer one of the ten `VISIBLE_TRADES` rows. The server still deletes `holdingTimeTotalMinutes` for every period that **still counts** that trade (its duration was never supplied), but the client accepted a missing aggregate **only while the row was visible**. The two drift apart and the payload becomes invalid.
- **FIX.** `validStrategy` now accepts the missing holding-time aggregate when the payload **declares** the owner-reported trade via `reportedPerformance`, which survives the ten-row slice — not merely when the row is still on screen. Being counted and being displayed are different facts. The declaration is validated strictly: id, source, market, side, leverage, `netPnl` 1754, `returnPct` 7.8 and both dates are pinned, and every unsupplied field must be an explicit `null`, so this cannot be used to smuggle a payload that is simply missing fields.
- **TESTED.** Three new regression tests in `kseniaReportedTrade.test.ts`, all failing-then-passing where they should: (a) the 17 Sep ROI 7D contract — passes on main too, which is the point; (b) the section stays valid at 2026-09-24 once the row is evicted — **fails on main, passes with the fix**; (c) a payload that merely omits the aggregate, or tampers with the declaration, is still rejected — **fails on main, passes with the fix**. Suite 5/5. Backend `tsc` PASS, frontend `tsc` PASS.
- **No regressions.** Failing-test list across `src/services/copyTrading` and `frontend/src/lib/__tests__/copy*`, with and without this change: **identical**. Five tests fail on `main` already, all in the Copy Trading first-load area (task 2 of the brief), and none of them are touched here.
- **NOT VERIFIED.** Whether the Render backend is serving a build that contains #106 — this sandbox cannot reach that deployment. Nothing here changes the production symptom if the running backend predates #106; that has to be checked on Render itself.
- Not merged to `main`, not deployed to production.

### 2026-09-17 — Claude — making the backend deploy identifiable (same branch)

- **Why.** The brief asks whether Render is really serving a build that contains #106. That could not be answered from anywhere: `/health` returned `{status:'ok'}` and nothing else, and the frontend (Cloudflare Pages) and the API (Render) deploy independently — so "the site updated" is no evidence about the API.
- **What.** `/health` now also returns `commit`, `branch` and `startedAt`. `status` is unchanged for anything already polling it. `resolveBuildCommit()` reads `RENDER_GIT_COMMIT`, then `GIT_COMMIT`, `SOURCE_VERSION`, `VERCEL_GIT_COMMIT_SHA`, and returns **`null`** when none is set or the variable is exported empty — an admitted unknown, never a guess, because a wrong SHA here would be believed.
- **TESTED.** `src/__tests__/buildCommit.test.ts`, 3/3: precedence, null-not-guess (including empty and whitespace), and trimming. Backend `tsc` PASS.
- **How to use it.** `curl https://<render-host>/health` and compare `commit` against the SHA you expect. If it predates #106, the Ksenia symptom is a stale backend and no frontend deploy will change it.

### 2026-09-17 — Claude — Copy Trading first load: last confirmed data instead of a skeleton (same branch)

- **What was already there.** The store keeps a per-section last-good value **in memory** and already refuses to clear one section because another failed — `next` starts from the current state, so an invalid section keeps its previous value and `fetchedAt`. Session partitioning already existed too. What did not exist was any persistence: a hard reload started from `empty()`, so the first paint had nothing and the page drew skeletons over figures the browser had already been told were true.
- **What was added.** `copyMarketplaceCache.ts` writes the last CONFIRMED snapshot to `localStorage` and reads it back on the next visit. Three rules make showing it honest: only sections that already passed the live validator are written; everything is **re-validated through those same validators** on read, so a snapshot from an older build or a truncated write is dropped rather than trusted for being ours; and the key carries an FNV-1a digest of the session token, so one login can never read another's snapshot. The token itself is never written — only the digest, and it sits beside a token the app already keeps in the same storage, so it adds no exposure that was not already there.
- **Honest about what it is.** A hydrated state keeps `settled: false`: the figures are real and previously confirmed, but this visit has not heard from the server yet and the UI is entitled to know a live answer is coming. `getState()`'s existing rule still marks a section stale when its `simulatedAt` is older than today. Logging out clears that session's snapshot, so nothing is readable on a shared machine. Storage that throws or is absent degrades to today's behaviour rather than breaking the page. **No hardcoded ROI/PnL, no fabricated fallback metrics, and no extra polling** — the same single bootstrap request runs.
- **TESTED.** `copyMarketplaceCache.test.ts`, **6/6**, driven with a REAL Ksenia section off the production path (`kseniaReviewResponse → summarizeStrategy → withKseniaReportedTrade`): restores before any response arrives; never shows one login another's snapshot; drops a snapshot that stops validating; a failing section does not clear the one cached beside it; the snapshot is forgotten at logout; and it still works where the browser refuses storage. Frontend `tsc` PASS, production build PASS.
- **No regressions.** Failing-test list across `src/services/copyTrading` and `frontend/src/lib/__tests__/copy*` is **identical to the `main` baseline** — no suite broken.
- **NOT DONE for this task.** The five tests that already fail on `main` in `copyFirstLoad.test.ts` are **not** addressed here. They encode the OLD intent — one asserts the profile shows **more than ten** `[data-unavailable]` placeholders on first paint, which is exactly the skeleton this task exists to remove. Reconciling them is a deliberate rewrite of their intent, not a tweak, and it has not been done. They fail on `main` for their own reasons already.
- **NOT VERIFIED.** Hard reload, navigate-away-and-back and opening Ksenia's profile were not driven in a real browser; the guarantees above are covered at the store boundary by unit tests.

### 2026-09-17 — Claude — "ROI 7D dropped from 51% to 41.9%" is the window rolling, not a lost trade (same branch)

- **Reported.** "ROI · 7D is +41.9% today but was ~51% yesterday, even with yesterday's trade and today's already counted."
- **Measured, and it reproduces the owner's two figures exactly.** On the canonical model with the overlay applied: `simulatedAt = 2026-09-16` → ROI 7D **50.9208%**; `simulatedAt = 2026-09-17` → **41.9214%**.
- **The whole of the drop is the window rolling forward one day.** On the 16th the window was 09-10…09-16 and contained **2026-09-10 at +11.343%**. On the 17th it is 09-11…09-17, so the 10th **aged out** and **2026-09-17 at +2.344%** came in. −11.343 + 2.344 = **−8.999pp**, and 50.9208 − 8.999 ≈ **41.92**. It matches to the 4-decimal rounding the published `roi` already carries.
- **The reported trade never left.** In the 17 Sep window, 2026-09-16 reads **+9.344%** — the canonical day plus the owner-reported **+7.8pp**, asserted against the un-overlaid model. It is counted, exactly as the earlier test proved.
- **NO CODE WAS CHANGED, deliberately.** Making the number stop falling would mean publishing a figure the data does not support, which the brief forbids. `ROI · 7D` is by definition the last seven days; a high day leaving the window is the metric working.
- **TESTED.** A sixth test in `kseniaReportedTrade.test.ts` pins this: both published figures, the first day of each window, the roll-off arithmetic, and that 16 Sep inside the 17 Sep window still carries +7.8pp over its canonical value. Suite **6/6**. It exists so the next person to see a ~9pp overnight drop finds the arithmetic written down instead of "fixing" it.
- **OPEN, for the owner to decide.** A large rolling-window drop with no explanation is indistinguishable from data loss to a reader. Making the window legible — naming the seven days the figure covers, or a one-line note — would remove the confusion without touching any number. Not done: it changes user-visible presentation and is the owner's call.

### 2026-09-17 — Claude — Homepage opens on its last-good six-hour market snapshot (same branch, PR #107)

- **Task.** Instant `/` open: the last successfully validated REAL homepage market snapshot is persisted in the browser and painted synchronously on the next visit, before any request; under six hours old it is used as-is with no market request; older it is painted, flagged stale and refreshed once in the background; a failed refresh never takes a confirmed value off the screen. Public homepage presentation data only — Trading/Futures/Wallet/execution/private feeds untouched (`git diff --name-only`: `frontend/src/pages/home/*`, home tests, one QA script, one workflow).
- **Material files.** NEW `frontend/src/pages/home/homeMarketSnapshot.ts` — one versioned record `voltex.home.market.v1` (`{version, savedAt, tickerUpdatedAt, tickerSource, tickers, priceHistory, hero:{pair,book,candles,trades,updatedAt}, rankings, rankingsUpdatedAt, global, fearGreed, globalUpdatedAt, cfd, cfdPriceHistory, cfdUpdatedAt, futuresSymbols, futuresUpdatedAt}`), `readHomeSnapshot` re-validates every section with the live rules (bad JSON / other version / missing `savedAt` → nothing; a ticker row without a finite price, a history series with a non-number, a hero for another pair, a candle with `low > min(open, close)` or out of order, a trade with an unknown side, a global figure that is not a number, a CFD row with wrong types → that section or part dropped on its own, never patched; every section needs its own finite observation time; caps on every array). `writeHomeSnapshot` serialises only committed real values — never `loading`/`error`/`refreshing`. REWRITTEN `frontend/src/pages/home/useHomeMarket.ts` — hydrates in `useState` initialisers (status `ok` for a restored section, `loading` otherwise; `tickersStale`/`hero.stale` from the observation age); the existing per-section `*StartedAt` gates are seeded from each section's `observedAt`, so a fresh section skips its request and a stale one issues it with no second code path; a `committed` mirror is persisted after every successful section; the hero is persisted only when book, candles and trades were all confirmed in the same refresh; a restored hero missing a part is re-requested now; `armExpiry()` arms one `setTimeout` for the moment the earliest not-yet-due section turns six hours old (never a zero delay — due-but-gated sections are left to the visibility/intersection handlers); rankings/global/futures-config bootstrap reads now follow the same six-hour gate and skip a hidden tab (they were one unconditional read per mount). `HOME_MARKET_REFRESH_MS = 6 * 60 * 60 * 1000` and `window.setInterval(refresh, HOME_MARKET_REFRESH_MS)` unchanged. NEW `scripts/qa-home-snapshot-reload.cjs` + a step in `.github/workflows/home-laptop-first-load.yml`.
- **Preserved from Codex.** The six-hour cadence, CSS-only laptop motion, no synthetic sparklines, the "6h snapshot"/"Stale" badge copy, Futures direct lazy route, Admin pagination, Ksenia ROI · 7D work — none touched. `homeLiveMarket.test.ts` keeps its 15 pre-existing tests; the one changed expectation is that rankings/global/config are now read again after six hours (2 calls, product behaviour), not 1.
- **Request reduction.** Before: 15 s polling = up to 240 refresh cycles per hour per open homepage. Now: with a snapshot under six hours old a mount issues **zero** market-data requests (tickers, order book, candles, trades, rankings, global, CFD, futures config); one refresh per source at expiry; then one per six hours. The only `/market/`-prefixed request a warm reload still makes is `/market/assets/icons?symbols=…` from the logo lookup — asset metadata, not a provider read; left as is on purpose (needed for logos across the app, analysed separately as the brief asked).

Frontend `tsc --noEmit` PASS, `npm run build` PASS (also with `VITE_API_URL=/api/v1`), backend `tsc --noEmit` PASS. ### 2026-09-17 — Claude — Final QA pass: Copy Trading last-good in a real browser; homepage capture after a reveal walk (same branch, PR #107)

- **Task.** Final QA before merge, no new product functionality: (1) drive the Copy Trading last-good cache through the full user flow in Chromium, as a browser regression, not only store tests; (2) make the homepage snapshot capture show the whole page by walking every `.vx-reveal` section before the full-page screenshot, without touching the production `Reveal`.

Evidence in `docs/qa/copy-last-good-browser/` and `docs/qa/home-snapshot-reload/`.
- **BUG found by the browser flow, fixed.** The PR body said "Logout clears the session snapshot", and the store does clear it — but only inside `checkSession()`, which runs when something asks the store for state. A logout is an in-app route change: the marketplace page has already unmounted when the token is cleared, nothing asks the store, and the logged-out session's snapshot stayed in `localStorage` (observed: session A's key present after logout). Fix: the store exposes `syncSession()` and `useCopyMarketplace.ts` registers it with the existing `onSessionChange` broadcast from `api.ts` (the same channel `futuresAccountStore` already uses). Not a second code path: it is the existing `checkSession` triggered at the right moment. The browser regression now asserts the logged-out session's key is gone immediately (`afterLogout: none → none`). No figure, validator or request behaviour changed.
- **Preserved.** Production `Reveal.tsx`/CSS untouched (the walk proved it works: 7/7 sections shown). All Codex work on the branch untouched. `HOME_MARKET_REFRESH_MS`, Trading/Futures/Wallet cadences untouched.
- **TESTED — Copy Trading browser regression** (`node scripts/qa-copy-last-good-browser.cjs`, 4 consecutive green runs after the final script fix): first authenticated visit paints both cards from the live response in ~2.4 s (Nazar ROI +873.5 %, 64 followers, AUM $7.2M, DD 1,81 %; Ksenia ROI +488.6 %, 48 followers, AUM $5.4M, DD 8,10 %, Win Rate 89,5 %, copiers' profit $5.1M, sparklines drawn, 0 placeholders); snapshot persisted under one key for session A (~395 KB, no token, no `refreshing`/`settled`); HARD RELOAD with the marketplace request held unanswered: both cards drawn at the FIRST DOM commit (t≈157 ms) with the identical figures and sparkline paths, 0 skeletons, no "Данные недоступны", status line "Загрузка данных…", no real → skeleton → real transition in the mutation timeline; Ksenia's profile opened before the API answered: chart drawn, hero Followers 48 / AUM 5 400 000 USDT / Max Drawdown 8,10 %, ROI · 90D +488.6 %, 90 daily bars — byte-identical to the live profile; navigate away (Crypto Card) and back in-app: both cards drawn again at one commit, no blank commit after, figures identical, request still held; 503 refresh: "Данные требуют обновления. Показаны последние загруженные значения.", every figure kept; malformed 200 refresh: same; another token in a fresh tab: cards all `—`, "Данные недоступны" button, no sparkline, no key until its own response; logout: token cleared, session A's key removed at once, a third token sees nothing of A, then paints from its own response. 0 page errors, 0 write requests to the QA server. ### 2026-09-18 — Claude — Proving the Wallet's margin and unrealized P&L are a live engine (same branch)

- **The doubt.** The owner read `IM 0,00% · MM 0,00% · Нереализованный PnL 0,00` as decoration and asked for "a working engine that really shows what I trade". Two things were true at once: the gauge had lost its CSS (fixed in this branch), and the account genuinely had no open positions, so the zeros were correct. Nothing in the suite proved the other half — that the figures MOVE — because the review stand could not open a position.
- **Why it could not.** Two stand limitations, neither of them production: `instrument()` threw `NOT_NEEDED`, so the engine could not size or margin an order; and `quoteFor` returned the same price as best bid and best ask, which `assertPrivateFreshQuote` rejects (`market_data_invalid`) because a crossed book is not a book. The stand now returns an ordinary linear-perpetual spec and a one-tick spread either side of the mark. **No production code was changed for this** — only the isolated review server.
- **NEW `scripts/qa-wallet-live-margin.cjs`, 15/15.** Flat → the zeros are correct and the header says which fact they are (`Нет открытых позиций`); OPEN a real 5× BTCUSDT LONG on 10 000 USDT through the REAL native engine → the Wallet header prints **the engine's own** `IM 10 027,50`, `MM 277,50`, `uPnL −5,00` (asserted equal to `/native/wallet`, not to a constant), both gauges fill, the idle note goes; CLOSE → the reading returns to flat. Evidence: `docs/qa/wallet-overview/unified-flat-margin.png` and `unified-live-margin.png`.
- **What this settles.** The Wallet is not drawing decoration: it reads the same `CrossAccount` object `/futures` prints, which `demoAccount()` builds by summing `positionRisk()` over OPEN positions at live marks. On production the figures are zero because there are no open positions — and the page now says so instead of leaving three bare zeros.

### 2026-09-18 — Claude — An admin credit is a flow, not performance (`claude/performance-manual-flows`)

- **The symptom.** Production showed `P&L · 7Д +450 864,86 %`. The account went from ~$6k to ~$31m in a day, and the whole jump was an Admin top-up.
- **The cause.** `WalletPortfolioService.realSeries` removed only CREDITED deposits and SENT withdrawals before measuring a day's return. Manual adjustments — `BalanceAdjustmentService` on the real ledger, `DemoTradingService.topUp` on the simulation one — moved the recorded total but were never removed, so the engine read "somebody put money in" as "this account earned money".
- **The fix.** `realSeries` now also reads the two audited actions (`BALANCE_ADJUSTED`, `DEMO_BALANCE_ADJUSTED`) and folds their `metadata.delta` into the day's net flow. The delta already carries its sign, so a debit needs no special case, and the engine's existing rule `r(t) = (value(t) − flow(t)) / value(t−1) − 1` does the rest: a day whose entire change was a credit returns 0 %.
- **WHICH LEDGER, and why it matters.** The recorded snapshot is the account's authoritative equity: the NATIVE simulation ledger for an account that has one (`NativeDemoAccount` exists), the real spot/futures ledgers for everyone else. They are separate books. A real deposit cannot move a native total and a demo top-up cannot move a real one, so each account now counts the flows of its own book only. Counting the other book's movement would not merely fail to help — it would subtract value the total never received and **invent a loss**. That is also why real deposits/withdrawals stopped counting for a native-ledger account; that was previously wrong in the same direction.
- **Defensive parsing.** `metadata` is untyped JSON. A row that does not yield a string asset and a finite, non-zero delta is SKIPPED, never guessed at, because a mis-parsed flow is subtracted from a real return.
- **Material files.** `src/services/WalletPortfolioService.ts` (+ module-level `ADJUSTMENT_ACTIONS` and `readAdjustment`). `PortfolioPerformanceEngine.ts` is **untouched** — the arithmetic was already right, it was being fed an incomplete flow. Its hash pin still matches; `WalletPortfolioService.ts`'s pin in `walletUxRefinement.test.ts` was re-taken with the reason recorded beside it.
- **TESTED.** `npx tsc --noEmit` PASS. `WalletPortfolioService.test.ts` **33/33** (9 new: a credit is not profit; a debit is not a loss; trading around a credit is still reported (+10 %); the ledger split proved in both directions, each account reading 0 % off its own book only; a same-day top-up recorded AFTER that day's snapshot lands on the next observation; a flow no stored snapshot contains yet is not subtracted at all; a real deposit is not subtracted from a native-ledger account; five unreadable audit shapes are skipped and the real +10 % stands; the query reads both actions for that user only). Full backend service suites were **301/303** before the timing rework, the 2 failures (`CfdMarketDataService`, `DepositService` minimum-deposit) **pre-existing on `main`** — verified in a worktree of `origin/main`; not re-run in full after the rework, which touches only this service.
- **NOT VERIFIED.** The production figure after deploy — the correction only takes effect for days that have snapshots, and the historical `+450 864 %` day will read 0 % once this ships, but the exact published number cannot be checked from this sandbox.
- **CORRECTED ON THE SHARED BRANCH (commits `e85c7dc`, `4823084`, `35d4cdd`, by the owner/Codex, reviewed and kept).** The first version assigned a flow to its UTC calendar day. That is wrong whenever the once-daily snapshot was taken BEFORE a later same-day top-up: the credit would be subtracted from an observation that never contained it, flattening the wrong day and letting the next day's jump read as profit. `realSeries` now assigns each flow to the FIRST SNAPSHOT AT OR AFTER its timestamp (binary search over the `createdAt asc` snapshot list), and a flow newer than the last stored snapshot is left alone until an observation exists that contains it — removed exactly once, never twice. Two regressions cover both cases, and the loosest assertion in my own ledger-split test (`<= 0`) was tightened to an exact 0 %. NEW `.github/workflows/wallet-performance.yml` runs the backend build and this suite on any PR touching the performance path.

### 2026-09-18 — Claude — Futures terminal: no dead rectangle, order panel and depth at the navbar, compact instrument row (`claude/futures-terminal-refinements`)

- **Source.** The owner's `VOLTEX_terminal_changes_bundle.zip` (two annotated screenshots + `README.txt` + `CODEX_PROMPT.txt`). Refinement of the existing terminal — no new design, no page rewrite, no change to orders, positions, PnL, margin, book data, chart data, wallet, leverage or TP/SL.
- **ROOT CAUSE of the "empty block above order entry".** Not a spacer in the markup — there is nothing there to delete. `.ticker-bar` was placed `grid-column:2 / 5`, spanning the order-entry column, while `.order-form-area` was pushed to `grid-row:2 / 4`. The eight instrument cells measure ~1067px, so the rest of that span rendered as a large empty panel and the ticket started a full row below the navbar. Four stylesheets set this placement in sequence and the LAST-loaded one won: ReferenceFuturesTerminal (`col 1/4`, `row 1/4`) → TerminalPresentationPolish (`col 1/-1`, `row 2/4`) → FuturesStudio (`col 2/4`, `row 1/4`) → FuturesDesignVariants (`col 2/5`, `row 2/4`). The correction lives in `TerminalAccountPanel.css`, the last-loaded layer that already owns the desktop track, at matching specificity.
- **Material files.** `frontend/src/pages/trade-terminal/TerminalAccountPanel.css` — ticker `grid-column:2 / 4` and order panel `grid-row:1 / 4` from 1500px; depth `grid-row:1 / 3` with the ticker inside the chart column from 1760px; ticker gap 24→14px; the `fo-panel` top nudge that cleared the old bar removed; pair-selector icon rules. `frontend/src/components/FuturesTickerBar.tsx` — `CryptoIcon` (same component and 20px the market list uses) before the pair; mark price as the NUMBER ALONE, with `futures.markPrice` kept as `title` + `aria-label` so the cell is still self-describing; 24h change now `+1 654,18 (+2,34%)`. `frontend/src/pages/FuturesPage.tsx` — the `Рынки` `<h2>` removed, so search and Избранное rise to the top of the rail; the aside keeps its `aria-label`, so nothing is lost for assistive tech.
- **WIDTH GATE, stated plainly.** The instrument row needs ~1020–1070px on one line. Columns 2+3 only supply that from ~1500px, and columns 2 alone from ~1760px. Below those the previous full-span bar is kept, because the alternatives were a scrolling ticker or a wrapped one that takes height back out of the chart. So on a <1500px desktop the order panel still starts below the bar. Not fixed, and not hidden: it is a real limit of fitting eight metrics on one line.
- **The 24h absolute figure is DERIVED, not fetched.** `LiveQuote` carries a last price and a percent but no previous close, and the collector does not carry Bybit's `prevPrice24h`. The open is recovered by exact algebra from the two published values — `last * p / (100 + p)` — so it agrees with the percent beside it by construction instead of being a second number that could disagree. Null on a -100% move (open is zero, division undefined) and the percent still renders alone. Threading `prevPrice24h` through the market-data pipeline would make it exact to the provider's own rounding; not done, deliberately, as it is display-only and would widen the diff through shared plumbing.
- **TESTED.** Frontend `tsc` PASS, `npm run build` PASS. `futuresTickerHeader.test.ts` 22/24 — the 2 failures are the **pre-existing** `frontend/src/lib/api.ts` and `src/api/routes/futures.ts` hash pins, untouched here and failing identically on `main` (verified in a worktree of `origin/main`). Across the four futures header/polish suites: **19 failing on `main`, 19 failing here, zero new** — compared by test name from Jest JSON on both trees. 4 new tests added and passing: the gain, the fall, the -100% guard and the no-quote dash. Two harness additions: a `CryptoIcon` stub and an additive `__ticker` override. One existing assertion was UPDATED, not deleted — the mark-price cell is now asserted to print the bare number AND to keep the label in `title`/`aria-label`.
- **Browser (Chromium, `serve-native-demo-review.cjs`, 1280/1440/1600/1920): 33/33.** Measured, not eyeballed: zero horizontal overflow on the bar and the document at every width; no `Рынки` heading; no `Маркировочная цена:` label; artwork before the pair name; search at the top of the rail. At 1600/1920 the order panel starts at the navbar (y=49, not y=120) and the bar no longer reaches into its column. At 1920 depth also starts at the navbar and grows 628→699px, which `referenceRowCount` turns into more price levels. **Chart height is 628px at every width, before and after** — it did not shrink.
- **NOT VERIFIED.** Live figures in a browser: this sandbox's egress to `stream.bybit.com` and the icon CDN is blocked, so every cell renders its honest `—` and the BTC mark falls back to the branded letter avatar. The `+1 654,18 (+2,34%)` format and the icon element are proven by test and by DOM inspection, not by a live screenshot. Non-RU locales not checked in a browser; mobile/tablet untouched by this change (all rules are `min-width` gated).

### 2026-09-18 — Claude — Futures instrument row: even rhythm, lighter 24h cell (`claude/futures-ticker-rhythm`)

- **Why.** The owner looked at production after PR #119 and reported the row's text as unevenly distributed, and asked for a slightly smaller font on the two-figure 24h cell.
- **MEASURED CAUSE, not a guess.** `justify-content:flex-start` with a fixed `gap:14px` packed every cell left and let all the slack collect in one dead strip at the right end: **99px at 1600, 168px at 1920, 296px at 2048**. With cell widths of 127/132/92/64/59/109/130/184 the result read as crowded on the left, empty on the right.
- **Fix.** `justify-content:space-between` spends that slack on the gaps instead of hoarding it at the end. `gap:14px` stays as the FLOOR, so a narrow bar can never push cells closer than before — distribution only ever adds. Scoped to `min-width:1500px`, the same gate as the layout change, because below it the bar still spans the order column and spreading cells across that would park a metric above order entry. `.value.change` 13px → 12px: it is the one cell carrying two figures, so at the shared size it was the heaviest thing in the row and competed with the last price above it.
- **Material file.** `frontend/src/pages/trade-terminal/TerminalAccountPanel.css` only. No markup, no component, no data path touched.
- **MEASURED AFTER.** Trailing gap **168px → 12px** (the bar's own padding) at 1600/1920/2048; inter-cell gaps now equal at every width — 27/26/26/27/26/27/26 at 1600, 37/36/36/36/37/36/36 at 1920, 55/54/55/54/55/54/55 at 2048 (1px variance is sub-pixel rounding). `.value.change` 12px, every other value still 13px.
- **TESTED.** `npm run build` PASS. Browser (Chromium, `serve-native-demo-review.cjs`, 1280/1440/1600/1920) **33/33** — the full PR #119 regression set still green, including zero horizontal overflow and the ticket and depth still at the navbar.
- **NOT VERIFIED.** Live figures: this sandbox has no market feed, so cells render `—`; the gap and font measurements are taken from the real laid-out DOM, which is what this change is about. Below 1500px the row keeps the previous left-packed rhythm, deliberately.

### 2026-09-18 — Claude — Order book: the flicker was the row key, measured on a replayed feed (`claude/orderbook-calm-motion`)

- **Source.** The owner's `0918.mp4` — a Binance web book in a narrow column — with the brief "1:1, fewer jumps and flicker, muted colours, calm numbers and depth bars". Existing terminal refined; no new terminal, no new design.
- **MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED — and one hypothesis died in the process.** NEW `scripts/qa-orderbook-motion.cjs`: serves the real build, points venue socket URLs at a local WebSocket replaying a **fully pre-generated seeded frame list** (seed 20260918, 401 frames, 100 ms apart, drifting mid), and counts DOM churn with a MutationObserver. Same seed in, same frames out, so before/after is the same market rather than two markets. The FIRST version of that generator was wrong and said so loudly: it moved the mid without sending size-0 deletions, the book accumulated bids above its own best ask, the client's crossed-book guard threw `DepthDesyncError`, and the panel went stale mid-run. That stale banner sits in the flex flow and shifted the ladder — it looked exactly like the reported jump. It was **a fault of my fixture, not the product**, and is recorded here so nobody re-reports it. With the generator corrected the panel never goes stale and its height and row count are constant.
- **THE REAL CAUSE.** `key={exact}` — rows keyed by their PRICE. A ladder's prices move with the market, so every shift handed React a new key for a slot that had not gone anywhere: **215 rows torn down and rebuilt in 15 s, 14.3/s** (mobile 225). A remounted row restarts its depth-bar transition from zero, which is the twitch. Fixed by keying on the depth SLOT (`${side}-${index}`) — row N is "the Nth level out from the touch", and price/size/cumulative are its contents. **After: 0 remounts, desktop and mobile.**
- **Also fixed, both from the reference.** The centre price had the direction arrow concatenated into its string, so `↑77,264.70` and `77,264.70` are different widths and the digits slid sideways on a tick that changed no digit; the arrow now has its own fixed-width slot AFTER the number, as the reference draws it. Depth bars were 30 % alpha — a solid block that flashes on every size change — now .13, and the transform transition 260 ms → 140 ms so a bar settles before the next 300 ms publish instead of living permanently mid-animation. The dead 30 % rules were deleted rather than shadowed.
- **Material files.** `frontend/src/components/FuturesReferenceBook.tsx` (row key, centre-price markup), `frontend/src/pages/trade-terminal/TerminalAccountPanel.css` (bar alpha, transition, arrow slot; old rules removed), NEW `scripts/qa-orderbook-motion.cjs`, NEW `frontend/src/lib/__tests__/orderBookCalmMotion.test.ts`, evidence in `docs/qa/orderbook/`.
- **DATA UNTOUCHED.** No averaging, no interpolation, no invented liquidity, no change to flush cadence, sequence handling, delta application, the crossed-book guard, or the stale/unavailable/connecting distinction — all four are pinned by the new suite. Execution, orders, balances, margin, fees, P&L and liquidation were not touched at all.
- **TESTED.** Frontend `tsc` PASS, `npm run build` PASS. NEW `orderBookCalmMotion.test.ts` **10/10**. Across `futuresDepth`, `referenceBook`, `spotOrderBook`, `nativeFuturesTerminal`, `terminalPresentation`, `futuresUiPolish`: **6 failing on `main`, 6 here, zero new** (compared by test name from Jest JSON in a worktree of `origin/main`). One existing assertion was UPDATED, not deleted: `referenceBook.test.ts` pinned the old `↓100.25` concatenation and now asserts the number and the arrow as the two elements the panel actually guarantees.
- **HONEST COUNTER-NUMBER.** Text rewrites rose 2 105 → 4 489 (desktop). That is the same work changing shape, not new work: those updates used to arrive as whole-node replacements counted under childList. Stated in `docs/qa/orderbook/README.md` rather than omitted.
- **STILL DIFFERENT FROM THE VIDEO, not claimed as 1:1.** The centre row shows `Спред` where Binance shows the mark price beside the last. Our ladder renders as many rows as the panel's height allows (≈19 a side at 1920) against the reference's 7 at its grouping. Row density, font and the grouping steps offered are ours, not Binance's. Spot (`OrderBookPanel.tsx`) was NOT touched — this is the Futures book only.

### 2026-09-18 — Claude — CI follow-up on PR #124: the removed class the browser QA steered by

- **What broke.** All 12 `native` browser checks failed on `bf7475e`, every one of them inside `ready()` in `scripts/qa-native-demo-browser.cjs`. One cause: the account card's `futures-account-balance` class. I had rebuilt the card's markup and dropped that class as dead styling, not noticing it was the hook the browser QA waits for (`.futures-account-balance .mono`) before it considers the terminal composed, and the same hook the outage checks read to prove an unknown balance renders a dash.
- **Reproduced before fixing.** Ran the same script locally with only the component change stashed: **10 failures, identical names and identical errors to CI**. With the fix: `qa-native-demo-browser.cjs` **28/28** and `qa-large-numbers-browser.cjs` **58/58**.
- **Fix.** Restored `futures-account-balance` on the margin-balance row and gave the other three figures the same treatment — `futures-account-available`, `futures-account-im`, `futures-account-mm`. They carry no styling; they exist so anything outside the component can address a specific figure, and so renaming a visible label can never again silently break a check that was reading the balance.
- **`accountSummary()` re-pointed, NOT weakened.** It was written against the old markup and would have failed as soon as `ready()` stopped timing out. It now reads the margin mode from the working switcher at the top of the panel (and asserts the duplicate row is NOT back in the card), compares the balance and available figures on their digits after normalising group separators and the non-breaking space, reads IM/MM from their own rows, and encodes the `<0.01%` rule instead of assuming two decimals. Two assertions were ADDED while I was there: the currency must be its own element starting with a non-breaking space, and a long balance must be grouped and never abbreviated or truncated.
- **A stale claim of mine, corrected.** My whole-repo comparison for the first commit ran BEFORE I added the `<0.01%` rule, so it did not cover it. `futuresReferenceRow.test.ts` pinned the old one-line `showPct` verbatim and was failing. Re-run properly: **103 failing on the baseline, 103 here, zero new** across 4 100 tests. The guard was updated to pin the new rule explicitly — including that the band is strictly above zero, so a real zero still prints `0.00%` — rather than deleted. One of my own new tests was updated the same way, to assert the non-breaking space rather than the bare `USDT` it used to expect.
- **A second self-inflicted false alarm, recorded so nobody chases it.** One full-suite run showed a new failure in `homepageTailwindUtilities.test.ts`. It was not a regression: that test reads `frontend/dist`, and the native QA script builds its own shimmed bundle into the same directory. After a plain `npm run build` it passes. Tests that assert on `dist` have to be run against a `dist` that a normal build produced.
- **Material files.** `frontend/src/components/FuturesAccountSummary.tsx` (row hooks), `scripts/qa-native-demo-browser.cjs` (`accountSummary` re-pointed and extended), `frontend/src/lib/__tests__/futuresReferenceRow.test.ts` and `orderPanelNavCleanup.test.ts` (both guards updated), refreshed evidence in `docs/qa/native-demo/`.
- **NOT VERIFIED.** Still no production check. The `validate` job's result on the new head is not in this entry.
