### 2026-09-18 — Claude — v4: the order ticket gets its width, its surfaces and its controls (`claude/native-qa-account-hooks`)

- **Why this branch and not a new one.** The v4 brief says to extend the UI follow-up already in flight rather than open a competing branch. `claude/native-qa-account-hooks` (PR #126) was cut from fresh `main`, is unmerged, and already carries the QA-locator work §10 asks about, so v4 went on top of it after merging the new `main` (`e6e3fb5`, which brought in Codex's PR #125 pair-label work — clean merge, no conflicts, none of my files touched).
- **MEASURED, NOT EYEBALLED — and the owner's screenshot was NOT taken at face value.** The two full-terminal references were captured in one browser at one scale, so they compare to each other even though neither yields a CSS size. Sampling and measuring both: our panel `#111922` against the reference's `#101014` — **75% brighter and strongly blue**; panel-to-control contrast **1.15 vs 1.20**; and every field of ours also drew a visible `#304050` outline where the reference draws none. The marked control row there is **1.18×** ours, and the **leverage select 1.36×** — that one control was the squeezed one, not the row as a whole.
- **The one trap in the brief, checked rather than assumed.** The owner's VOLTEX frame shows dim submit buttons. That is the DISABLED state: the quantity field in it is empty. Confirmed on the real build — the pair enables the moment a price and a quantity are present, and the derived rows fill in. The enabled fills `#00b879` / `#f33451` already sit within a couple of points of the reference's own sampled fills and were **left exactly as they were**. Nothing was disabled, bypassed or brightened to produce a better frame.
- **What changed.** Scoped tokens on the ticket (`--fo-surface` `#0c1118`, `--fo-control` `#1d2531`, hairline `#232c39`, `--fo-text` pure white, two quieter label tones) instead of scattered overrides — contrast now **1.23**, panel luminance 0.0055 against the reference's 0.0053, blue channel still +12 over red so it stays VOLTEX navy. Order column **278 → 312px** (+12.2%), taken from the flexible chart track; the pair list and depth column keep their exact widths and page overflow stays 0. Select split **1.55/1 → 1.18/1**, which is the reference's own ratio, so the leverage control goes 93 → 124px. Fields **40 → 44px** tall, radius **3 → 8px**, solid fill and a hairline instead of an outline, value at 15px pure white. Submit pair **123×40 r4 → 138×44 r999px**. Disabled became its own style at full opacity — blanket `opacity:.45` had been taking the label down with the fill, leaving a button whose text could not be read.
- **Two defects found only by looking at the render.** The sticky submit pair masked scrolling content with `box-shadow: 0 -8px 0 var(--workspace-bg)`, which is no longer the panel colour, so it painted a lighter strip above the buttons. And each field carried TWO focus indicators: the row's border plus the input's own outline and glow, and because the row clips its children the only surviving part of the inner one was a stray gold vertical line between the number and its unit. Both fixed.
- **Wording.** `Купить / Лонг` → `Открыть Лонг`, `Продать / Шорт` → `Открыть Шорт` (Cyrillic «Шорт», not the reference frame's stray English), all seven locales. New `futures.closeShort` / `futures.closeLong`: in reduce-only the pair reads `Закрыть Шорт` / `Закрыть Лонг` — a reduce-only BUY closes a short, so the labels are deliberately crossed. **Only the wording changes**: side, the reduce-only flag, the guard and the submitted command are untouched, and the tests pin that. `Маржинальный баланс` → `Баланс маржи`, same key, same figure, same source.
- **A scope error I caught and undid.** Changing `futures.collateralIncomplete` also changed the WALLET page's valuation note, which this brief explicitly does not touch. Reverted to its original wording, assets and all, and the ticket's compact tooltip got its own key `futures.collateralPartial` — "Часть активов не учтена в сумме: оценка временно недоступна." No asset codes and no "lower bound" in the ticket: which ticker is unpriced is a fact about our providers, not about the trader. `unpricedAssets` still DECIDES whether the mark appears, so the warning cannot be shown without one or suppressed while one exists.
- **The previous round's residual difference is closed.** An eight-digit balance beside `Маржинальный баланс` did not fit one line in a 278px column. The wider column plus the shorter label fixes it: every account row is a single line at 1440×900, 1366×768 and 390×844.

### 2026-09-18 — Claude — the Депозит button opens the wallets, not a dropdown (`claude/deposit-all-wallets`)

- **Owner's request.** «Кнопка Депозит повинна відкривати і показувати зразу кошельки для поповнення.» The header modal previously opened a «Сеть» dropdown and showed ONE address, for the one network that happened to be selected first.
- **What it does now.** Every configured wallet is listed at once, each card naming its network, the exact assets that chain will credit, the address and its own copy button. No asset dropdown either: the per-asset minimum equivalent it fed is gone from this screen, and the authoritative USD minimum is shown instead.
- **One request, not one per chain.** `/deposit-chains?includeConfig=true` already resolved every chain's config and threw the address away. It now carries `address` per chain. Not a new disclosure: the same authenticated caller could already read the same value from `/deposit-address/:chain`, and a chain only reaches the array once its address resolved. **The bare array contract is unchanged** — it strips `address` exactly as it already stripped `supportedAssets`, so anything reading the older shape sees no difference.
- **The deploy-window hazard, handled rather than ignored.** The frontend (Cloudflare) and the API (Render) deploy separately. A frontend that hard-required the new field would break deposits outright while live against a not-yet-updated API. So `validDepositConfig` treats the address as OPTIONAL, and `useDepositWallets` falls back to `/deposit-address/:chain` for any chain that arrives without one — with the same cross-checks as before, so a destination naming another chain or an asset the config never listed is discarded. What is NOT tolerated is a present-but-empty address: that would print a blank into a funds-receiving field, so the whole config is rejected.
- **A chain whose address cannot be resolved is DROPPED and flagged**, never rendered blank, and the screen says an address could not be loaded rather than silently listing fewer wallets.
- **Material files.** `src/api/routes/deposits.ts` (address in the envelope only), `frontend/src/lib/depositMinimum.ts` (optional field, strict when present), `frontend/src/lib/useDepositOptions.ts` (NEW `useDepositWallets`; `useDepositOptions` itself untouched), `frontend/src/components/DepositModal.tsx` (rewritten render), seven locale files (NEW `deposit.warningAll`), NEW `frontend/src/lib/__tests__/depositAllWallets.test.ts`, NEW `src/api/routes/__tests__/deposits.test.ts` cases, NEW `scripts/qa-deposit-wallets.cjs`, evidence in `docs/qa/deposit-wallets/`.
- **NOT TOUCHED, deliberately.** The Wallet page's own deposit modal (`pages/wallet-v3/DepositModal.tsx`) keeps its approved archive design and its network/asset selects — the owner asked about the header button. `useDepositOptions` is unchanged, so that page's behaviour is identical. No change to deposit crediting, verification, treasury resolution, auth or CORS.
- **TESTED.** Backend `tsc` PASS, frontend `tsc` PASS, `npm run build` PASS. `deposits.test.ts` **19/19** (+5 new). NEW `depositAllWallets.test.ts` **15/15**. Whole repo compared by test name against the same tree with these changes stashed: **103 failing before, 103 after, zero new and zero fixed** (4 117 → 4 136 tests).
- **BROWSER-VERIFIED** (Chromium, real build, loopback fixtures, 1440×900 and 390×844): all six wallets printed in full with no `<select>`; the current-API path and the per-chain fallback render a byte-identical list; an unresolvable chain is absent and announced; copy puts the full address on the clipboard and only the pressed button confirms; **0 copy buttons covered by a floating overlay** with the modal scrolled to its end — the support launcher stacks above the modal (999 vs 100) but does not reach a button at either viewport. Measured, left alone, and now guarded.
- **ALREADY-FAILING, not caused here.** `walletUxRefinement.test.ts` pins `wallet-v3/DepositModal.tsx` and `lib/api.ts` by hash and `sharedHeaderStylesheetOwnership.test.ts` fails a page-root reset count. All three are red on the base commit too — confirmed by re-running them with these changes stashed — and neither of those two files is modified here.
- **STILL OPEN, not changed.** The modal's title is `deposit.title` = «Пополнение» while the button that opens it says «Депозит», and `DepositModal` has no `keydown` handler so Escape does not close it. Both pre-date this work and both were raised with the owner; neither was answered, so neither was touched.
- **NOT VERIFIED.** No production check. This branch is cut from `claude/native-qa-account-hooks` (PR #126) because `main` still lacks that PR's `native` CI repair; it should land after #126. Not merged, no deploy.

### 2026-09-18 — Claude — order-book depth bars: each side scaled against its own depth (`claude/deposit-all-wallets`)

- **Why this was not done before, stated plainly.** Both order-panel briefs forbade it: v2 §"Не змінювати стакан, графік, таблицю позицій"; v4 "Не перебудовуй графік, стакан, лівий список активів чи таблицю позицій" and "стакан у цьому PR не змінювати". The red frames on the owner's own marked references (`07_bybit…`, `08_voltex…`) sit on the margin/leverage selects, the price/quantity fields, the submit pair and the balance rows — **not** on the book. The owner has since asked for it directly, which lifts that restriction.
- **MEASURED, from the owner's two same-scale reference frames.** The reference terminal scales each side's depth bar against THAT SIDE's deepest visible level: its asks run 0.038 down to 0.002 and the shortest bar is ≈5% of the track, which is 0.002/0.038 — not 0.002 against the bid side's 0.079. Ours divided both sides by one shared maximum (`Math.max(lastBuy, lastSell)`), so on the owner's own frame — bids cumulating to 6.009 against asks' 1.936 — every ask bar was capped at **32%** of its track and the ask ladder read as one flat band with no shape.
- **Change.** `maxDepth` split into `maxBuyDepth` / `maxSellDepth` in `FuturesReferenceBook.tsx`; the row divides by the maximum belonging to its own side. Still clamped with `Math.min(1, …)`. The cross-side imbalance is not lost — the B/S ratio strip under the ladder reports it from the same visible window, unchanged.
- **VERIFIED in the browser, on a byte-identical replayed feed** (`scripts/qa-orderbook-motion.cjs`, same seed before and after, `docs/qa/orderbook-1to1/`). **The visible difference on THAT feed is small and I am not claiming otherwise**: it is nearly balanced (asks 57.165 against bids 62.207), so the top ask bar moves 0.919 → 1.000, about 8%. The case it actually fixes is the lopsided one in the owner's production frame, 0.322 → 1.000. Motion is unchanged: 0 rows remounted, identical row counts and body heights before and after, desktop and mobile.
- **An existing assertion was UPDATED, not deleted.** `orderBookCalmMotion.test.ts` pinned the literal `level.cumulative / maxDepth`. Its intent — the bar width still comes from the level's own cumulative — is preserved, now pinning the per-side divisor, plus a new test asserting the shared maximum is gone, that neither side's divisor is built from the other side's levels, that the clamp survives, and that the ratio strip still reports the imbalance.
- **Material files.** `frontend/src/components/FuturesReferenceBook.tsx`, `frontend/src/lib/__tests__/orderBookCalmMotion.test.ts`, evidence in `docs/qa/orderbook-1to1/`.
- **MEASURED BUT NOT CHANGED, awaiting the owner's decision.** Three further differences from the reference: (a) its centre row shows last price plus MARK price, ours shows last price plus the spread; (b) it has four ladder-layout toggles, we have three; (c) tab labels «Книга ордеров»/«Последние сделки» against our «Стакан»/«Сделки». (a) needs a mark-price source on this screen, so it is not a CSS change; none were done unasked.
- **TESTED.** Frontend `tsc` PASS, `npm run build` PASS. `orderBookCalmMotion.test.ts`, `referenceBook.test.ts`, `privateChartTicket.test.ts` all PASS. Whole repo compared by test name against this branch without the order-book change: **103 failing before, 103 after, zero new and zero fixed**. `futuresFinalPolish.test.ts`'s 11 failures are pre-existing — confirmed by re-running it with the change stashed.
- **NOT VERIFIED.** No production check; the sandbox has no live venue feed, so all evidence is the seeded replay plus arithmetic from the owner's own frames.

### 2026-09-18 — Claude — deposit: asset before network, one data path, amber per-address warnings (`claude/deposit-modal-fixes`)

- **The reported "hang" was reproduced before anything was changed, and it was not a hang.** `scripts/qa-wallet-deposit-select.cjs` drove the real build: picking a different network DID change the address and the asset list, and picking a different asset DID work. What made the screen read as stuck is that it asked for the NETWORK first, and on most chains the asset list that followed held exactly one entry — Bitcoin credits BTC only, TRC-20 credits USDT only. Opening it changed nothing. So the owner's own suggestion is the fix, not a cosmetic reorder.
- **Asset first, then the networks that carry it.** New `useDepositSelection` derives the asset list from the wallets, narrows the networks to those whose own `assets` include the chosen asset, and resolves the address from that wallet. Neither list is ever widened beyond what the backend said it will credit. A choice the current wallets cannot honour falls back rather than sticking, because the config is re-read on each opening and a chain can disappear from it.
- **A real defect found while reproducing: Escape closed the whole dialog instead of the open dropdown.** `Modal` and `Select` both listen for Escape on `document`, and the modal registered first. `Select` now listens in the CAPTURE phase and stops propagation, so one key does one thing. No option, value, label, disabled rule, focus order or ARIA role changed.
- **One deposit data path, not two.** `useDepositOptions` is deleted; both the nav screen and the Wallet screen read `useDepositWallets`, so they cannot disagree about which address belongs to which chain. The minimum-equivalent quote moved to its own `useMinimumEquivalent` — same ticker source, same 30s refresh, still display-only, with `DepositService` re-pricing at credit time. The deposit config is now fetched in exactly one place, and a test pins that.
- **The nav screen's warning, to the owner's wording.** The single red block is gone; each wallet card carries its own amber caution under the address it belongs to: «Отправляй на этот адрес только {assets} в сеть {chain}…». New `deposit.warningAddress` plus six bare network names (`deposit.network.*` — `TRC-20`, `ERC-20`, `BEP-20`, …) so the sentence does not read "only USDT on the USDT (TRC-20) network". The superseded `deposit.warningAll` was REMOVED from all seven locales, not left behind unused. Amber, not red: red is this app's colour for a loss or a failure, and a red block over every wallet reads as "something is wrong here".
- **A hash guard re-pinned, deliberately and narrowly.** `walletUxRefinement.test.ts` pins `wallet-v3/ui.tsx` by SHA-256; the Escape fix changes that file, so the pin was updated with a comment stating exactly what changed and what did not. The suite's two OTHER pins (`wallet-v3/DepositModal.tsx`, `lib/api.ts`) were already failing on `main` before this work — that file's own comment says so — and were left untouched. I changed `DepositModal.tsx` here, so its pin stays red; `api.ts` I neither touched nor reviewed, so it is not mine to bless.
- **Material files.** `frontend/src/lib/useDepositOptions.ts` (rewritten: `useDepositWallets`, `useMinimumEquivalent`, `useDepositSelection`; `useDepositOptions` deleted), `pages/wallet-v3/DepositModal.tsx` (asset above network, narrowed network list), `pages/wallet-v3/ui.tsx` (Escape capture), `components/DepositModal.tsx` (per-card amber warning, bare network names), seven locale files, `lib/__tests__/depositAllWallets.test.ts` (18 tests), `lib/__tests__/walletUxRefinement.test.ts` (one pin), NEW `scripts/qa-wallet-deposit-select.cjs`, `scripts/qa-deposit-wallets.cjs` (warning assertions re-pointed), evidence in `docs/qa/wallet-deposit/`.
- **TESTED.** Frontend `tsc` PASS, `npm run build` PASS. `depositAllWallets.test.ts` **18/18**. `qa-deposit-wallets.cjs` PASS (6 wallets at 1440 and 390, both API shapes, degraded chain, clipboard, zero covered copy buttons). `qa-wallet-deposit-select.cjs` PASS with zero page errors. Whole repo compared by test name against `origin/main` in the same tree: **102 failing before, 102 after, zero new and zero fixed** once the deliberate re-pin is applied (103 before the re-pin, and the one extra was exactly that pin).
- **NOT CHANGED, and worth deciding on.** The Wallet page keeps its own wording («…через сеть USDT (TRC-20)») — the owner asked for the new sentence on the nav screen only, so the two now differ by a few words. The nav modal is roughly twice as tall with six warnings; collapsing them back to one notice is a one-line change if that reads better. The modal title is still «Пополнение» under a «Депозит» button, and `DepositModal` still has no Escape handler of its own — both raised earlier, neither answered.
- **NOT VERIFIED.** No production check; all evidence is the real build against local fixtures. Non-RU locales checked for key presence only.
### 2026-09-18 — Claude — One premium palette for the Futures terminal (`claude/terminal-premium-palette`)

- **Source.** The owner's two terminal screenshots and the brief that followed them: take the calmer contrast of the one and the premium detail of the other, name the exact colours, and put it on the exchange. Branch cut from `origin/main` at `e6396e2` plus the QA-hook fix `d4677d6`, then `origin/main` `2bde9db` (PR #127) merged in.
- **The palette.** canvas `#0b0e11`, panel `#12161c`, raised `#1a1f27`, raised hover `#1f252e`, border `#232a34`, field border `#2d3540`; text `#eaeef3` / `#98a2b3` / `#5c6675`; one accent `#f5a524` (hover `#ffb84d`); buy `#2ebd85`, sell `#f6465d`, with `.14` dim variants. Typography, sizes and spacing are untouched — the terminal already has its own.
- **HOW, not by hand.** A frozen map of 94 old literals → palette values is applied by a script that regenerates each sheet from its committed version with postcss, so a re-run is byte-identical (verified: second run produced no diff). A new last layer, `TerminalPremium.css`, re-values the design tokens on the Futures root and settles what older sheets hard-code — three text levels, one accent, and the chart chrome that lives in sheets Spot shares.
- **COLOUR ONLY, MEASURED.** No declaration in the new sheet sets a size, spacing, radius or border width; the map only rewrites colour literals. A first draft DID add `border:1px solid` and `border-radius:8px` to the panels — which the approved Studio composition deliberately sets to `0` — and it was removed. Chromium, real build, 1440 and 390, against a build of the base commit: 13 panels, buttons, fields, tables and chart boxes identical in width, height, position, border, radius and padding; **zero clipped text and no horizontal overflow on either build**. One inherited `font-weight` difference was found this way (the caption rule sat on `.fo-label`, which WRAPS the field, so the typed value inherited 500) and was moved onto `.fo-fieldCaption`.
- **SPOT AND CFD ARE NOT TOUCHED.** Five of these sheets are loaded by the Spot and CFD terminals too. Every changed declaration was classified by whether its selector can reach those roots: **182 Futures-scoped declarations changed, and 120 that reached Spot, CFD or nothing in particular were reverted in full** — a first pass had re-valued them and left both pages half-migrated (two ambers side by side, `--text-tertiary` collapsed into `--text-secondary`). `TradeTerminal.css` and `ProfessionalTerminal.css` end byte-identical to `main`, so the Spot fingerprint in `spotOrdersPresentation.test.ts` is unchanged and was NOT re-taken.
- **PRESERVED FROM THE OTHER AGENT.** PR #127's order ticket landed mid-flight with its own measured `--fo-*` palette, pinned by `orderPanelNavCleanup.test.ts`. This layer stops at the ticket edge: its fields, labels, info rows and Long/Short buttons were removed from `TerminalPremium.css` after the merge, and the ticket keeps the values that brief chose. The one place the two differ (the ticket's surfaces are a slightly different navy from the palette's) is deliberate and left to the owner.
- **The private Futures-style terminal** (`/futures?card=`) carries the same root classes, so it was already being re-coloured by the shared sheets. It now imports `TerminalPremium.css` itself and its two own sheets are on the palette, so the page never depends on which route loaded first.
- **Reviewed by five independent passes** (leftovers, blast radius, contrast, specificity, guards) with adversarial verification. What survived: the buy/sell ratio strip under the ladder kept an old wedge `#17372e` and label `#ea454f` — fixed, with the wedge kept OPAQUE (`#183731`, buy at the track's own `.2` over panel) because it is layered on the red track and a translucent token reads brown. Dead rules the passes found were deleted rather than left: the Spot ticket's classes, the Spot book's `.ob-*`, `.rb-price`, `.rb-center.up strong` (the direction class is on the `<strong>`), `.chart-trade-action`, `.pairs-title-row`. Two `!important` rules that the layer could not beat were re-valued in place instead.
- **TESTED.** Frontend `tsc` PASS, `npm run build --prefix frontend` PASS. Whole frontend suite compared test by test against a build of the base commit: **86 failing before, 85 after, zero new** (1 795 tests; the one that flipped to passing is the `dist`-reading homepage test, which only passes against a normal build — the QA script leaves a shimmed bundle behind). `qa-native-demo-browser.cjs` **28/28**, `qa-large-numbers-browser.cjs` **58/58**, evidence refreshed in `docs/qa/native-demo/`.
- **One guard updated, not deleted.** `orderBookCalmMotion.test.ts` pinned the depth-bar alpha by its colour literals, which a palette change makes meaningless. It now sweeps every `.rb-depth` rule in the sheets that paint that bar plus the `--buy-dim`/`--sell-dim` tokens, and still fails above `.15` — so a re-valued palette cannot quietly bring the weight back up.
- **DELIBERATE DEVIATIONS, stated rather than hidden.** `--text-tertiary` `#5c6675` on the panel is 3.1:1, and white on the sell fill is 3.5:1 — both below WCAG AA for body text, both matching what the reference exchanges ship. Tertiary is used only for hints and placeholders; the currency unit was moved UP to the secondary level so no figure depends on the dim step. The order book's own `.30` fallback alpha is untouched for designs this panel never uses.
- **NOT VERIFIED.** No production check — all browser evidence is local fixtures, no market feed, no production credentials. Spot, CFD and the wallet, markets, copy-trading and card pages are NOT migrated and keep the colours they have today; that is the obvious follow-up and needs its own QA. No engine, order, balance, margin, fee, P&L or liquidation path was touched, and PR #123 was not involved.

### 2026-09-18 — Claude — Wallet rail: «Финансирование» gets the circled dollar (`claude/wallet-funding-icon`)

- **One icon.** `WalletSideNav.tsx` used `PiggyBankIcon` for the `funding` item. It is now `CircleDollarSignIcon` — the SAME icon the Обзор account row already used for that account, and the one on the owner's reference. A piggy bank reads as savings; Финансирование is the spot ledger deposits land on, and the rail and the row naming one account two different ways is how a user ends up thinking they are two accounts.
- **Nothing else.** No label, no route, no section behaviour, no other rail item, no Wallet logic, no balances. `PiggyBankIcon` is no longer imported anywhere in the file.
- **Material files.** `frontend/src/pages/wallet-v3/WalletSideNav.tsx` — three lines, one of them the import.
- **TESTED.** Frontend `tsc` PASS, `npm run build` PASS. `walletOverview.test.ts` + `walletUnifiedAccount.test.ts` (the two suites that read this file) **59/59**. Whole repo compared by test name against `origin/main` (`0a94681`) in the same tree: **102 failing before, 102 after, zero new and zero fixed**. Wallet page re-rendered in Chromium with zero page errors, and the rail now shows the circled dollar beside Финансирование.
- **NOT VERIFIED.** No production check.

### 2026-09-18 — Claude — Order book: the reconnect that was being cancelled every second, and a ladder that stopped being two blocks of colour (`claude/orderbook-calm`)

- **THE BANNER'S REAL CAUSE, reproduced before anything was changed.** «Данные не обновляются — переподключение» (`trade.bookStale`) is raised when no depth frame has been accepted for 12s. The label was accurate; the reconnect it promised was the thing being prevented. `FuturesDepthTransport`'s 1s heartbeat decided the socket was dead purely from `now - lastAccepted`, and once a book is stale that condition stays true no matter how young the socket is — `lastAccepted` by definition predates every reconnect attempt. So one second after each new socket opened, the same tick called `reconnect()`, which `clearSocket()`s it mid-handshake. **Measured on the fake-timer harness before the fix: eight sockets opened in eighty seconds, every one closed, each alive for exactly one tick** (t+14000, 17000, 22000, 31000, 47000, 63000, 79000 — one second of life per backoff cycle). Any visitor whose handshake + subscribe + first `orderbook.200` snapshot takes longer than that second never recovered, and the banner was permanent rather than momentary.
- **The fix is one condition.** New `SOCKET_GRACE_MS = 6_000` and a `socketStartedAt` stamp; the heartbeat may only tear the socket down once it has had its own six seconds. Six is what `FALLBACK_AFTER_MS` already gives a healthy connection, and the backend fallback polls throughout, so nothing waits on it. A socket that really is mute is still replaced — just not instantly.
- **TWO NEW REGRESSIONS, both proved to fail without the fix.** `futuresDepth.test.ts` gains *a reconnect is given time to finish connecting instead of being torn down every second* (drives the book to stale, waits for the replacement socket, asserts it is still open three seconds later and that a handshake completing then brings the book back to `live`) and *a socket that stays mute past its grace is still replaced* (so the grace can never be made infinite). Reverting the one condition fails both: **2 failed / 24 passed**; with it, **26/26**.
- **WHAT I COULD NOT ESTABLISH.** The sandbox cannot reach production, so I cannot say this is the *only* thing failing there. The book's second source — `GET /market/futures/orderbook/:symbol` on our own backend — is correctly wired (`FuturesPage` sets the fallback base to `API_BASE`, so the Cloudflare/Render split is handled, and `marketDepthRouter` is mounted), but if that route is answering `available:false` (venue unreachable from Render, breaker open, or no universe) the banner can still appear. That is a server-side check the owner or Codex would have to run against production. No auth, CORS or bypass was touched.
- **THE LADDER, against the owner's Binance reference.** The bar weight was already 13%; the complaint is that the bars **merge**. A cumulative bar grows monotonically from the touch outwards, so the far half of each side sits above 50% of its track, and at `inset:0` nineteen of them butt together into one unbroken red field above the mid and one unbroken green field below. Three changes, none of which touch what the bar measures — same cumulative value, same per-side maximum, same right anchor, same `scaleX`, same 140ms: a **1px gap top and bottom** (this does most of the work), **13% → 10% alpha**, and the **`Всего` column stepped down to the secondary text colour** so three columns at equal brightness over a tinted row stop competing. The B/S ratio strip was `rgba(246,70,93,.2)` under a solid `#183731` wedge — the loudest band in the panel made out of the smallest fact in it, framing the whole book in red; it now draws at the ladder's weight and the B/S letters lose their badge borders.
- **Four reds and greens became two.** The three layout-toggle glyphs carried `#ef454a`/`#20b985` while every price beside them is `#f6465d`/`#2ebd85`. Now the panel's own pair.
- **Every visual rule is colour, alpha or a 1px inset.** Not one sets a size, row height, column width or font size, so no figure a trader reads moved. Re-measured on the seeded replay (`scripts/qa-orderbook-motion.cjs`, same seed 20260918): rows remounted **0 / 0** before and after, row counts `[38]` desktop / `[12]` mobile unchanged, body heights `[831]`/`[306]` unchanged. Before/after frames in `docs/qa/orderbook/calm-before-after.png`.
- **The existing guard was honoured, not edited.** `orderBookCalmMotion.test.ts` caps every depth-bar alpha at ≤0.15 and the transform transition under 300ms; 10% and 140ms sit inside both, and the file is unchanged.
- **Material files.** `frontend/src/lib/futuresDepth.ts` (grace constant, `socketStartedAt`, one heartbeat condition), `frontend/src/lib/__tests__/futuresDepth.test.ts` (+2 tests), `frontend/src/pages/trade-terminal/TerminalAccountPanel.css` (alpha + new calm section), `frontend/src/components/FuturesReferenceBook.tsx` (two hex literals in the toggle glyphs), NEW `docs/qa/orderbook/calm-before-after.png`.
- **PRESERVED FROM THE OTHER AGENT.** Codex's `TerminalPremium.css` palette is untouched — the new rules live in `TerminalAccountPanel.css`, which already owned the depth bar, and resolve `--text-secondary` from Codex's tokens rather than hard-coding a grey. The premium sheet's `--buy-dim`/`--sell-dim` are unchanged.
- **NOT TOUCHED.** No trading math, execution, balances, order engine, wallet, margin, fee or liquidation path. No change to what the book *says*: `stale` / `unavailable` / `connecting` still mean what they meant, the aggregator is the only source of levels, and nothing is averaged, interpolated or invented.
- **TESTED.** Frontend `tsc` PASS, `npm run build` PASS. `futuresDepth.test.ts` + `orderBookCalmMotion.test.ts` **37/37**. Whole repo compared test by test against `origin/main` (`44d6513`) in the same tree: **126 failing before, 126 after — zero new, zero fixed** (4 165 tests).
- **MAIN IS RED AND IT IS NOT FROM THIS BRANCH.** That 126 is the state of `main` itself, up from the 102 measured against `0a94681` two tasks ago; the 24 new ones arrived with `ec0ff96` (PR #133) and are concentrated in source-guard suites — `walletUxRefinement` (22), `cfdChartFallback` (20), `nazaraCardPresentation` (16), `futuresFinalPolish` (11), `nazarProfileCorrection` (8), 30 suites in all. Flagged for the owner; not fixed here, because guessing at which of those are stale hash pins and which are real would mean editing another agent's guards without a brief.
- **NOT VERIFIED.** No production check, no market feed, no production credentials. All browser evidence is the local seeded replay. Spot's own order book (`.ob-*`) and the CFD terminal are not touched.

### 2026-09-19 — Claude — Futures: the instrument row moves to the far-left edge, and search becomes a chooser under the pair selector (`claude/futures-top-row-search`)

- **THE ROW'S LEFT EDGE, measured before and after.** On the built page at 1366 / 1440 / 1664 / 1920 the instrument row started at **x=228** while the workspace's own content edge is at **x=1**, and the rail ran the full height beside it (`railTop 49`, `barBottom 119` — the rail overlapped the row's band by 70px). After: **barLeft = workspaceLeft = 1 at every one of those widths**, `railTop 120` against `barBottom 119` — a **1px** grid gap, no dead rectangle. Evidence: `docs/qa/futures-search/{before,after}-metrics.json` and `top-row-before-after-1440.png`.
- **WHICH SHEET ACTUALLY DECIDES IT.** Three layers place this bar, and the one that wins is neither of the obvious two. `ReferenceFuturesTerminal.css` already had the reference placement (`ticker-bar 1 / 4`, `sidebar 2 / 4`); `FuturesStudio.css` overrode it to `2 / 4` and `1 / 4`; `FuturesDesignVariants.css` then overrode THAT to `2 / 5`; and `TerminalAccountPanel.css` overrides everything above 1500px (`2 / 4`) and 1760px (`2 / 3`). The FuturesStudio edit alone measured **no different at all** — it is included because it makes the base layer agree with the reference, but the change that lands is in `TerminalAccountPanel.css`, appended after the existing owner-corrections block it amends.
- **THAT EARLIER CORRECTION IS PRESERVED, not replaced.** It pulled the bar in from `2 / 5` so its unused tail stopped rendering as a dead panel over order entry, and raised the ticket and the depth column to the navbar. All of it is kept: the ticket still owns column 4 for the full height, the book still rises at 1760. Only the row's LEFT edge moves, column 2 → column 1. The ticket is raised to row 1 at every desktop width for the same reason that block raised it at 1500 — otherwise row 1 of its column becomes the dead rectangle. **No panel's width changed at any width**, which the harness asserts per panel.
- **THE RAIL'S SEARCH ROW IS GONE, not collapsed.** No header, no magnifier, no `X`, no spacer, no hidden input. `.pairs-head*` is deleted from both the component and the stylesheet; the rail's first child is the favourites row. The field is now rendered only behind a `searchable` prop, by the chooser and by the mobile market dialog — **one component, one field, one filter**, used twice.
- **THE CHOOSER IS A LAYER PLACED IN THE RAIL'S OWN GRID CELL.** `grid-column:1; grid-row:2 / 4` with `align-self:start` and a z-index: two grid items sharing a cell overlap rather than displacing each other, so it hangs under the BTC/USDT selector at the far-left edge and **the chart, the book and the ticket keep every pixel** — asserted on left, top and width for all three, with the chooser open and closed. No measured rectangle, no `position:fixed`, no frame to wait for.
- **INSTANT, and the measurement is honest about what it can claim.** Click → focused field: **42–86 ms**, both entry points, all five widths (caret 42–51 ms, list glyph 54–86 ms — the brief's «under ~50 ms» is met by the caret and missed slightly by the glyph; stated rather than rounded). Requests: the endpoint-set difference over a short window was the wrong instrument — at 3s it charged the chart's candle refresh to opening, at 10s the market snapshot and the access check to typing. It now takes the baseline as **everything the untouched page requested from first paint through a ten-second idle sit**, and asserts search reaches no endpoint the page has never reached (**zero**, on open and on typing, at every width). Volume is then checked **per endpoint on the only two the market list can touch** — `/market/assets/icons` and `/market/live` — ten seconds with the chooser open against ten idle. The aggregate deliberately is NOT asserted: it is dominated by the depth REST fallback polling at 1 Hz because this sandbox cannot reach the venue WebSocket, and it drifts by several per window whatever the page is doing.
- **INTERACTION, all of it driven.** Both entry points open the same state through `openMarkets`; `data-market-entry` on both is what stops the outside-click handler closing on pointerdown and the button's own click re-opening it, so the control that opens it can also close it. The listener is attached from an effect, after the opening click has finished dispatching, so no first click is swallowed. **Twenty open/close pairs alternating between the two buttons** leave it closed and it still opens. Escape closes; a click outside closes; picking a contract selects it and dismisses the layer; crossing the 1025px breakpoint drops the desktop layer so it cannot race the mobile dialog.
- **FILTERING is local and unchanged.** `btc`, `BTC`, `BTCUSDT`, `BTC/USDT` and `eth` each drive in the browser and each return only matching rows; the predicate is the same case- and slash-normalising `.filter()` over the `symbols` prop it has always been. Favourites, the count, `Цена ⇅`, `24ч % ⇅`, the sort order, the windowing and the selected market are untouched — the rail's sort is clicked in the harness after a selection and still reports itself pressed.
- **TWO GUARDS UPDATED, both deliberately, neither weakened.** `futuresMarketSearch.test.ts` pinned the previous shape of this feature (the magnifier header from PR #134); its eight header assertions were **replaced** by eight describing the chooser, and everything they protected — no request on open, focus in the mounting commit, local filtering, one shared handler, the rail's own controls — is still pinned, now 20 tests. `cryptoCatalogue.test.ts`'s hash pin on `FuturesPairList.tsx` was re-pinned with a comment saying what changed; what that guard exists to protect (the universe arrives as the `symbols` prop, the whitelist stays the server's, no pair added/removed/renamed/derived) is untouched.
- **Material files.** `frontend/src/components/FuturesPairList.tsx` (search row removed, `searchable`/`onDismiss` props), `frontend/src/components/FuturesPairList.css` (`.pairs-head*` deleted, chooser + field added), `frontend/src/components/FuturesTickerBar.tsx` (`data-market-entry`, `aria-expanded`), `frontend/src/pages/FuturesPage.tsx` (one chooser state, Escape/outside-click effect, breakpoint guard), `frontend/src/pages/trade-terminal/FuturesStudio.css`, `frontend/src/pages/trade-terminal/TerminalAccountPanel.css`, `frontend/src/lib/__tests__/futuresMarketSearch.test.ts`, `frontend/src/lib/__tests__/cryptoCatalogue.test.ts`, `scripts/qa-futures-market-search.cjs` (rewritten: geometry measurement, both entry points, 20× hammer, outside click, `QA_GEOMETRY_ONLY` for the before half), evidence in `docs/qa/futures-search/`.
- **PRESERVED FROM THE OTHER AGENT.** Codex's `TerminalPremium.css` is untouched — it declares itself colour-only and adding a grid placement there would have broken that contract. The positions-table and panel-rhythm work merged in PR #132 is untouched. The two entry points themselves came from PR #134 and are kept as they were; only what they open changed.
- **NOT TOUCHED.** No trading engine, positions, orders, balances, margin, P&L, fees, liquidation, order book, price sources, API polling cadence or Wallet. No dictionary value changed. PR #123 not involved.
- **TESTED.** Frontend `tsc` PASS, `npm run build` PASS. `futuresMarketSearch.test.ts` **20/20**, `cryptoCatalogue.test.ts` **30/30**. Browser harness **PASS at 1920 / 1664 / 1440 / 1366 / 390**, zero page errors at every width, zero horizontal overflow at rest and with the chooser open. Whole repo compared test by test against `origin/main` (`6c9662a`) in the same tree: **126 failing before, 126 after — zero new, zero fixed** (4 185 tests).
- **A NOTE ON THAT BASELINE.** The 126 is `main`'s own state and is not from this branch; it is the same 126 recorded in the previous entry, still concentrated in source-guard suites from `ec0ff96`. The base run was taken at `6c9662a`, one commit behind this branch's `da1204b`, because `da1204b` (PR #136) had itself measured zero new failures against `6c9662a`.

### 2026-09-19 — Claude — Three visual corrections: funding label, «Кошелёк», text contrast (`claude/futures-visual-polish`)

- **`main` HAD ALL THREE PREREQUISITES.** The brief asked me to confirm the bottom panel, the top-row/search work and the premium recolouring were in before starting: PR #137 is merged at `3b5887f` and PR #138 at `317ac52`, both with the owner's own sync commits (`474b4df`, `817f454`) on top. Nothing conflicted, so nothing had to be reconciled.
- **1. THE FUNDING LABEL.** `futures.headerFunding` was `Ставка / Отсчет до финансирования` — the slash was naming a second figure the row already shows, since the countdown renders directly beneath the rate. It is now `Ставка финансирования`, shortened in **all seven locales** (`Funding rate`, `Tasa de financiación`, `फंडिंग दर`, `資金調達率`, `펀딩 비율`, `资金费率`). **Only the label string changed** — the rate, the countdown, the component and the layout are untouched.
- **2. «КОШЕЛЁК».** It was `--h-chip` (#1f242c), the header's neutral raised step: a cold graphite sitting beside a gold CTA and reading as unrelated to it. Now the same hue family several steps down in value — `linear-gradient(180deg,#37301f,#29231a)` with a warm hairline and a #ecdcb4 label, rising on hover and pressing visibly on `:active`. The pair reads as one family with the deposit button still unmistakably primary, because this one is dark where that one is bright. **The hairline is an inset shadow, not a border**, so padding, height and radius are byte-for-byte what they were; a border would have added two pixels to both axes.
- **3. TEXT CONTRAST, measured rather than judged.** NEW `scripts/qa-terminal-text-contrast.cjs` samples every text-bearing element in the Futures terminal, resolves the colour it is painted in against the background it is painted on, and reports the WCAG ratio.

  | | before | after |
  |---|---|---|
  | median ratio | 7.51 | **8.66** |
  | worst ratio | 3.98 | **5.56** |
  | texts below AA 4.5:1 | **4** | **0** |
  | of those, small text | 3 | 0 |
  | distinct text colours | 13 | 11 |

  `--text-secondary` #98a2b3 → **#aab4c3** (7.1 → 8.9:1) and `--text-tertiary` #808a9a → **#8b95a5** (5.3 → 5.9:1), which keeps three clearly separated steps roughly 1.7x and 1.5x apart instead of flattening them towards white. Three literals the tokens could not reach joined the scale: the book's unit suffixes (`(USDT)`, `(BTC)`) were **the worst text in the terminal at 3.98:1**, at 11px, on the panel the eye spends most of its time on; plus the connecting label and the empty-state column strip, and the order ticket's own `--fo-text-3`.
- **TWO THINGS THE PROBE GOT WRONG FIRST, and both are fixed in it.** It scored a near-black label on the gold CTA at **1.11:1** because `backgroundColor` is `transparent` on an element painted with a gradient, so the backdrop walk sailed straight past the button — those samples are now returned as unknown and excluded. And it flagged white on «Открыть Шорт» at 4.13:1: that is a **market-colour surface**, which the brief forbids recolouring, and the Short button's fill is `rgb(224,66,86)` rather than the palette's `rgb(246,70,93)`, so the exclusion now matches buy/sell controls by class as well as by value.
- **GREEN AND RED ARE UNTOUCHED.** Not one buy/sell colour changed, as text or as a surface. The probe excludes them deliberately and says so.
- **ONE GUARD UPDATED.** `futuresTickerHeader.test.ts` pinned the old label verbatim; it now pins the new one, with a comment saying why and noting that what the guard exists to protect — the Futures header having its own derivatives terminology rather than falling back on Spot's volume wording — is unchanged.
- **Material files.** `frontend/src/lib/i18n/locales/{ru,en,es,hi,ja,ko,zh}.ts` (one string each), `frontend/src/index.css` (the wallet chip), `frontend/src/pages/trade-terminal/TerminalPremium.css` (two tokens + three literals onto the scale), `frontend/src/pages/trade-terminal/TerminalAccountPanel.css` (one token value), `frontend/src/lib/__tests__/futuresTickerHeader.test.ts`, NEW `scripts/qa-terminal-text-contrast.cjs`, evidence in `docs/qa/terminal-contrast/`.
- **STYLES AND ONE STRING, nothing else.** No component file changed. No trading logic, engine, execution, API, provider, wallet calculation, market-data behaviour or order-book polling. No layout moved.
- **TESTED.** Frontend `tsc` PASS, `npm run build` PASS. Contrast harness **PASS** at 1440, zero page errors. Whole repo compared test by test against `origin/main` (`317ac52`) in the same tree: **126 failing before, 126 after — zero new, zero fixed** (4 185 tests).
- **NOT VERIFIED.** No production check. Contrast was sampled at 1440 only, on a fixture with no open position, so the positions table's own text is not in the sample. The wallet button's hover and active states are CSS-verified but not clicked in a browser.

### 2026-09-19 — Claude — Chart toolbar: crosshair cursor, collapse control at the foot (`claude/chart-toolbar-crosshair-collapse`)

- **CUT FROM FRESH `main` AT `fa7df5c`**, which already carries PR #136 (order-book reconnect), #137 (top row + market chooser), #138 (bottom panel) and #139 (funding label, wallet chip, text contrast). Nothing on those four was duplicated or reverted; the open PR list was checked before starting.
- **TWO SEPARATE ELEMENTS, kept separate.** The crosshair is the **cursor tool's glyph** at the top of the rail; the collapse control is a **different button** at its foot. Neither took on the other's job: the cursor button still toggles `cursor` mode through the existing handler, the toggle still only shows and hides the rail.
- **1. THE CURSOR GLYPH.** `CursorIcon` was an OS arrow — a "select things" symbol for a mode that draws a crosshair on the chart. It is now four rays with a gap at the centre, as four `<path>` elements in the rail's own 24-unit box (never a text glyph or an emoji), at `strokeWidth 1.5` because at the rail's 1.8 a pure-line icon read as the heaviest thing on the panel. **Button size, hit area and the active gold are unchanged**; the browser run measures the button at the same pixels and the active class on the same element.
- **2. THE COLLAPSE CONTROL MOVED DOWN.** It used to sit above the tools in a full-width bar of its own, held there by `order:-1`; the first thing under the chart heading was a control about the panel rather than a tool. Removing `order:-1` puts it where it already is in the DOM — last — and it is drawn there as a compact **26×22 chip** at the left edge with the rail's hairline above it instead of below. The old bar is gone: no empty row, no second button. Measured at 1440: toggle top **232 → 866**, rail bottom **894 → 861**, `order` **-1 → 0**, size **43×24 → 26×22**.
- **THE RESTORE TAB MOVED WITH IT**, from the chart's top-left (`top:8px`) to its **bottom-left** (`bottom:10px`), measured at 884 against a chart bottom of 894. The toggle lives OUTSIDE the scrollable rail, so a short screen scrolls the tools and never the control that brings them back.
- **SCOPED TO FUTURES, and Spot is measured rather than assumed.** `.terminal-chart-shell` is shared with Spot (`/trade`) and the private terminal, so every new rule carries `.futures-reference`, which Spot's root (`.spot-terminal.market-reference`) does not have. The harness now opens `/trade` too: Spot's toggle keeps `order:-1`, keeps its **43×24 bar above the tools** (top 225 vs rail top 249) and lands on the same pixels before and after this change, with zero page errors. **The one thing Spot does inherit is the crosshair**, because that is the component's icon rather than a style — deliberate, and asserted on Spot so it cannot drift back.
- **NOTHING RESTARTS THE CHART.** Proven in the browser at every width, not by reading the code: the canvas is stamped before collapsing and the stamp is still there afterwards (`chartRemounted: false`), a drawing survives (`16 → 16` paths, and again after twenty cycles), and **no endpoint the idle page had not already reached is requested** by one toggle (`[]`) or by twenty (`[]`). No `fitContent`, no scale reset, no refetch, no stream reconnect — the rail and the canvas are siblings and only the rail's width changes. Collapse completes in **237–257 ms** with no width animation.
- **KEYBOARD AND LABELS.** A real `<button type="button">` with `aria-expanded` flipping true/false and an `aria-label` that changes with state. Tooltips shortened in **all seven locales** — ru `Скрыть инструменты` / `Показать инструменты` (was `Свернуть/Развернуть инструменты рисования`, too long for a 26px chip's title). Reached in **9 Tab presses** from the page, collapses and reopens on Enter, and the focus ring is a real measured `2px solid rgb(240,201,100)` — read from the focused element's computed style after a Tab walk, because `getComputedStyle(el, ':focus-visible')` returns nothing (that argument is for pseudo-*elements*) and `focus({focusVisible:true})` does not make the pseudo-class match in Chromium.
- **MOBILE KEPT ITS OWN SHAPE.** Below 900px the rail is a horizontal strip with the toggle as a column at its end; a `@media (max-width:900px)` block restores exactly that geometry rather than forcing the desktop's vertical chip onto a phone. Measured at 390: toggle **24×41**, four cursor paths, zero page errors.
